The festival is cancelled. Mrs. Thompson finally takes down the ribbons she refused to strip before the first frost, and they go into a trunk no one opens again. The grief is quieter than it is in novels: plates carried away untouched, conversations Alexander rejoins halfway through with no memory of the first half. Niall stays through all of it, though a private secretary has no plain reason to stay once a family's business has narrowed to search parties. In February, Alexander asks him outright whether he believes Catherine is alive. Niall does not flinch from the question as the rest of the house has learned to, and Alexander decides it is the only honest conversation he has had in four months. Every chapter in the book is anchored to a place and date, and this one spans a year, so the chapter heading gives the full span and each scene opens with the month, much as Alexander's commonplace book entries do. The scene establishes the length of the grief and the depth of the bond, and it reserves Niall's own concealment as dramatic irony for the reader who will later look back on it.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to keep going and to find one honest person.

**Obstacle.** The searches fail, the household grieves, and everyone around him evades.

**Turning point.** In February, Niall answers "Do you believe she's alive?" honestly.

**Consequence.** Alexander is bound more tightly to Niall. The reader sees Niall's concealment from outside and doesn't yet know what it is.

**Location.** The coast, barns, and Sterling Castle across months. It ends in the library in February.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall, Mrs. Thompson, the household, and search parties.

**Weather and light.** The seasons turn: autumn gales on the coast, a hard winter, a thin February light.

**Mood.** Numb endurance.

**Date and time of day.** October 1851 through February 1852\. Each beat opens with its month.

**Time since previous scene.** This overlaps 3.3.

**Scene duration.** Five months, told as a montage.

**Carried forward.** The commonplace book entries.

**Setups and plants.** The cancelled festival of 1852 (mentioned). The ribbons packed in a trunk. Niall's stay beyond his duties. The reader's later reinterpretation of his silence.

**Rules to honor.** The next festival is cancelled.

**Target length.** About 900 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 3.4**

**Continuity in.** Overlaps 3.3. The commonplace book entries give the scene its month-by-month shape.

**Continuity out.** Alexander is bound more tightly to Niall. The reader has watched Niall's silence from outside and will reread it after 4.4 and 10.3.

**Alexander.** Grief as tasks: he doesn't eat, doesn't sleep, rejoins conversations halfway. He practices throwing alone at odd hours when he can't sleep.

**Niall.**

* What he knows and keeps: the Vanguard's search (Simmons and Carrow were only the first), the feather in his coat, and Calum, whom he learned in 1848 had been Turned. He does not yet connect Calum to Catherine.  
* A private secretary has no plain reason to stay. His orders are to guard the heir. His reason is Alexander.  
* His answer in February (decided): he believes she is alive, because whoever took her went to trouble no one spends on the dead. It is true, it is his own reasoning, and it hides everything behind it.  
* Care stays practical: a fire laid, a chair kept, a plate put in front of Alexander.

**Mrs. Thompson.** Takes down the ribbons after the first frost that came the night Catherine was taken. Hands folded hard in her apron when she has decided not to cry.

**Location.** The coast, barns within twenty miles, Sterling Castle across the months, ending in the library in February.

**Threads touched.** The 1852 festival is cancelled (mention it forward). The ribbons go into a trunk no one opens. Niall's staying beyond his duties.

#### **Scene 5\. London, November 1852**

**Scene summary.** The city is in black for the Duke of Wellington, whose funeral passes through streets that are mourning a national hero while the Sterlings mourn in private. Lady Aldermere and Mrs. Fenhallow call, and their condolences shade into curiosity. Lady Aldermere mentions the Ashcroft Institute's new lecture season, and "Lord Silas, as he insists," with the particular sneer old money keeps for new titles. Mrs. Fenhallow's brother-in-law has heard of a young woman answering Catherine's description seen on the northern circuits. Pike calls too, freshly back from the north, where he was told again and again that the family's private inquiries had already looked and found nothing. Alexander covers for his father without knowing what he is covering. That night a letter comes. Edward breaks the seal standing, which he never does, and Amelia is at the bureau opening a leather case Alexander has never seen before the letter is fully read. A young woman has been seen. By midnight his parents' carriage has gone north. Edward sets both hands on his son's shoulders, an old gesture that has always meant more than words from him, and says that Alexander is the only heir the family has left and must stay. Alexander watches the empty square and knows with a cold certainty that he is being protected from something, and that protection and exclusion now feel exactly the same. The scene establishes Institute texture, the sighting that sends Edward and Amelia north, and Alexander's flaw under pressure. Plant: the Ashcroft name.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to be let into whatever has seized his parents.

**Obstacle.** His father's protectiveness, with Alexander designated "the only heir."

**Turning point.** The letter comes, and his parents are gone by midnight.

**Consequence.** He stands at the window, sure that protection and exclusion are the same thing, and returns to Norfolk to wait. That sets up Chapter 4\.

**Location.** The Sterling townhouse in a London square: the drawing room, then the front hall and the steps.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall, Edward, Amelia, Lady Aldermere, Mrs. Fenhallow, Pike.

**Weather and light.** A London November, with coal fog, black crape in shop windows for Wellington, and early dark.

**Mood.** Public mourning outside, private dread inside.

**Date and time of day.** Visitors on Thursday 18 November 1852 (Wellington's funeral day). Pike on Friday 19 November. The letter on the night of Saturday 20 November, about nine, with the carriage gone by midnight.

**Time since previous scene.** About nine months since 3.4.

**Scene duration.** Three days, told in three beats.

**Carried forward.** Pike's letters twice a month have continued. Alexander is 23 now.

**Setups and plants.** The Ashcroft name and "Lord Silas." The sighting. The leather case. Hands on shoulders ("only heir"). The parents' departure.

**Rules to honor.** Wellington's funeral was on 18 November 1852 (historical). The sighting sends Edward and Amelia north. The bite happened about a month earlier (mid-October 1852), off the page.

**Target length.** About 1,100 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 3.5**

**Continuity in.** About nine months since 3.4. Alexander turned 23 in March. Pike's letters have kept coming. The family has come up to the townhouse. Catherine was bitten about a month ago, off the page; she is now a full vampire.

**Continuity out.** Edward and Amelia are gone north by midnight. Alexander returns to Norfolk about a week later to wait, with Niall.

**First appearance: Lady Aldermere and Mrs. Fenhallow.**

* Lady Aldermere: tall, jet-beaded, fond of lorgnettes, old money. The first on-page sneer at "Lord Silas, as he insists." (Silas bought a barony in the 1830s; properly "Lord Ashcroft.")  
* Mrs. Fenhallow: small, plump, never still. Her brother-in-law's rumor of a young woman on the northern circuits.  
* Both: condolence shading into curiosity.

**Pike.** Freshly back from the north on his own time, told everywhere that the family's private inquiries had already been and gone. Alexander covers for his father without knowing what he is covering.

**The sighting letter (decided).** Sent by Ewan Macrae, the family's Highland courier since 1845: a young woman answering Catherine's description glimpsed near Loch Maree. Ewan's later parcel (the brush and the swallow he found on a waystone) is what confirms it in Chapter 4\.

**The leather case (decided).** Amelia's field case: leathers, concealed daggers, gloves. Alexander has never seen it because it has never been used where he could see.

**Edward.** Breaks the seal standing, which he never does. Both hands on his son's shoulders. "Only heir" is love and exclusion at once. He leaves Niall behind with standing orders to guard Alexander.

**Niall.** Present, silent, and under those orders. He knows what the letter means.

**Location.** The Sterling townhouse in a London square: drawing room, front hall, steps. Coal fog, black crape in shop windows, early dark.

**Rules in play.** Wellington's funeral, Thursday 18 November 1852 (historical). The letter on Saturday 20 November, about nine; the carriage gone by midnight.

**Threads touched.** The Ashcroft name and the Institute's lecture season (Institute texture). Protection and exclusion made identical, Alexander's flaw under pressure.

---

### **Chapter 4: Letters Through the Storm**

*Sterling Castle, Norfolk, Winter 1852*

**CHAPTER OVERVIEW**

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants the truth, and he wants to be allowed into it.

**Obstacle.** The iron-strapped door, Niall's orders, and the secret seven years in the making.

**Turning point.** The two letters that contradict each other, and Niall's "Yes."

**Consequence.** The chapter ends as Alexander stands at the top of the stair down into the hidden world, betrayed, awake, and choosing to go on. Plot Turn One has landed.

**Span and length.** Tuesday 30 November to the small hours of Wednesday 1 December 1852\. Target about 4,400 words.

#### **Scene 1\. The iron-strapped door**

**Scene summary.** Alexander returns to Norfolk alone at the start of winter. Thomas Barrow meets him in the hall with a new directness, the manner of a man who has been carrying a household on his own. In the undercroft Alexander finds himself in front of an iron-strapped door he has been kept away from all his life, and asks Niall to open it. "I can't. Not without his order." Something in Niall's jaw tightens in a way Alexander cannot place. It is the last time Niall will refuse him on his father's authority. The scene puts the wall between them into a physical object and raises the pressure.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to see what lies past the door he has never been allowed through.

**Obstacle.** Niall refuses on Edward's authority.

**Turning point.** "I can't. Not without his order." Niall's jaw tightens, and it's the last refusal on Edward's authority.

**Consequence.** Alexander is frustrated, and the wall between them is now a physical door. That sets up its opening in 4.5.

**Location.** The Sterling Castle entrance hall, then the undercroft passage and the iron-strapped door.

**Characters.** Alexander, Thomas, Niall.

**Weather and light.** The afternoon light is going, and a storm is building off the North Sea. The wind rises in the chimneys.

**Mood.** Restless and taut.

**Date and time of day.** Tuesday 30 November 1852, about three to four in the afternoon (sunset 3:49).

**Time since previous scene.** About ten days. Alexander returned from London about a week ago.

**Scene duration.** About thirty minutes.

**Carried forward.** The parents have been gone for ten days. Thomas is noticeably more direct.

**Setups and plants.** The iron-strapped door (4.5). Thomas's steward arc.

**Rules to honor.** Thomas is under-steward, in his forties.

**Target length.** About 700 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 4.1**

**Continuity in.** About ten days since his parents left; Alexander has been back in Norfolk about a week. The house has been carried by Thomas.

**Continuity out.** The wall between Alexander and Niall is now a physical door. It opens in 4.5.

**Thomas Barrow.** New directness: responsibility has made his Norfolk voice plainer. He is growing toward steward of the whole community (formally asked in Chapter 25).

**Niall.** "I can't. Not without his order." His jaw tightens because he is already at the edge of disobeying. This is the last refusal he gives on Edward's authority.

**Alexander.** He knows this door exists and has been steered from it all his life. Asking outright is new behavior; the waiting-to-be-allowed flaw is beginning to strain.

**Location.** The entrance hall, then the undercroft passage and the iron-strapped door. Beyond it (unseen yet): a stair through centuries of masonry to the council chamber, the sanctuary, and the infirmary beneath the keep.

**Rules in play.** Sunset 3:49; the storm building off the North Sea. The west wing moans in a wind like this.

#### **Scene 2\. The gatehouse bell**

**Scene summary.** Near midnight, in the storm, the gatehouse bell rings in a pattern that is not the one for fire or for thieves. The rider has come past the point where riding makes any sense, his greatcoat frozen stiff at the shoulders. "Ewan," Niall says, and for a moment his face is entirely unguarded. Ewan Macrae, twenty-four now and weathered, has two letters and a small oilcloth parcel pressed flat inside his coat. His glance goes to Niall to ask permission, and Alexander watches a whole wordless exchange pass between the two men that he is shut out of. Mrs. Thompson arrives in her dressing gown and bundles Ewan off to the kitchen. The scene shows Alexander the language of the hidden world before he knows it exists, and it plants the parcel.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to know who is at the gate and why.

**Obstacle.** The storm, and the private language between Ewan and Niall that shuts him out.

**Turning point.** Ewan asks Niall's permission with a look.

**Consequence.** Alexander realizes there's a whole world between those two men. Ewan is fed, and the letters are in hand.

**Location.** The gatehouse, then the courtyard and the kitchen passage.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall, Ewan, Mrs. Thompson, and the gatekeeper.

**Weather and light.** A full storm: sleet driven sideways and a black sky. The moon is nearly full and hidden. The gate lanterns gutter.

**Mood.** Urgency out of the dark.

**Date and time of day.** Tuesday 30 November 1852, about quarter to midnight.

**Time since previous scene.** About eight hours.

**Scene duration.** About twenty minutes.

**Carried forward.** The door from 4.1.

**Setups and plants.** Ewan's oilcloth parcel with the brush (Chapter 6). The bell pattern. Ewan is 24\.

**Rules to honor.** Ewan is Highland, "sir" to Alexander, and turns his cap once when he's nervous. He rode from the nearest railhead through the storm.

**Target length.** About 800 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 4.2**

**Continuity in.** About eight hours after the door. The storm has fully arrived.

**Continuity out.** Ewan is fed; the two letters and the parcel are in hand.

**First appearance: Ewan Macrae.**

24. Weather-hardened, lean from long riding, sandy-brown hair, cheeks reddened by wind, a rider's bowed ease, cap pulled low. Tonight his greatcoat is frozen stiff at the shoulders.  
* Soft Highland burr, plain and practical, respectful of rank but no longer frozen by it. "Sir" to Alexander. Turns his cap once in his hands when nervous. Uses Rhoswen's name without noticing he has done it (Alexander won't know the name until 4.4 gives it meaning).  
* He met Niall at Loch Maree in 1845, when he was seventeen and rode through a storm to Sterling Castle the first time. Tonight repeats that ride. It is why Niall's face goes unguarded.  
* Carries, always, Edward's black-cord packet from 1845 (not to be mentioned; opened in Book Four).  
* He sent the sighting letter (decided in 3.5) and rode with the confirming parcel.

**Mrs. Thompson.** Dressing gown, "love," and a kitchen fire. She bundles Ewan off before anyone can question him.

**Gatekeeper.** Unnamed. The bell pattern is Vanguard: neither fire nor thieves.

**Location.** The gatehouse, the courtyard, the kitchen passage. Sleet sideways, gate lanterns guttering, a nearly full moon hidden.

**Threads touched.** The oilcloth parcel (brush and pencil copy of the swallow, Chapter 6 with Harcourt). The bell pattern.

#### **Scene 3\. Two letters**

**Scene summary.** The study still smells of cold pipe ash, and Niall lights the fire because he needs something ordinary to do with his hands. The letter addressed to Alexander is in his father's hand. *You will want the whole of it all at once. You are my son, and I raised you to want exactly that.* Edward tells him, in outline, that the family serves the Crown in a hidden work, that Catherine was taken by a creature the work exists to stand against, and that Niall will tell him the rest. Then he forbids Alexander to follow. The second letter is addressed to Niall, and it is in Amelia's hand. Niall reads it twice and his jaw sets. When Alexander demands to see it, Niall hands it over, the first time he has ever chosen Alexander's right to know over an order. *Bring him. He has a right to choose, and we have kept him from choosing long enough.* Alexander's parents disagree about him, in writing, from four hundred miles away. The scene reveals the truth in its broad shape, gives Alexander a real choice, and makes the book's theme of protection against agency concrete in two sheets of paper.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to read what his father sent.

**Obstacle.** The letter reveals the truth and forbids him in the same breath, and Niall's letter is kept from him until Niall chooses otherwise.

**Turning point.** Niall hands over Amelia's letter: "Bring him."

**Consequence.** Alexander holds two contradictory orders from his parents, and the choice is suddenly his. That sets up 4.4.

**Location.** Edward's study, with cold pipe ash and a fire Niall lights.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall.

**Weather and light.** The storm batters the windows.

**Mood.** Stunned and electric.

**Date and time of day.** Wednesday 1 December 1852, about quarter past midnight.

**Time since previous scene.** About thirty minutes.

**Scene duration.** About twenty minutes.

**Carried forward.** The two letters from 4.2.

**Setups and plants.** The divergent letters (Chapter 11's quarrel). Niall's first choice of Alexander's right over an order.

**Rules to honor.** Edward's letter outlines the Vanguard and names Niall as the one to tell the rest.

**Target length.** About 900 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 4.3**

**Continuity in.** About thirty minutes later. Ewan is in the kitchen. The commonplace book lies open on the study desk where Alexander left it when the bell rang.

**Continuity out.** Two contradictory orders, and the choice is suddenly Alexander's. The disagreement between his parents resurfaces in Chapter 11's quarrel.

**Edward's letter.** In his voice: grave, economical, loving. Line, exact: "You will want the whole of it all at once. You are my son, and I raised you to want exactly that." Outlines the hidden work, names a creature as Catherine's taker, names Niall to tell the rest, forbids Alexander to follow.

**Amelia's letter (to Niall).** Line, exact: "Bring him. He has a right to choose, and we have kept him from choosing long enough." She has already decided there will be no more rooms her son is kept out of.

**Niall.** Lights the fire to have something ordinary to do with his hands. Reads twice. Handing over the letter is the first time he chooses Alexander's right over an order: the start of his arc.

**Alexander.** Surprise: stillness, the room catalogued, "I see" when he doesn't. He reads each letter twice before trusting his own voice.

**Location.** Edward's study: cold pipe ash, the new fire, "For Crown and Kin" above the hearth, storm on the windows.

#### **Scene 4\. "Yes."**

**Scene summary.** Alexander asks the question he already knows the answer to: *You knew. All this time.* "Yes." The single word rearranges seven years of memory: the bandaged hand after Loch Maree, the antiquarian correspondence that never seemed to produce any antiquities, the pause before every throw. Niall tells him about the kelpie, gauging each word like ice he is not sure will hold, and then about Loch Maree, and then about what he is. He is not a secretary. He is an operative of the Shadow Vanguard, made so in a flooded cave when Alexander was sixteen. Alexander goes very still, as he always does when he is angry, and grows quieter rather than louder. He does not forgive Niall and does not say whether he will. On the desk his commonplace book lies open where he left it when the bell rang, and its last Grey Year entry stops mid-sentence. This is Plot Turn One. There is no road back to the life he had. It plants the commonplace book.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to know how long Niall has known.

**Obstacle.** Internal. Anger and love at once, and a friendship remade in a single word.

**Turning point.** "Yes." Then Loch Maree, and what Niall is.

**Consequence.** Plot Turn One. Alexander is quiet with anger and awake to the hidden world. The commonplace book stops mid-sentence.

**Location.** Edward's study.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall.

**Weather and light.** The storm is easing. There's rain now instead of sleet.

**Mood.** A reckoning.

**Date and time of day.** Wednesday 1 December 1852, about quarter to one to half past one in the morning.

**Time since previous scene.** Continuous with 4.3.

**Scene duration.** About forty minutes.

**Carried forward.** The bandaged hand after Loch Maree, the antiquarian correspondence, the pause before a throw.

**Setups and plants.** Alexander's anger that doesn't forgive (Chapter 5). The kelpie (Chapter 11). Niall's status as a Vanguard operative.

**Rules to honor.** Niall was made an operative at Loch Maree in 1845, when Alexander was 16\. Alexander grows still and quiet when angry.

**Target length.** About 1,100 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 4.4**

**Continuity in.** Continuous with 4.3. Both letters are on the desk.

**Continuity out.** Plot Turn One. Alexander is awake to the hidden world, quiet with anger, and unforgiving for now (Chapter 5). The commonplace book stops mid-sentence.

**Alexander.**

* Anger: stillness and a lowered voice, every word placed. Never shouting. Available lines: "Say that again, if you would, and more slowly." / "Indeed. And when, precisely, were you planning to tell me?"  
* The deepest fear touched: that seven years of friendship were an assignment.  
* He does not forgive and does not say whether he will.

**Niall.**

* "Yes." Then the kelpie, Loch Maree, and what he is, each word tested like ice.  
* Burr thickens under strain; "dinnae" only if it breaks. Thumb on the palm scar.  
* What he still withholds: Calum and 1848, the feather, and what he feels. Calum comes out only at the birch wood (Chapter 10), where "Nothing I wasn't fit to carry" breaks.

**What the "Yes" rearranges.** The bandaged hand after Loch Maree (1845, Alexander 16); the antiquarian correspondence that never produced antiquities (Niall's cover); the pause before every throw. Optionally, the ledger that fell in his father's study the night of the sale (decided in 2.1 as deliberate); Alexander may realize it now or Niall may admit it.

**Loch Maree facts Niall can give.** Two deaths reported by Ewan; an Ashcroft capture operation; Rhoswen and her foals freed; Niall froze, then threw across thirty-one yards and saved Amelia; he was made a junior operative in the cave that night. He was nineteen and three weeks into service.

**Location.** Edward's study. The storm easing to rain.

**Threads touched.** The kelpie (Chapter 11). The Ashcroft name gains weight. The commonplace book's last Grey Year entry, stopped mid-sentence.

#### **Scene 5\. Down**

**Scene summary.** Niall says the council has to be woken now that the sighting is confirmed, and that Alexander ought to be in the room when it happens, whatever else the night has cost him. They go to the iron-strapped door together, and this time Niall opens it without asking anyone's leave. The stairs go down much further than Alexander would have thought the ground beneath the castle allowed. The Sterling stone gives way, step by step, to something older and plainer. The chapter ends on the threshold of the hidden world, and Chapter 5 opens below it.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to go down now, whatever the night has cost.

**Obstacle.** The weight of seven years, and a stair that goes deeper than the ground should allow.

**Turning point.** Niall opens the door without anyone's leave.

**Consequence.** Alexander stands at the threshold of the hidden world, and Chapter 5 opens below it.

**Location.** The undercroft and the iron-strapped door, then the first steps down.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall.

**Weather and light.** None down here. The cold rises from below and the lantern light shakes.

**Mood.** Threshold awe.

**Date and time of day.** Wednesday 1 December 1852, about quarter to two in the morning.

**Time since previous scene.** About fifteen minutes.

**Scene duration.** About five minutes.

**Carried forward.** The sighting has been confirmed, so the council must be woken.

**Setups and plants.** The masonry changes from Sterling stone to something older (Chapter 5, scene 1).

**Target length.** About 400 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 4.5**

**Continuity in.** About fifteen minutes later. Edward's letter confirms the sighting, so the council must be woken.

**Continuity out.** Alexander on the stair into the hidden world. Chapter 5 opens below.

**Niall.** Opens the door without anyone's leave. His second choice of Alexander's right over an order in one night.

**Alexander.** Awe under anger. He goes down with the man he has not forgiven.

**Location.** The undercroft and the iron-strapped door, then the first steps. The stair descends through centuries of masonry, each layer plainer and older than the last, and deeper than the ground should allow. Below lie the council chamber (Beatrice presiding, Sebastian by the door), the sanctuary, the infirmary, and the records room.

**Rules in play.** Lantern light only; cold rising from below.

---

## **PART TWO: THE ROAD NORTH**

---

### **Chapter 5: The Castle Beneath the Castle**

*Sterling Castle, Norfolk, Winter 1852*

**CHAPTER OVERVIEW**

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to understand what his family has been, and to know whether he can still trust Niall.

**Obstacle.** The size of what was hidden, an institution in which he holds no rank, and Sebastian's opposition.

**Turning point.** The boggart on the stair, where he acts by instinct, and the Pryce file, where he learns the reason for his loneliness.

**Consequence.** By dawn he has accepted Niall's promise without quite believing it. He's calmer and more dangerous, and ready to set terms. That sets up Chapter 6, where the departure is decided.

**Span and length.** Wednesday 1 December 1852, about two to half past seven in the morning. Target about 4,700 words.

#### **Scene 1\. What the undercroft kept**

**Scene summary.** Past the iron-strapped door the stair goes down through centuries of masonry, each layer plainer and older than the last. The first room Niall shows him is a storeroom of the prequel's costs. Silver-threaded mesh hangs tarnished along one wall, folded in haste rather than care. A winch drum with a cracked casing stands beside a waxed-canvas battery housing that no longer holds a charge. Niall names the price of that night without drama: a dislocated jaw, three ribs he felt every morning for a year, and Amelia's hands burned so badly she wore gloves the whole winter and told everyone it was the cold. Alexander remembers those gloves. He had teased her about them. For the first time he sees the whole of his boyhood laid over this room like tracing paper, and the scene makes the concealment physical and the Institute's machinery concrete. Plant: the prequel machinery that every later Institute device grows from.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to see the hidden world with his own eyes.

**Obstacle.** Emotional. Every object reframes his boyhood.

**Turning point.** Niall names Amelia's gloves, and Alexander remembers teasing her about them.

**Consequence.** His boyhood is overwritten, and he walks on toward the council chamber carrying the cost of Loch Maree.

**Location.** The first storeroom below the iron-strapped door, with the prequel's machinery.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall.

**Weather and light.** Underground. Dry cold, old stone, the smell of tarnish and lamp oil.

**Mood.** Revelatory and uneasy.

**Date and time of day.** Wednesday 1 December 1852, about two in the morning.

**Time since previous scene.** About fifteen minutes.

**Scene duration.** About twenty minutes.

**Carried forward.** The stair from 4.5.

**Setups and plants.** The silver-threaded mesh, the winch drum, and the battery housing, which the convoy's improved versions in Chapter 9 grow from.

**Rules to honor.** Niall's injuries at Loch Maree were a dislocated jaw and three ribs. Amelia's hands were burned and she wore gloves all winter.

**Target length.** About 700 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 5.1**

**Continuity in.** Fifteen minutes after 4.5. Alexander has just gone down the stair behind a man he has not forgiven. The cold that rose from below is now dry cold and lamp oil.

**Continuity out.** Alexander walks on toward the council chamber carrying the cost of Loch Maree. His boyhood has been overwritten.

**Niall.** Names the price of 1845 flatly, like an inventory, with no bid for sympathy. He is showing rather than confessing, which is his kind of honesty. He rubs his palm scar with his thumb when he reaches the ribs. He is watching Alexander's face and pretending not to.

**Alexander.** Notices first, feels second. He reads the room object by object, then the gloves land. Fingertips tap thumb to little finger. He is still angry at Niall, and the anger sits under the awe without going away. Formal register ("I see," "indeed") covers what he cannot yet say.

**The objects.** Silver-threaded mesh folded in haste along one wall, tarnished. A winch drum with cracked casing (copper-lined). A waxed-canvas battery housing, dead. The Institute's open eye above a cage may be stamped on the fittings. Optional detail from the prequel: Percival's abandoned walking stick was among the recovered equipment.

**Location.** First storeroom below the iron-strapped door. The stair walls have changed from Sterling stone to something older. Dry cold, old stone, tarnish, lamp oil.

**Rules in play.**

* Amelia's palms: blistered at Loch Maree, and thin silver-grey lines remain beneath the skin. The gloves hid them all winter 1845, and she blamed the cold.  
* Dr. Hale saw the hands and kept the dressings (Book Four evidence). He does not need to appear.  
* Alexander was sixteen and teased her about the gloves.

**Threads touched.** The mesh, winch and battery are the seed of every improved Institute device (convoy, 9.2). Amelia's silver lines (Book Four). Seven years of concealment.

**Flags.** The outline gives Niall a "dislocated jaw, three ribs." The prequel (which cannot be contradicted) has Ewan see Niall's "bruised jaw and bound ribs," and Niall takes a pike haft across the forearm. **DECIDED:** Niall says a jaw that would not close properly for a week and ribs he felt every morning for a year. This fits the prequel and keeps the outline's cost. Logged for the bible.

#### **Scene 2\. The council chamber**

**Scene summary.** The long table is scarred by a century of tea rings and ink. Lady Beatrice Vale stands at its head. She is tall and quicker than her early sixties, and she touches a ward-worked brooch once, the way someone checks a locked door. Lord Sebastian Thorne is fair, groomed, and watchful by the door, and he looks at Alexander with a courtesy that does not reach his eyes. He argues that the heir belongs in Norfolk, safe and ignorant as his father intended, and he frames ambition as concern. "He has proposed nothing at all yet, Sebastian," Beatrice says, in a voice that fills the room without rising. She gives Alexander the institution in its bones. It is the Office of Unusual Affairs to the world and the Shadow Vanguard to its initiates. The Crown is its head and the Sterling duke its second. Admission is earned by field record and never by ceremony. And Alexander holds no rank in it at all. The scene establishes the political thread, Sebastian's grievance, and the gap between Alexander's birth and his standing. Plant: Beatrice's brooch.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to be taken seriously by the council.

**Obstacle.** Institutional and political. He has no rank, and Sebastian argues he should be kept ignorant.

**Turning point.** Beatrice says, "He has proposed nothing at all yet, Sebastian."

**Consequence.** Alexander understands the institution and his own lack of standing in it. The brooch is planted.

**Location.** The council chamber, with its long scarred table.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall, Beatrice, Sebastian.

**Weather and light.** Underground and lamplit. The fire is newly built.

**Mood.** Formal and adversarial.

**Date and time of day.** Wednesday 1 December 1852, about half past two in the morning.

**Time since previous scene.** About fifteen minutes. The council was woken by a servant.

**Scene duration.** About forty minutes.

**Carried forward.** The sighting is confirmed by Edward's letter.

**Setups and plants.** Beatrice's brooch (Chapter 13 and later). Sebastian's grievance. "Admission is earned by field record."

**Rules to honor.** The Office of Unusual Affairs, with the Crown at its head and the Sterling duke second. Beatrice is in her early sixties.

**Target length.** About 900 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 5.2**

**Continuity in.** Fifteen minutes after 5.1. A servant has woken the council. Edward's letter has confirmed the sighting.

**Continuity out.** Alexander grasps the institution and his lack of standing in it. The brooch is planted. He is now a man in the room who holds no rank.

**First appearance: Lady Beatrice Vale.**

* 62, tall, and quicker than her years. Iron-grey hair in a severe coil, grey eyes. A widow's plain dark dress. A ward-worked brooch, her husband's last gift.  
* Voice never rises and still fills the room. Precise, procedural, plain even when severe. Respectful without deference. Her fingers find seams in the panelling others cannot.  
* Tic: touches the brooch "the way someone checks a locked door." Closes ledgers with a small decisive sound.  
* She is a Warden by philosophy and a Crown loyalist by duty. Do not explain Stronsay or Laurence yet. The brooch is planted only.  
* Her title tonight is not "Acting Commander." Edward is alive and commands. She is the senior officer with standing emergency authority over the Sterling household and estate while the Duke is away.

**First appearance: Lord Sebastian Thorne.**

* 50, lean, the look of a good field operative who stopped. Meticulously barbered and tailored. Stands near the door in watchful stillness.  
* Clipped voice, patronizing, oily when speaking upward. Presents self-interest as concern. Quirk: repeats a phrase for emphasis ("Until it doesn't.").  
* Grievances (do not state them all): passed over for a field command that Edward gave to Alasdair Fraser (so he is cold to Niall), and lost a council seat to Amelia.  
* His Institute ties stay invisible. Nothing here may hint at them.

**Niall.** Stands close behind Alexander. He goes still around Sebastian. His father's name is in this room's history.

**Alexander.** Wants to be taken seriously and is not yet a person the room has to take seriously. Courteous, controlled, quieter as the argument tightens. Do not let him shout.

**Location.** The council chamber. Long table scarred by a century of tea rings and ink. Fire newly built. Optional echo of 26.x: Edward's and Amelia's chairs stand empty because they are north.

**Rules in play.**

* Office of Unusual Affairs to the world, Shadow Vanguard to initiates. The Crown is head, the Sterling Duke the operational second. Admission is by field record, never ceremony.  
* Edward meant to initiate Alexander at twenty-five. Alexander is twenty-three. This is why Sebastian can say "as his father intended."  
* Factions exist (Wardens, Exterminators, Collectors, Crown loyalists, Reformers). Sebastian is a Collector.

**Threads touched.** Council fracture. Alexander's gap between title and standing (runs to Book Four). Brooch (13.x).

**Flags.** The outline calls Sebastian "fair." The Main Character Bible says "prematurely grey." **DECIDED:** fair-complexioned, hair prematurely grey. Both hold. Logged for the bible.

#### **Scene 3\. The sanctuary**

**Scene summary.** Beyond the council chamber the vault opens into a hall lit gold by hanging lamps rather than the cold blue of the corridors. Pallets line one wall. Two families have come in from the fen country, driven out when a hunting party burned a warren it had no business burning. Mrs. Ashworth keeps their accounts in the same ledger style as the linen counts. Beatrice sets out the Vanguard's ethic as the question it always asks: not whether a being is human, but whether it can reason, whether it acts from hunger, fear, coercion, or deliberate cruelty, and whether it consents. Alexander does not pretend to understand what he is seeing. He inclines his head to the families the way he has watched his father greet tenants at the estate gate, and moves on when Beatrice does. The scene widens the hidden world and plants the ethic the whole series is argued through.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to understand what the Vanguard actually does.

**Obstacle.** Nothing blocks him. The challenge is taking it in.

**Turning point.** Beatrice states the ethic as the question it asks.

**Consequence.** Alexander has a moral map. He greets the families the way his father greets tenants.

**Location.** The sanctuary hall, lit gold by hanging lamps.

**Characters.** Alexander, Beatrice, Niall, Mrs. Ashworth, and two fen boggart families.

**Weather and light.** Underground. Warm, and damp around the pallets.

**Mood.** Wonder and responsibility.

**Date and time of day.** Wednesday 1 December 1852, about half past three in the morning.

**Time since previous scene.** About fifteen minutes.

**Scene duration.** About twenty minutes.

**Carried forward.** The council's framing of the ethic.

**Setups and plants.** The boggart families (the profile). Mrs. Ashworth's ledger (Chapter 12).

**Rules to honor.** The ethic questions: can it reason, why does it act, can it consent.

**Target length.** About 600 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 5.3**

**Continuity in.** Fifteen minutes after 5.2. Beatrice has told him he holds no rank. She now walks him on, not because he asked.

**Continuity out.** Alexander has a moral map and a first sight of what the Vanguard protects. He greets the families as his father greets tenants.

**First appearance: Mrs. Ashworth.**

* Neat, fortyish, keys at her waist, housekeeper's black, a pencil pinned in her hair. Her face goes the grey of a woman doing rapid, unhappy arithmetic.  
* Brisk. The same voice for linen counts and creature feeding. She keeps the supernatural accounts in the linen-ledger style.  
* She reads the boggarts' clicks and translates them.

**First appearance: the fen boggarts.**

* Child-sized, mud-brown, amber eyes, narrow hunched shoulders, long-fingered hands, a pelt like wet reeds.  
* No human words. They click like snipe, and Mrs. Ashworth can read the clicks. Gesture matters.  
* What humans believe: mischief spirits that sour milk and lead travellers into bogs. What is true: small, shy, reasoning family creatures of undrained fen, who lead people astray only to keep them from their young. They want wet ground left wet. Drainage, dogs and iron traps injure them. They can consent to sanctuary.

**Beatrice.** Now teaching, not testing. States the ethic in her plain voice. Approval is earned, so none is given here.

**Alexander.** Out of his depth, and he does not pretend otherwise. He gets by on manners: a slight inclination of the head, the tenants' gate greeting.

**Niall.** Knows this hall and keeps quiet.

**Location.** The sanctuary hall past the council chamber. A vault lit gold by hanging lamps, unlike the cold blue corridors. Pallets along one wall. Warm, damp around the pallets.

**Rules in play.** The ethic: can the being reason; does it act from hunger, fear, coercion or deliberate cruelty; can it be contained without destruction; does it consent. It is never simply "is it human."

**Threads touched.** Mrs. Ashworth's ledger (Ch. 12). Boggart child (5.4, Ch. 12). Harrow's Fen (Edward would not sell; 1.1, 2.x).

**Flags.** The outline says the families were "driven out when a hunting party burned a warren it had no business burning." It does not say whose party or which fen. The Supporting Bible says the Harrow's Fen family consented to Sterling protection, so these families may or may not be that family. **Keep it unattributed on the page** and do not name the hunters. Ask Fox if a later book needs to say. Not resolved here.

#### **Scene 4\. The boggart on the stair**

**Scene summary.** Noise on the upper stair. A creature no bigger than a child, pelted like wet reeds and soot, has bolted from the sanctuary and is cornered against the gallery wall, clicking like a frightened snipe. A household guard, roused and out of his depth, reaches for his belt on the reasoning that anything he cannot name wants killing. Beatrice is three strides ahead of everyone, but Alexander is closer. He goes down on one knee with his hands open, empty, turned palm up, and talks to it in the voice he uses on frightened horses, knowing it cannot understand a word. The creature weighs him the way Beatrice weighed him an hour before, and its shoulders drop. Niall's low murmur coaxes it into a length of old curtain from the linen room. Beatrice says nothing to Alexander about it, and he sees her mark it anyway. The scene reveals his instinct for protection before he has any training, and it plants the boggart child, which will need a species profile before it recurs.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to keep a frightened creature from being killed.

**Obstacle.** Physical. A guard with a weapon, a panicked creature, and his own lack of training.

**Turning point.** He kneels with open hands, and the creature's shoulders drop.

**Consequence.** Beatrice marks him without saying a word. Alexander is shaken and quietly proud.

**Location.** The upper stair and gallery of the sanctuary.

**Characters.** Alexander, the boggart child, a household guard, Beatrice, Niall.

**Weather and light.** Underground. The stair is lamplit and draughty.

**Mood.** Tense, then released.

**Date and time of day.** Wednesday 1 December 1852, about four in the morning.

**Time since previous scene.** About ten minutes.

**Scene duration.** About ten minutes.

**Carried forward.** The boggart families from 5.3.

**Setups and plants.** The open-hands gesture (Rhoswen in Chapter 11 and Chapter 21). The boggart child in Chapter 12\.

**Rules to honor.** The boggart child clicks like a snipe and has no human words.

**Target length.** About 700 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 5.4**

**Continuity in.** Ten minutes after 5.3. Beatrice and Alexander are still in the sanctuary area. The boggart families are inside the hall.

**Continuity out.** Beatrice marks him without a word. The child is wrapped in old curtain and returned. Alexander is shaken and privately proud.

**The boggart child.** Child-sized, pelted like wet reeds and soot, amber eyes, clicks like a frightened snipe. No human words. It weighs Alexander as Beatrice did an hour ago. Shoulders drop when his hands open.

**The household guard.** Unnamed, roused, out of his depth. Reaches for his belt on the logic that anything he cannot name wants killing. Not a villain.

**Alexander.** Acts before training or thought. Drops to one knee, hands open, empty, palm up. Uses the voice he uses on frightened horses. Speaks knowing the creature can't follow the words. This is protection without possession, the series' central contrast, shown before he knows the name for it. He does not know Edward once knelt unarmed before Rhoswen. The reader of the prequel does.

**Niall.** His low murmur, "Easy now," coaxes the creature into the old curtain from the linen room. The phrase is his for the frightened, the injured and Alexander.

**Beatrice.** Three strides ahead and stops. She says nothing. Alexander sees her mark it.

**Location.** The upper stair and gallery above the sanctuary. Lamplit, draughty, cornered against the gallery wall.

**Threads touched.** Open hands is the gesture that reaches Rhoswen (11.x, 21.x). The child recurs in Ch. 12 (bolts past Mrs. Ashworth).

#### **Scene 5\. Samuel Pryce**

**Scene summary.** In Beatrice's records room, among personnel files going back a century, Alexander finds a name he has not heard spoken in eleven years. Beatrice tells him plainly. Samuel Pryce, the Sterling butler, was an Ashcroft spy placed in the house around 1837 and exposed and dismissed in 1841\. His son went with him. No one knows where. Alexander understands, standing there, that his one boyhood friendship ended because of a war he was never told he was living inside, and that the tightening of his father's silence after 1841 was not coldness but fear. He learns the name Ashcroft properly now: Silas, the new baron who built a fortune on chemistry and railway shares, and Percival, who led the Loch Maree operation and carries Edward's blade under his jaw. The scene gives the Institute a personal face early and names the root of Alexander's loneliness. Will himself stays unseen.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to know the enemy's name.

**Obstacle.** Emotional. The name he finds is a friend's.

**Turning point.** He finds Samuel Pryce's file.

**Consequence.** His loneliness now has a cause. The Institute has a face, and he learns of Silas and Percival.

**Location.** Beatrice's records room, with shelves of personnel files.

**Characters.** Alexander, Beatrice, Niall.

**Weather and light.** Underground. Dry, with dust and paper.

**Mood.** A quiet grief.

**Date and time of day.** Wednesday 1 December 1852, about half past four in the morning.

**Time since previous scene.** About twenty minutes.

**Scene duration.** About thirty minutes.

**Carried forward.** His father's silence after 1841\.

**Setups and plants.** Will Pryce, unseen (a later book). Percival's scar from Edward's blade (Chapter 9).

**Rules to honor.** Pryce spied from about 1837 to 1841\. Silas is the new baron, with a fortune in chemistry and railway shares.

**Target length.** About 700 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 5.5**

**Continuity in.** Twenty minutes after 5.4. The night has been heavy, and Beatrice now takes him where the answers are kept.

**Continuity out.** Alexander's loneliness has a cause. The Institute has a face. He carries this to the window.

**Beatrice.** Tells it plainly: Samuel Pryce was the Sterling butler c. 1837 to 1841, an Ashcroft spy, exposed and dismissed. His son went with him. No one knows where. She does not soften it, and she does not linger.

**Alexander.** Has not heard the name in eleven years. Reads the file twice before he trusts his voice. Fingertips tap. He passes the butler's pantry door without looking at it, a habit planted in 1.1, and this is where it gets a reason. He was eight to twelve when Will Pryce was his friend. His childhood fear is that people he loves can be removed by decisions made over his head. He reads Edward's tightened silence after 1841 as fear, not coldness. This is the same wound Niall's secrecy touches.

**Will Pryce.** Never on the page here. Do not give his age, looks or fate. Alexander does not learn he was raised inside the Institute (Book Three).

**The Ashcroft names he learns.**

* Silas: 62, new baron (bought in the 1830s), fortune from industrial chemistry and railway shares. "Lord Silas" is the household's affectation; Beatrice would say "Ashcroft" or "the baron."  
* Percival: 32, led the Loch Maree operation, wears Edward's blade scar beneath his jaw.  
* Alexander may recall the Institute's lecture season (Lady Aldermere in 3.x).

**Niall.** Present, silent. He watches Alexander find Will's name and is already thinking what it means for the promise he is about to make.

**Location.** Beatrice's records room. Shelves of personnel files going back a century. Dry, dust and paper.

**Threads touched.** Will Pryce returns in Book Three. Percival's scar (9.x). Samuel Pryce's exposure (1841, series timeline).

#### **Scene 6\. The cold window**

**Scene summary.** Before dawn Alexander and Niall stand at a gallery window while the last of the storm rolls out to sea, close enough that their shoulders nearly touch, as they have stood at a hundred windows without either remarking on it. "I don't know yet whether I trust you," Alexander says. "Not the way I did yesterday. I want to." Niall does not defend himself. "Then ask me. Whatever you need to know, from now on. I'll not decide for you again what you're fit to carry." Alexander takes the promise and does not quite believe it, and the reader, who met Calum in Chapter 3, knows it is already broken. The scene moves trust by a visible degree and sets the dramatic irony that pays off at the frozen crossing.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to be honest about where he stands.

**Obstacle.** A trust that's damaged but not broken.

**Turning point.** Niall's promise: "I'll not decide for you again what you're fit to carry."

**Consequence.** Alexander accepts provisionally, while the reader knows the promise is already broken. That sets up Chapter 10\.

**Location.** An upper gallery window of the castle, looking east to the sea.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall.

**Weather and light.** The storm is rolling out to sea, with a clearing sky and a grey dawn before sunrise. The last of the moon is in the west.

**Mood.** Raw and tender.

**Date and time of day.** Wednesday 1 December 1852, about quarter past seven in the morning (sunrise 7:49).

**Time since previous scene.** About two hours. They have been up all night.

**Scene duration.** About fifteen minutes.

**Carried forward.** Everything from the night.

**Setups and plants.** The promise (Chapter 10, scene 4, and Chapter 13, scene 2).

**Target length.** About 600 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 5.6**

**Continuity in.** About two hours after 5.5. They have been up all night. The storm is leaving.

**Continuity out.** A provisional trust. The reader knows the promise is already compromised (Calum, 3.3). Sets up Chapter 6 (terms) and Chapter 10 (the broken promise).

**Alexander.** Honest, raw, dry. Asks rather than declares. "I want to" is as close to reaching for Niall as he comes. He takes the promise and does not quite believe it. He does not shout.

**Niall.** Does not defend himself. His pause before answering, then the plain line. His promise is sincere and already compromised: he still holds Calum (the 1848 meeting), the money to the MacDuffs, his feelings, the rib scar, the coded notebook. Write him omitting, never lying.

**Address.** "Alex" is available to Niall only in private, danger or honesty. This scene qualifies, but the outline gives "I don't know yet whether I trust you" and "Then ask me" without it. Use it only if it lands as honesty.

**Location.** An upper gallery window looking east to the sea. Grey dawn before sunrise (7:49), last of the storm rolling out, last of the moon in the west. Their shoulders nearly touch, as at a hundred windows.

**Threads touched.** The promise (10.4, 13.2). Alexander's "waiting to be allowed" flaw. Trust moves one visible degree.

**Flags.** None. The dramatic irony depends on nobody mentioning Calum in this scene.

### **Chapter 6: Terms of Departure**

*Sterling Castle, Norfolk, Winter 1852*

**CHAPTER OVERVIEW**

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to go north and find his sister, on terms he can accept.

**Obstacle.** Beatrice's authority, Sebastian's objections, and his own inexperience.

**Turning point.** Seraphina's point about the seal decides it: he goes with Beatrice's knowledge, under Niall's command.

**Consequence.** He leaves Sterling land before dawn, armed, afraid, and resolved. That sets up the road north in Chapter 7\.

**Span and length.** Wednesday 1 December to about five in the morning on Friday 3 December 1852\. Target about 4,800 words.

#### **Scene 1\. The physician's table**

**Scene summary.** The hidden infirmary is a room of steel tables and gas lamps turned up too bright for comfort. Dr. Miriam Harcourt does not look up when they come in. She is compact, her sleeves pushed to the elbow and her hair scraped back with more efficiency than vanity. On her table lie the contents of Ewan's oilcloth parcel: a small painter's brush, its bristles stiff with dried blood, and Ewan's careful pencil copy of the swallow he found painted in that blood on the waystone. She speaks of "the subject." Alexander tells her the subject has a name. Harcourt answers that precision serves the girl better than sentiment, and that she has treated a great many subjects who were also somebody's sister. Then, after a pause, she says, "Catherine," as though testing its weight, and notes it. She will stay in Norfolk with the brush while everyone else goes north. The scene introduces Harcourt, plants the scientific heart of the cure search, and gives her arc its first crack.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants Catherine treated as a person.

**Obstacle.** Social. Harcourt's clinical detachment.

**Turning point.** Harcourt says "Catherine" and makes a note.

**Consequence.** Alexander has a grudging respect for her. Harcourt is established, and she stays.

**Location.** The hidden infirmary, with steel tables and gas lamps.

**Characters.** Alexander, Harcourt, Niall.

**Weather and light.** Underground, with bright gas light.

**Mood.** Clinical and prickly.

**Date and time of day.** Wednesday 1 December 1852, about eleven in the morning.

**Time since previous scene.** About four hours. They slept briefly.

**Scene duration.** About twenty minutes.

**Carried forward.** Ewan's parcel from 4.2.

**Setups and plants.** The brush and the pre-bite blood (Chapters 12 and 25). Harcourt's arc.

**Rules to honor.** Harcourt is compact and sharp. She uses "property," "corpuscles," and "contagion," and speaks of "the subject".

**Target length.** About 800 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 6.1**

**Continuity in.** About four hours after 5.6. They slept briefly. Ewan's oilcloth parcel (4.2) has been on Harcourt's table since it arrived.

**Continuity out.** Grudging respect on both sides. Harcourt stays in Norfolk with the brush while everyone goes north.

**First appearance: Dr. Miriam Harcourt.**

* Mid-forties, compact, sharp. Spectacles. Dark hair scraped back and going grey at the parting. Sleeves pushed to the elbow. A leather notebook at her side. Quick, unsentimental fingers. She catalogues a person the way she would a specimen.  
* Brisk and exact. Period vocabulary only: the blood's "property" or "principle," red and white "corpuscles," "contagion," "disease." She says "the subject." She delivers hard truths unsoftened.  
* She does not look up when they enter.  
* Her detachment has a cause (a supernatural patient she once treated as a specimen). Do not reveal it here. It cracks in 12.x.

**Alexander.** Wants Catherine treated as a person. Stays courteous but goes quieter as he presses. Correcting her on the name is his first act of command in the hidden world.

**Niall.** Present, silent. He has known Harcourt for years and reads the crack before Alexander does.

**The parcel.** A small painter's brush, bristles stiff with dried blood. Ewan's careful pencil copy of the swallow he found painted in blood on the waystone.

**Location.** The hidden infirmary. Steel tables, gas lamps turned up too bright for comfort. Underground.

**Rules in play.**

* The blood on the brush is Catherine's from before the bite (the swallow marks were made while she was still human, 3.3). Harcourt cannot know that yet. She should not state it.  
* Blood spoils within hours. Everything she can learn comes from what has dried on the bristles.  
* Harcourt finds nothing yet. The unidentified property is reported in 12.x and told to Alexander at the book's end. No finding, no hope, and no cure in this scene.

**Threads touched.** The cure search begins at this table (its scientific heart). Harcourt's arc, Chapters 12 and 25\. The brush (12.x). The swallow as Catherine's emblem.

#### **Scene 2\. Seraphina and the seal**

**Scene summary.** Seraphina has walked over from her cottage and arrived first, as she always does. She has Edward's copy of the deed under her scrying water again. Alexander has known her all his life as his mother's odd friend with the strange eye, and now he watches her tell him what the covenant is: an ancient law bound to land, rightful authority, blood, and invitation. A lawful sale let Hessian past the outer protections. The deepest seal has never answered to anyone who was not a Sterling by birth, whatever paper changes hands above it, and so a Sterling may be needed at the crossing itself. He asks her what she felt in Hessian at the festival. Her eye silvers, her hand finds the amulet, and she answers something adjacent to the question. Alexander notices, as his mother once did, that she has told him the exact truth in a shape that answers nothing. The scene establishes a covenant rule and keeps Seraphina's concealed failure in view.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to understand the covenant, and what Seraphina felt at the festival.

**Obstacle.** Seraphina's evasion.

**Turning point.** She answers an adjacent question.

**Consequence.** He understands a rule, and that Seraphina is hiding something.

**Location.** The council chamber annex, with the scrying basin on the table.

**Characters.** Alexander, Seraphina, Niall.

**Weather and light.** Underground and lamplit.

**Mood.** Mysterious and evasive.

**Date and time of day.** Wednesday 1 December 1852, about two in the afternoon.

**Time since previous scene.** About three hours.

**Scene duration.** About thirty minutes.

**Carried forward.** Edward's copy of the deed.

**Setups and plants.** Sterling blood at the crossing (Chapters 14 and 22). Seraphina's secret (Chapter 13).

**Rules to honor.** The covenant is bound to land, authority, blood, and invitation. The deepest seal answers to Sterling guardianship.

**Target length.** About 700 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 6.2**

**Continuity in.** About three hours after 6.1. Seraphina has walked over from her cottage and arrived before anyone. Edward's hand-copied deed (2.x) lies under her scrying water again.

**Continuity out.** Alexander has a covenant rule and knows Seraphina is holding something back. The argument for a Sterling at the crossing is planted for 6.4.

**Seraphina (returning).** Tall, spare, upright. Left eye altered: it catches light strangely, and goes silver when magic, deep feeling or the truth her oath forbids comes close. Her hand goes to the amulet at her throat. Dark hair with one white streak. Plain grey or moss wool. She speaks to her scrying water rather than to the person in the room. Melodic, deliberate, with occasional archaic phrasing. Surprise: "Well. That's not nothing."

**Alexander.** Has known her all his life as his mother's odd friend. Now he listens as a student. He notices, as his mother did in 2.x, that she has given him the exact truth in a shape that answers nothing. Asks questions rather than accusing.

**Niall.** Present. His father's notebooks touch the covenant lore. He listens for cadence, not content.

**What Seraphina can say.** The covenant is an ancient law bound to land, rightful authority, blood and invitation. A lawful sale let Hessian past the outer protections. The deepest seal answers to Sterling guardianship, whatever paper changes hands above it. A Sterling may be needed at the crossing itself.

**What she cannot say.** In Hessian's distortion at the festival she felt an echo of an old, hunted bloodline (Amelia's), and has stayed silent under her oath to Anton Veress. Her adjacent answer should be true and about something else: how his concealment was laid over what he is. Do not let her name the echo.

**Location.** The council chamber annex, scrying basin on the table. It is a different room from the cottage. Underground, lamplit.

**Rules in play.** Ink at Hessian's signature darkened rather than ran (2.x). Her defining line ("The covenant does not respond to possession. It responds to purpose.") is reserved for Ch. 17 and 23\. Use at most a softer precursor.

**Threads touched.** Sterling blood at the crossing (14.x, 22.x). Seraphina's concealed failure (breaks in 13.x, with the eye going dark for good). The oath.

**Flags.** The outline says a Sterling may be needed at the crossing, but Edward is a Sterling by birth and is already north. It does not say why Alexander is needed too. **DECIDED:** Seraphina frames it as a hazard, not a prophecy. The crossing can be made once, and guardianship is carried by blood, so if Edward is lost at the crossing another Sterling must be at hand. This foreshadows 17.x without saying it. Logged for the bible.

#### **Scene 3\. Terms in the yard**

**Scene summary.** At night in the lower training yard, with his coat off despite the cold, Niall puts live steel in Alexander's hand for the first time. "Your shoulders give you away. Every time. Again." For a quarter of an hour Alexander is only a man being tested, and he is grateful for it. Afterward, on the bench against the wall that has heard every hard conversation they have ever had, they set terms. In the field Alexander will obey Niall without argument, heir or not. In return Niall will answer anything Alexander asks, whenever he asks it. Niall agrees to both, and something in his stillness says he knows exactly which question Alexander has not thought to ask. The scene establishes the chain of command that the climax will invert, and the romance's register of care expressed through instruction.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to be ready, and to know where he stands with Niall.

**Obstacle.** Physical, since his body gives him away, and relational, in the rank inversion.

**Turning point.** The terms are set: obedience in the field for honesty on demand.

**Consequence.** The chain of command is fixed, and the climax will invert it. Niall knows which question hasn't been asked.

**Location.** The lower training yard at night, lit by a lantern hung on the wall.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall.

**Weather and light.** A clear, hard frost, a waning gibbous moon not yet up, and bright stars. Their breath smokes.

**Mood.** Physical and intimate.

**Date and time of day.** Wednesday 1 December 1852, about eight to nine in the evening.

**Time since previous scene.** About six hours.

**Scene duration.** About an hour.

**Carried forward.** The promise from 5.6.

**Setups and plants.** "Again." The terms (Chapter 19, scene 6, and Chapter 23, scene 3).

**Rules to honor.** Care through instruction. Niall takes his coat off despite the cold.

**Target length.** About 900 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 6.3**

**Continuity in.** About six hours after 6.2. Niall promised at dawn (5.6): "Then ask me... I'll not decide for you again what you're fit to carry." It is night on the same day.

**Continuity out.** The chain of command is fixed and the climax will invert it (19.6, 23.3). Niall knows the one question Alexander has not thought to ask.

**Niall.** Coat off despite the frost. Instructs with few words. Corrects the wrist or shoulder and takes his hand away a moment too soon, as always. His agreement to both terms is sincere, and his stillness carries what he still holds (Calum). Write him withholding, never lying. "Alex" is available in private; use it only if it lands.

**Alexander.** For a quarter of an hour he is only a man being tested, and he is grateful. He learns what the body gives away. His flaw shows in the bargain: he asks for honesty but not the right question.

**The terms.**

* In the field Alexander obeys Niall without argument, heir or not.  
* In return Niall answers anything Alexander asks, whenever he asks.

**Location.** The lower training yard at night. The old chestnut butt as target. The bench against the wall that has heard every hard conversation. One lantern hung on the wall.

**Rules in play.**

* Live steel: the first time it is his hand, not the blunt practice knife (which he still keeps in Niall's diagram, 6.6). Silver-edged is optional here.  
* Weather: clear, hard frost. Waning gibbous moon not yet up. Bright stars. Breath smokes.  
* Lesson count: seven years now (Oct 1845 to Dec 1852). Use "seven years." The outline elsewhere says "six," which was true in autumn 1851\.  
* Line: "Your shoulders give you away. Every time. Again."

**Threads touched.** "Again." The terms (19.6, 23.3). The unasked question about Calum (10.4). Care through instruction, the romance's register.

#### **Scene 4\. The smuggler**

**Scene summary.** Beneath the stables, in a vault older than the horses above it, Gideon Wren spins a stamped metal token on a crate lid while he thinks. He is quick, clever, and forgettable by design, with ink ground into the creases of his right hand. He offers no condolences, only routes. There is a man on foot near the coast road, twice this week, asking the wrong questions of the right people, so the Institute is watching the railheads. His rates have gone up because his usefulness has. "A man who tells you exactly what he wants is usually lying about something else entirely," he says. When Alexander suggests his word is cheap, he is stung, and for a moment it shows. Beatrice sets the terms. Her official party, with herself, Sebastian, Seraphina, and a handful of operatives, will go north by sea. Alexander will go by Gideon's land route with Niall and Ewan, with Beatrice's knowledge though not yet her blessing, and under Niall's authority. Sebastian objects that the heir should not travel without command of his own rescue, and Seraphina's point about the seal is what ends the argument. The scene introduces Gideon, splits the forces, and puts Alexander's lack of standing into policy.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants a route north, and permission to take it.

**Obstacle.** Political. Sebastian objects and Beatrice sets conditions.

**Turning point.** Seraphina's seal argument ends the debate.

**Consequence.** The forces split. Alexander goes by land with Gideon.

**Location.** The vault beneath the stables, with the smuggler's table.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall, Gideon, Beatrice, Sebastian, Seraphina.

**Weather and light.** Underground, smelling of horse and damp.

**Mood.** Brisk and transactional.

**Date and time of day.** Wednesday 1 December 1852, about eleven at night.

**Time since previous scene.** About two hours.

**Scene duration.** About forty-five minutes.

**Carried forward.** Seraphina's point from 6.2.

**Setups and plants.** Gideon's token (Chapter 11). The Institute watching railheads (Chapter 7). The sea party.

**Rules to honor.** Gideon is quick and forgettable, with ink ground into his right hand, and he spins a token.

**Target length.** About 900 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 6.4**

**Continuity in.** About two hours after 6.3. Seraphina's covenant point (6.2). Alexander has come in from the yard.

**Continuity out.** The forces are split: Beatrice's official party goes north by sea, Alexander by Gideon's land route with Niall and Ewan. Alexander's standing is now written into policy: under Niall's authority, with Beatrice's knowledge though not yet her blessing.

**First appearance: Gideon Wren.**

* Human. Narrow, quick, thirties. A clever, ordinary face built to be forgotten. Ink ground into the creases of his right hand. A good coat worn badly on purpose.  
* Fast, irreverent, transactional, openly mercenary about his own honesty. "A man who tells you exactly what he wants is usually lying about something else entirely." He spins a stamped metal token on a crate lid while he thinks.  
* Offers no condolences, only routes. His rates have gone up because his usefulness has.  
* Stung, for a moment, when Alexander suggests his word is cheap. Let it show and pass.  
* His loyalty is eventually given freely (refuses payment in 25.x). Not yet.

**Information he carries.** A man on foot near the coast road twice this week, asking the wrong questions of the right people. The Institute is watching the railheads.

**Beatrice.** Sets terms with a plain voice. Her authority here is emergency authority, not command. She weighs Alexander again.

**Sebastian.** Objects that the heir should not travel without command of his own rescue. Presents self-interest as concern. Do not hint at Institute ties.

**Seraphina.** Her seal argument (6.2 flag) ends the debate.

**Alexander.** Wants a route and permission. Holds his temper. He has no rank, so he argues as a person, not as an heir.

**Location.** The vault beneath the stables, older than the horses above it. Horse and damp. Gideon's table, crates, forged manifests.

**Rules in play.**

* The sea party: Beatrice, Sebastian, Seraphina and a handful of operatives. (Captain Ellery leads the operatives in the field; she is introduced on the page later.) Do not name her here unless a later chapter demands it.  
* The land party: Alexander, Niall (authority), Ewan (courier), Gideon (guide). Ewan's role at this point is still to be settled (6.6).

**Threads touched.** Gideon's token (11.x). The Institute at the railheads (7.x). Sebastian's opposition. The sea party's late arrival at Poolewe (12.x).

#### **Scene 5\. The gatehouse**

**Scene summary.** This is a home-front interlude from Thomas Barrow's point of view. Wagons are moving at first light, crates chalk-marked in a code only the household can read, and Inspector Pike arrives on foot to watch them go. Thomas tells him it is winter stores, moved before the roads close, as they are most years. Every word is true, and Pike knows the shape of what he is not being told. Pike touches his hat and walks back down the drive. O'Brien, afterward, makes a note in his ledger without comment and tells Thomas he did well, better than most men manage when telling that particular truth. Thomas feels the discomfort of a man whose careful honesty has done the work of a lie. The scene carries Thomas's steward arc and keeps Pike's file open.

**Perspective and goal.** Thomas (interlude). He wants to protect the house without lying to Pike.

**Obstacle.** Pike's patience.

**Turning point.** Pike touches his hat and leaves.

**Consequence.** Thomas is uncomfortable, and O'Brien approves.

**Location.** The gatehouse and the drive.

**Characters.** Thomas, Pike, O'Brien, and wagoners.

**Weather and light.** First light, hoarfrost on the drive, a pale sky.

**Mood.** Wary courtesy.

**Date and time of day.** Thursday 2 December 1852, about quarter to eight in the morning (sunrise 7:51).

**Time since previous scene.** About nine hours.

**Scene duration.** About fifteen minutes.

**Carried forward.** Beatrice's party's crates, on their way to the railway and then the steamer.

**Setups and plants.** Pike's file. Thomas's truth-telling (Chapter 12, scene 5, and Chapter 25).

**Target length.** About 600 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 6.5**

**Continuity in.** About nine hours after 6.4. Beatrice's party's crates are moving at first light to the railway and then the steamer.

**Continuity out.** Pike walks back down the drive with a stone set down that he means to pick up again. O'Brien approves. Thomas is uneasy.

**Thomas Barrow (POV).** In his forties, big-handed, fair and freckled from outdoor work, in a coat too formal for what he does in it. A respectful Norfolk voice, plainer with every week of responsibility. His discipline is that every word he says to Pike is true. He is uncomfortable because careful honesty has done the work of a lie.

**Inspector Elias Pike (returning).** About forty, heavy-lidded, greying moustache, a brown coat too warm for the weather. Arrives on foot. Patient questions, exact repetition of what does not fit. He never raises his voice. He sets a question down the way a man sets down a stone he means to pick up again. He is never read in and chooses not to be. He touches his hat and leaves.

**O'Brien.** Makes a note in his ledger without comment. Tells Thomas he did well, better than most men manage when telling that particular truth.

**Location.** The gatehouse and the drive. First light, hoarfrost on the drive, pale sky. Wagons, crates chalk-marked in a household code.

**Rules in play.**

* Sunrise 7:51.  
* "Winter stores, moved before the roads close" is true in every word (Thomas's line, per outline).  
* Alexander is still in the castle. Thomas must not lie if asked. Have Pike not ask, or have Thomas answer truthfully and narrowly.

**Threads touched.** Pike's open file (his line to Alexander in 26.x: "I'll not close the file. I'll simply stop reading it."). Thomas's truth-telling (12.5, 25.x). Ivor Vane's firm (the loose thread Pike will not pull). The crate code.

#### **Scene 6\. Through the wall**

**Scene summary.** In the armory Niall lays out Edward's reserve sword, plain and silver-etched, and from a locked cabinet a case of silver-edged throwing knives in fitted felt. Alexander adds two things of his own to his kit and does not explain them: the blunt practice knife still wrapped in Niall's diagram, and Catherine's swallow handkerchief. At the gap in the estate wall that no map marks, Gideon waits with a lantern shuttered to a thread of light. Ewan is told he has done enough and can go home, and he turns his cap once in his hands and says he will not go home while His Grace's sister is somewhere he might be of use. Somewhere past the second mile the wards fall away. There is no gate, only a change in the air, as though Sterling land has let go of Alexander's coat. Niall, who once did the same thing leaving Edinburgh, tells him it does not get easier with looking back. The scene is the threshold crossing, and it plants the knife and the handkerchief in the field kit.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to leave with everything he needs.

**Obstacle.** Fear, and the pull of home.

**Turning point.** The wards fall away past the second mile.

**Consequence.** The threshold is crossed. He's armed and afraid.

**Location.** The armory, then the gap in the estate wall and the dark lane beyond.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall, Gideon, Ewan.

**Weather and light.** About five in the morning, pitch dark and bitter, with a rising frost fog. Gideon's lantern is shuttered to a thread.

**Mood.** Departure, and the start of real danger.

**Date and time of day.** Friday 3 December 1852, about five in the morning.

**Time since previous scene.** About a day. Thursday was spent in preparation.

**Scene duration.** About forty minutes.

**Carried forward.** The terms from 6.3.

**Setups and plants.** The knife in its diagram and the swallow handkerchief now travel with him. Edward's reserve sword.

**Rules to honor.** Silver-edged throwing knives. Edward's silver-etched reserve sword is plain.

**Target length.** About 800 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 6.6**

**Continuity in.** About a day after 6.5. Thursday was spent preparing. It is the small hours of Friday 3 December.

**Continuity out.** The threshold is crossed: armed, afraid and resolved. Sets up the road north (Ch. 7).

**Alexander.** Quiet. He adds two things to his kit and explains neither: the blunt practice knife still wrapped in Niall's hand-drawn grip diagram, and Catherine's swallow handkerchief (one of the two from 1.2). No breast-pocket touch yet: the gesture belongs to the signet and first appears at 20.5. He does not look back until the second mile.

**Niall.** Lays out the kit, checking each piece. He left Edinburgh as a nineteen-year-old. His line: it does not get easier with looking back.

**Ewan.** Told he has done enough and can go home. He turns his cap once in his hands and says he will not go home while His Grace's sister is somewhere he might be of use. He is 24\. "Sir" to Alexander.

* Edward's reserve sword, plain and silver-etched.  
* A case of silver-edged throwing knives in fitted felt, from a locked cabinet.
