## **PART THREE: LOCH MAREE**

---

### **Chapter 11: The Price of Mercy**

*Loch Maree, Scottish Highlands, Winter 1852*

**CHAPTER OVERVIEW**

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to return the foal, reach his parents, and be recognized.

**Obstacle.** Rhoswen's terms, Edward's anger at Amelia's letter, and the unresolved rupture with Niall.

**Turning point.** Rhoswen accepts him, and Amelia names what they did: "Control."

**Consequence.** He ends the chapter by the fire beside Niall, still unreconciled but at peace in the silence, as his mother watches. He is warm, and the Midpoint is coming. That sets up Chapter 12\.

**Span and length.** Thursday 16 December 1852, from dusk to about two the next morning. Target about 4,600 words.

#### **Scene 1\. The still water**

**Scene summary.** They come down to the loch at dusk on foot, leading the horses, because Beag will no longer be hurried and nobody has the heart to try. It is mid-December. The islands sit black on water so still it looks poured. Ewan, limping on his fence-rail splint, says his grandfather claimed the loch goes quiet like this when something below is listening. Gideon tries a joke and lets it trail off, because the place will not take one. Beag stops at the shingle's edge and lifts her head. The scene sets atmosphere and returns the book to the prequel's ground, seven years on.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to get the foal home.

**Obstacle.** Beag's slowness, and the uncanny loch.

**Turning point.** Beag stops and lifts her head.

**Consequence.** Something is listening.

**Location.** The eastern end of Loch Maree, on the shingle shore.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall, Gideon, Ewan, Beag.

**Weather and light.** A still dusk, black water like glass, and islands. Sunset is about 3:13, and a half moon is up in the southeast.

**Mood.** Hushed.

**Date and time of day.** Thursday 16 December 1852, about three in the afternoon.

**Time since previous scene.** About a day.

**Scene duration.** About fifteen minutes.

**Carried forward.** The birch bark.

**Setups and plants.** Rhoswen.

**Target length.** About 500 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 11.1**

**Continuity in.** About a day after 10.6. Alexander and Niall have not spoken since the birch wood beyond what the road demands. Ewan is riding with his fence-rail splint. Beag has slowed to a walk nobody has the heart to hurry.

**Continuity out.** Something below is listening. Beag has stopped and lifted her head.

**Alexander.** Wants to get the foal home. Quiet, tired, hollowed by cold. He is aware of the loch before he can say why.

**Ewan.** Home ground. His grandfather said the loch goes quiet like this when something below is listening. He is limping and does not complain.

**Gideon.** Tries a joke and lets it trail off. The loch will not take one.

**Niall.** Wary. He was here in 1845 and knows what waits.

**Beag.** Stops at the shingle's edge and lifts her head. Still favors the foreleg when tired.

**Location.** The eastern end of Loch Maree, on the shingle shore. Islands sit black on water still as glass. The eastern shore and village are Ewan's home (cottages, some with black cloth on the doors from 1845). About 3 p.m.

**Rules in play.**

* Sunset about 3:13. Horses press together, ears toward the water; no birds call. Even a joke will not take.  
* The Vanguard places no claim on the loch or its inhabitants.

**Threads touched.** Rhoswen. The birch bark. The prequel's ground, seven years on.

**Flags.** The outline has "a half moon up in the southeast." On 16 December the moon is about five days old, a young waxing crescent. **DECIDED:** a thick crescent low in the south-southeast, a little short of a half. It sets in the evening, so the watch in 11.6 is under stars only.

#### **Scene 2\. Rhoswen**

**Scene summary.** The water breaks all at once in a wall of foam and black shape, larger than any of the horses: the great scaled neck, the mane of weed. Niall goes carefully still, and his breath stops rather than slows. He remembers the chain, the silver, and how close he came. Alexander does not decide to kneel. He finds himself on the frozen shingle with his hands already open, as he was on the boggart's stair, as his father once knelt in a cave he never saw. Beag goes past him into the shallows and presses against her mother's flank. Rhoswen makes a sound low in her chest, not quite a word, the sound of grief answered. Then she lowers her head to Alexander. She presses clawed fingertips first to his temple, where Edward had scars and Alexander has none, and then to the pulse in his throat. It is the same gesture the prequel records with his father. She knows the family in him. She does not speak. The scene opens the mercy debt that becomes decisive at the climax, fulfilling the prequel's promise that Rhoswen's help is always chosen and never commanded.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to survive meeting Rhoswen.

**Obstacle.** Terror, and Niall's memory of her.

**Turning point.** Alexander kneels, and she touches his temple and throat.

**Consequence.** The mercy debt is opened.

**Location.** The shallows.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall, Beag, Rhoswen.

**Weather and light.** Foam, black water, and the last light.

**Mood.** Awe.

**Date and time of day.** Thursday 16 December 1852, about quarter past three.

**Time since previous scene.** About ten minutes.

**Scene duration.** About ten minutes.

**Carried forward.** The gesture from 5.4, and Edward's kneeling from the prequel.

**Setups and plants.** Chapters 21 and 24 (the touch recurs).

**Rules to honor.** Rhoswen does not speak (her speech is reserved). She touches the temple and the throat as she did to Edward in the prequel.

**Target length.** About 800 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 11.2**

**Continuity in.** About ten minutes after 11.1. Beag has lifted her head to the still water.

**Continuity out.** The mercy debt is opened. Rhoswen has touched him. She does not speak.

**Rhoswen (first on the page in Inheritance; last seen in the prequel).**

* Water-horse form: great scaled neck, mane of weed and coarse black strands, larger than any of the horses. Human form: a woman neither young nor old with sharp strange features, beautiful the way a Highland storm is. Silver eyes, fine scales at the temples and backs of the hands. Dark reed-tangled hair. Silver burn scars across the palms from the Ashcroft chain (1845).  
* She almost never speaks. Her one clear line in the prequel is "Not the little ones." No true speech here. Communication is through water and gesture.  
* She is not a mount or a pet. Her help is chosen and limited. She is descended from Eithne (the Sterlings do not learn this yet).  
* She touched Edward's old scars at the temple and then the pulse in his throat. She gives Alexander the same touch.

**Beag.** Goes past Alexander into the shallows and presses against her mother's flank. Rhoswen makes a sound low in her chest, not quite a word, the sound of grief answered.

**Alexander.** Does not decide to kneel. He finds himself on frozen shingle with his hands already open, as on the boggart's stair (5.4). He does not know his father knelt unarmed in a cave he never saw. He does not command, plead or bargain.

**Niall.** Goes carefully still; his breath stops rather than slows. He remembers the chain, the silver, and how close he came.

**Location.** The shallows at the eastern end. Foam, black water, the last light.

**Rules in play.**

* Rhoswen does not speak.  
* She touches the temple where Edward had scars (Alexander has none) and then the throat.  
* The debt is not named. It is simply opened, and comes due at 21.x and 24.x.

**Threads touched.** The open-hands gesture (5.4, 21.x, 24.x). Edward's kneeling in the prequel. The mercy debt that proves decisive at the climax.

**Flags.** The outline has her lower her head (horse form) and then press clawed fingertips (a hand). Both cannot be true at once. **DECIDED:** she rises from the shallows in the half-shifted, unstable shape the prequel shows, scaled shoulder and hand, and touches him in human form. Keep the transition brief.

#### **Scene 3\. Her terms**

**Scene summary.** The Vanguard camp lies on the far shore, and the only quick way across is through her water. Rhoswen shows them the passage the old way, with a current that pulls a stick out of Ewan's hand and lays it at the mouth of a channel between two worn standing stones. She will carry them once, and only without metal. Niall, who has learned her rules the hard way, makes them strip everything: blades, buckles, Edward's sword, Alexander's knife case. Gideon holds his stamped iron token a long moment. He has never once in his life had cause to leave it behind, and he sets it on the pile. Ewan, who cannot swim with his leg, will row the whole pile round the head of the loch in the morning. The crossing is cold, green, and silent, and it asks them to trust a guide they cannot command. The scene establishes Rhoswen's terms as a rule the book will test twice more, at the one-way passage and when Percival tries to cheat it.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to reach the camp.

**Obstacle.** Her terms: no metal.

**Turning point.** Gideon gives up his iron token.

**Consequence.** The crossing is made through her water.

**Location.** The two standing stones, then the channel across an arm of the loch.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall, Gideon, Ewan (who stays with the pile), Rhoswen.

**Weather and light.** Dark, freezing water, and a green gloom.

**Mood.** Trust tested.

**Date and time of day.** Thursday 16 December 1852, about four in the afternoon.

**Time since previous scene.** About thirty minutes.

**Scene duration.** About forty minutes.

**Carried forward.** Her terms.

**Setups and plants.** Ewan rows the steel round in the morning. The one-way passage (Chapter 14).

**Target length.** About 700 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 11.3**

**Continuity in.** About thirty minutes after 11.2. Rhoswen has accepted Alexander. The Vanguard camp is on the far shore and there is no quick way round.

**Continuity out.** The crossing is made through her water. Ewan will row the steel round in the morning.

**Rhoswen.** No speech. She shows them the passage the old way: a current pulls a stick out of Ewan's hand and lays it at the mouth of a channel between two worn standing stones. She will carry them once, and only without metal.

**Niall.** Has learned her rules the hard way (1845). Makes them strip everything: blades, buckles, Edward's sword, Alexander's knife case. He goes first.

**Alexander.** Trusts a guide he cannot command. This is the second time in a day his hands are open and empty. Keeps the swallow handkerchief (cloth) and the birch bark.

**Gideon.** Holds his stamped iron token a long moment. He has never once had cause to leave it behind. He sets it on the pile. Do not make it a joke.

**Ewan.** Cannot swim with his leg. He will row the whole pile round the head of the loch in the morning. He is 24, tired, and steady.

**Location.** The two standing stones at a channel mouth, then the channel across an arm of the loch. Dark, freezing water, green gloom. About 4 p.m. The crossing is cold, green and silent.

**Rules in play.**

* No metal. This is the first of three tests of her terms. The second is the one-way passage (14.x). The third is when Percival tries to cheat it (later).  
* Metal includes buckles and Gideon's token.

**Threads touched.** Ewan rows the steel round (12.x). The one-way passage (14.x). Rhoswen's terms.

**Flags.** The Location Bible puts the standing stones at the western end, on the northern shore, and says the same stones are where Seraphina breaks her oath (13.3) and where the party gathers before the refuge (14.x). The outline has the party arriving at the eastern end and crossing from stones there. **DECIDED:** the eastern landing has its own pair of stones (the "eastern stones"). The pair on the northern shore (13.3, 14.x) stay "the standing stones." Both pairs mark Rhoswen's water. Logged for the Location Bible.

#### **Scene 4\. The camp**

**Scene summary.** On the far shore, lantern light and canvas. Amelia sees them first and comes across the frozen grass faster than dignity allows, and Alexander finds he is running before he has decided to. Edward comes more slowly, a man who learned long ago not to trust his legs at speed, and he does not embrace his son so much as grip him by both shoulders and look. Niall tells them, dry as ever, that he caught Alexander on the ice at the crossing, and that the heir has apparently decided the story reads better if the catching sounds mutual. Amelia laughs, low and real. "It builds character, my love." "It builds pneumonia, Amelia." For a quarter of an hour, by the fire, it is almost the old family. The scene gives the reader the full warmth the next six chapters will take away.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants his parents.

**Obstacle.** None. This is the reunion.

**Turning point.** "It builds character," "It builds pneumonia."

**Consequence.** Warmth.

**Location.** The Vanguard camp on the far shore, with canvas tents and a fire.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall, Gideon, Edward, Amelia, and camp operatives.

**Weather and light.** A frosty night with fires and lantern light.

**Mood.** Joy.

**Date and time of day.** Thursday 16 December 1852, about seven in the evening.

**Time since previous scene.** About two hours.

**Scene duration.** About fifteen minutes.

**Carried forward.** Soaked clothes from the crossing.

**Setups and plants.** The quarrel (11.5).

**Rules to honor.** The "For Crown and Kin" exchange, spoken as Edward and Niall speak it.

**Target length.** About 700 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 11.4**

**Continuity in.** About two hours after 11.3. Everyone is soaked and cold. The camp fires are ahead.

**Continuity out.** For a quarter of an hour it is almost the old family. Warmth the next six chapters will take away.

**Amelia.** Sees them first and comes across the frozen grass faster than dignity allows. Dry wit, cutting. Field leathers, cloak, hair braided. Thin silver-grey lines beneath the skin of her palms. She laughs, low and real. Her line: "It builds character, my love."

**Edward.** Comes more slowly, a man who learned long ago not to trust his legs at speed (not injury: discipline). Does not embrace. Grips his son by both shoulders and looks. Grave, economical. Greying dark hair silvered at the temples, scarred hands. His answer: "It builds pneumonia, Amelia."

**Niall.** Reports dry as ever. His joke to the parents (he caught Alexander on the ice at the crossing, and the heir has apparently decided the story reads better if the catching sounds mutual) is Niall performing normalcy for their sake. The rupture (10.4) is still open. The "For Crown and Kin" exchange: Niall says it, Edward inclines his head and answers with it.

**Alexander.** Finds he is running before he decides to. The half-smile is real for the first time in days. He does not mention the birch wood.

**Location.** The Vanguard camp on the far shore. Canvas tents, a fire, a command tent, a map tent, the watch line. Frosty night, lanterns.

**Rules in play.**

* The exchange as the prequel gives it: Niall: "For Crown and Kin." Edward: "For Crown and Kin." (Later Alexander and Niall repeat it, 26.x.)  
* Camp operatives are unnamed. Captain Ellery arrives with Beatrice's party by sea (12.1).

**Threads touched.** The quarrel (11.5). The "builds character/pneumonia" banter (planted here for 17.x memory).

**Flags.** The outline gives the two lines without speakers. Both address Amelia ("my love" and "Amelia"). **DECIDED:** Amelia says "It builds character, my love" to Edward; Edward answers "It builds pneumonia, Amelia." Amelia calls Edward "my love" (Main Bible).

#### **Scene 5\. Two letters, one argument**

**Scene summary.** Later, Edward sets the two letters side by side on the camp table. He is angry, quietly, as Alexander always is, and Alexander recognizes where he learned it. He forbade the boy, and Amelia overruled him behind his back, in writing, through his own operative. Amelia does not apologize. "He has a right to choose, Edward. We kept him from choosing for twenty-three years." Alexander stands between them, and for the first time in his life he is not the child his parents are sheltering but the adult they are arguing about. When Edward asks him why he came, he answers that it was the one thing in a year that anyone had let him decide. Amelia names what they did, plainly, and it costs her to say it: "Control. We told ourselves we were sparing you. We were deciding for you." Edward does not concede, not tonight. He says only that they meant to tell him at twenty-five, and does not yet say why. The scene makes the theme of protection against agency a family quarrel, and it sets Edward's late turn in motion.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to understand why he was kept out.

**Obstacle.** His father's anger at his mother.

**Turning point.** "Control."

**Consequence.** Edward does not concede. The why of twenty-five is held back.

**Location.** The command tent.

**Characters.** Alexander, Edward, Amelia.

**Weather and light.** Wind on the canvas and a brazier.

**Mood.** Family conflict.

**Date and time of day.** Thursday 16 December 1852, about ten at night.

**Time since previous scene.** About three hours.

**Scene duration.** About thirty minutes.

**Carried forward.** The letters from 4.3.

**Setups and plants.** Edward's turn (Chapter 13, scene 4).

**Target length.** About 900 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 11.5**

**Continuity in.** About three hours after 11.4. The others have gone to bed or to watch. Edward has the two letters (4.3) on the table.

**Continuity out.** Edward has not conceded. The why of twenty-five is held back for 13.4.

**Edward.** Angry, quietly, "as Alexander always is," and Alexander sees where he learned it. Cold and grave, in the language of justice. He forbade the boy. He says only that they meant to tell him at twenty-five, and does not say why (his own initiation at fifteen killed his brother Richard, never spoken of).

**Amelia.** Does not apologize. "He has a right to choose, Edward. We kept him from choosing for twenty-three years." Then, plainly, at cost: "Control. We told ourselves we were sparing you. We were deciding for you."

**Alexander.** Stands between them and is, for the first time, the adult they are arguing about. When Edward asks why he came: it was the one thing in a year anyone had let him decide. Quiet, exact, no shouting. Fingertips tap. His father's anger has taught him his own.

**Location.** The command tent. Wind on the canvas, a brazier.

**Rules in play.**

* Edward set twenty-five as the age of initiation because of 1808 (initiated at fifteen; Richard died). Alexander does not know this.  
* Do not let Amelia soften what she just said.

**Threads touched.** Edward's turn (13.4). Protection against agency, the series theme. The letters. Richard's death, never told to Alexander.

#### **Scene 6\. The watch**

**Scene summary.** Niall takes the first watch without being asked, and Alexander sits it with him. The conversation from the frozen crossing is still unfinished, and Alexander says so, and says he is still not ready for it. Niall accepts that without argument. Neither of them notices Amelia at the tent flap, watching them sit shoulder to shoulder over the fire, not speaking and entirely at ease in the silence. She has always read her son more clearly than Edward has, and she respects his silences. She lets the flap fall and says nothing to anyone. The scene is the romance seen from outside, once, by the parent best placed to see it, and her silence is its own kind of protection. It raises a question she will not live to answer.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to be near Niall without the conversation.

**Obstacle.** He isn't ready.

**Turning point.** Amelia sees them and lets the tent flap fall.

**Consequence.** Rest, and a question she won't live to ask.

**Location.** The camp fire on the watch line.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall, Amelia (observing).

**Weather and light.** Clear skies, a hard frost, and the stars.

**Mood.** Tender.

**Date and time of day.** Friday 17 December 1852, about midnight to one in the morning.

**Time since previous scene.** About two hours.

**Scene duration.** About an hour.

**Carried forward.** The rupture from 10.4.

**Setups and plants.** The repair (Chapter 13).

**Rules to honor.** No confession. Amelia reads Alexander clearly.

**Target length.** About 600 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 11.6**

**Continuity in.** About two hours after 11.5. Friday 17 December, past midnight. Niall has taken the first watch unasked.

**Continuity out.** Rest, and a question Amelia will not live to ask. Sets up the repair (13.2).

**Niall.** Takes the watch without being asked. Accepts without argument that Alexander is not ready. Their shoulders nearly touch. Says nothing.

**Alexander.** Sits the watch with him. Says the conversation is unfinished, and that he is still not ready. Silent, at ease in the silence, without knowing it shows.

**Amelia.** At the tent flap. She reads her son more clearly than Edward does and respects his silences. She lets the flap fall and says nothing to anyone. Do not have her speak or decide. The scene is the romance seen once from outside, by the person best placed to see it.

**Location.** The camp fire on the watch line. Clear sky, hard frost, stars. The young moon has already set.

**Rules in play.** No confession. No word from anyone about what she sees.

**Threads touched.** The repair (13.2). A question she will not live to answer (she dies 19 December).

### **Chapter 12: Crown and Family**

*The Vanguard camp near Loch Maree, Winter 1852*

**CHAPTER OVERVIEW**

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander, with interludes from Harcourt and Thomas. He wants to be heard as a planner and to shape the rescue.

**Obstacle.** Sebastian's needling, Percival's bargain, and his father's pragmatism.

**Turning point.** He finds the timing flaw and Edward backs him. Calum sets the deadline.

**Consequence.** He ends the chapter with part of the recognition he has wanted, knowing the clock is Catherine's recovering will. That sets up the Midpoint in Chapter 13\.

**Span and length.** Friday 17 December to Saturday 18 December 1852\. Target about 4,800 words.

#### **Scene 1\. The party from the sea**

**Scene summary.** Beatrice's party comes in overland from the landing at Poolewe, having gone north by steamer. Beatrice is in practical wool and in command of the ground within a minute of arriving. With her come Sebastian, Seraphina, and a handful of operatives under Captain Ellery, a broad-shouldered woman near forty in a man's riding coat cut down to fit, who speaks in distances and hours. Sebastian's bow to Alexander is a fraction too correct for a Highland camp. He remarks that he understands there was some delay on the road north, a convoy engagement and a rescue that cost the party days. "You'd only have him think you thorough, Sebastian," Beatrice says, "which is a considerably more comfortable word than the one you mean." Ellery answers one of Sebastian's orders with a look, and it is plain this is not the first time. The scene gathers the cast and sharpens the political thread. Ellery is set up as a foil to Sebastian's prejudice.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants Beatrice's support.

**Obstacle.** Sebastian.

**Turning point.** Beatrice's rebuke to Sebastian.

**Consequence.** The cast is gathered and Ellery is established.

**Location.** The camp.

**Characters.** Alexander, Beatrice, Sebastian, Seraphina, Ellery, and operatives.

**Weather and light.** Grey morning light and sleet showers. Sunrise is about 8:39.

**Mood.** Political friction.

**Date and time of day.** Friday 17 December 1852, about ten in the morning.

**Time since previous scene.** About nine hours.

**Scene duration.** About twenty minutes.

**Carried forward.** The delay on the road, reported by Gideon.

**Setups and plants.** Ellery as a foil to Sebastian.

**Rules to honor.** Beatrice's party came by steamer, delayed by gales.

**Target length.** About 700 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 12.1**

**Continuity in.** About nine hours after 11.6. Friday 17 December, mid-morning. Beatrice's party went north by steamer and was delayed by gales; they landed at Poolewe and came overland. Gideon has given the camp his account of the road (the convoy fight, Beag, the days lost).

**Continuity out.** The cast is gathered and Ellery is established as Sebastian's foil. Beatrice has rebuked him in front of the camp.

**First appearance: Captain Ellery.**

* Vanguard field officer. A weathered woman near forty, broad through the shoulders, hair close-pinned, a man's riding coat cut down to fit.  
* Clipped, military, economical. She speaks in distances and hours.  
* She leads the operatives of Beatrice's official party and, at the second assault, lights the field and takes the gate road (22.x).  
* She answers one of Sebastian's orders with a look. It is plain this is not the first time. She is a standing rebuke to his prejudice about who leads.

**Beatrice (returning).** Practical wool. In command of the ground within a minute of arriving. Her rebuke: "You'd only have him think you thorough, Sebastian, which is a considerably more comfortable word than the one you mean." Not yet Acting Commander (Edward lives). Brooch, iron-grey coil.

**Sebastian (returning).** His bow to Alexander is a fraction too correct for a Highland camp. He mentions the delay on the road, having been given Gideon's account at the gate. Do not let it read as inside knowledge. Repeats a phrase for emphasis when he presses ("Until it doesn't.").

**Seraphina.** Arrives with the party, on foot from the landing as always. Eye, amulet.

**Alexander.** Wants Beatrice's backing. Reads the room's politics as he would a document: twice.

**Location.** The camp on the far shore. Grey morning light, sleet showers. Sunrise about 8:39.

**Rules in play.** Beatrice's party came by steamer, delayed by gales.

**Threads touched.** Ellery vs. Sebastian (22.x). The council fracture. The delay on the road.

#### **Scene 2\. The map tent**

**Scene summary.** Edward lays out what they know. Hessian's manor stands on the coast, on the tract Edward sold him, and beneath it lies the ancient refuge Edward never knew was there. Hessian's lawful ownership lets him past the outer protections, and the deepest seal still answers only to Sterling guardianship and to Sterling blood. Catherine is Sterling-blooded, and her willing blood would break it. Amelia explains that her compelled yes answers the seal only in part, which is why Hessian needs a ritual to finish what his compulsion began. Sebastian's ledger counts thrall patrols. Niall, arms folded by the tent flap, is already several conclusions ahead of everyone. Alexander listens more than he speaks, and reads each document twice before he trusts his own voice. The scene sets out the rules of the climax in advance, so the ending will feel earned rather than convenient.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants the rules.

**Obstacle.** Complexity.

**Turning point.** Amelia explains the partial seal.

**Consequence.** The climax mechanics are set out in advance.

**Location.** The map tent.

**Characters.** Alexander, Edward, Amelia, Beatrice, Sebastian, Niall.

**Weather and light.** Sleet on the canvas.

**Mood.** Strategic.

**Date and time of day.** Friday 17 December 1852, about noon.

**Time since previous scene.** About two hours.

**Scene duration.** About an hour.

**Carried forward.** The covenant rules from 6.2.

**Setups and plants.** Chapters 16 and 23\.

**Rules to honor.**, and the compelled yes gives only a partial answer.

**Target length.** About 800 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 12.2**

**Continuity in.** About two hours after 12.1. Noon. The council and the Sterlings gather in the map tent.

**Continuity out.** The climax's mechanics are set out in advance. Nothing here is news to the reader who knows 6.2.

**Edward.** Lays out the ground: Hessian's manor on the coast, on the tract Edward sold, and beneath it the ancient refuge Edward never knew was there. Grave, economical.

**Amelia.** Explains the partial seal: Catherine's compelled yes answers it only in part, which is why Hessian needs a ritual to finish what his compulsion began. Clinical, diagnostic.

**Sebastian.** His ledger counts thrall patrols. Quiet, exacting. Do not let it hint at an Institute source.

**Niall.** Arms folded by the tent flap, already several conclusions ahead. He knows Calum is at the manor and has not said so aloud (10.4).

**Alexander.** Listens more than he speaks and reads each document twice before he trusts his own voice.

**Location.** The map tent. Sleet on the canvas.

**Rules in play.**

* Lawful ownership (Edward's sale) let Hessian past the outer protections.  
* The deepest seal answers only to Sterling guardianship and Sterling blood. Catherine is Sterling-blooded. Her willing blood would break it.  
* Her compelled yes gives only a partial answer (Series Bible). Hessian needs the ritual to finish it.  
* Edward and Amelia do not yet know Catherine has been Turned (13.1).

**Threads touched.** Chapters 16 and 23 (the ritual and Alexander's claim). The covenant rules from 6.2.

#### **Scene 3\. Percival's offer**

**Scene summary.** Percival Ashcroft walks into the camp perimeter unannounced, immaculate, his boots barely marking the frost. He calls Alexander "Sterling" three times in as many minutes and never uses his title. He offers the schematics of a galvanic engine that can suppress wards in a local area for a measured window, in exchange for access to whatever lies beneath the manor. He does not love the family, he says, but he loves even less the idea of Hessian's apparatus running loose on British soil with no one's hand on the leash. Alexander objects out loud and in front of the council: three weeks ago this man had Rhoswen's child in silver mesh. Edward hears him and makes the pragmatic choice anyway, and Beatrice, by protocol, supports it. Then Alexander finds the flaw. The window is measured in minutes and the engine runs above ground, which means whoever works it controls the timing of the entire assault. He insists the signal belong to the party below. "He's right," Edward says. "Draft it that way, Percival, or the machinery stays behind." Percival looks at Alexander for a long moment and says he finds himself less inclined to underestimate him than he was an hour ago. Afterward Niall points out that three "Sterlings" and no title was not carelessness. Percival was reminding him that he has not yet decided Alexander is worth one. The scene gives Alexander the recognition he has wanted from his father all his life, grants it only in part, and plants the machinery and the timing clause the collapse will turn on.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to refuse Percival, then to control the timing.

**Obstacle.** His father's pragmatism.

**Turning point.** He finds the flaw: "He's right."

**Consequence.** Partial recognition. The machinery is planted.

**Location.** The camp perimeter, then the map tent.

**Characters.** Alexander, Edward, Percival, Beatrice, Niall, Amelia.

**Weather and light.** Dusk comes at about 3:13, then early dark.

**Mood.** Tense negotiation.

**Date and time of day.** Friday 17 December 1852, about three to four in the afternoon.

**Time since previous scene.** About three hours.

**Scene duration.** About an hour.

**Carried forward.** Percival followed them (from 9.5).

**Setups and plants.** The override and the early shutdown (Chapters 14 and 17). "Sterling" without a title.

**Target length.** About 1,000 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 12.3**

**Continuity in.** About three hours after 12.2. Dusk falls about 3:13. Percival followed the party from the ford (9.5) and now walks into the perimeter.

**Continuity out.** Partial recognition for Alexander. The machinery and its timing clause are planted for 14.x and 17.x.

**Percival (returning).**

* Immaculate, boots barely marking the frost. Silver-headed cane. Collar a fraction too high over the scar. Charming at a distance, cold up close.  
* Calls Alexander "Sterling" three times in three minutes and never uses his title. He is reminding Alexander that he has not decided he is worth one.  
* Two fingers to the scar when composure slips. Edward gave it to him. This is their first meeting since 1845\.  
* Inventory language: "assets," "stock." Aphoristic when cornered. His defining line ("Do not mistake resemblance for equality") is available but not required here.  
* Real motive under the offer: access to whatever lies beneath the manor (Silas catalogued the cave geometry in 1815).

**The offer.** Schematics of a galvanic engine that can suppress wards in a local area for a measured window, in exchange for access below the manor. He says he does not love the family but loves less the idea of Hessian's apparatus running loose on British soil with no one's hand on the leash.

**Alexander.** Objects out loud in front of the council: a week ago this man had Rhoswen's child in silver mesh. Then finds the flaw: the window is measured in minutes and the engine runs above ground, so whoever works it controls the timing of the assault. He insists the signal belong to the party below. Quiet baritone dropping lower as he presses.

**Edward.** Makes the pragmatic choice, hears his son, and says: "He's right. Draft it that way, Percival, or the machinery stays behind." First public recognition, granted in part.

**Beatrice.** Supports the choice by protocol.

**Niall.** Afterward points out three "Sterlings" and no title was not carelessness.

**Amelia.** Watches Percival's hands.

**Location.** The camp perimeter, then the map tent. Dusk, early dark.

**Rules in play.**

* Percival's successes are always partial. This is a bargain that will be tested.  
* Where Percival's men camp is not in the outline. **DECIDED:** outside the perimeter. He does not know Calum will come (12.6).

**Threads touched.** The override and the early shutdown (14.x, 17.x). "Sterling" without a title. Mutual recognition with Niall.

**Flags.** The outline has Alexander say "three weeks ago" this man had Rhoswen's child in mesh. The ford was 9 December, eight days earlier. **DECIDED:** "a week ago."

#### **Scene 4\. The microscope**

**Scene summary.** This is a home-front interlude from Harcourt's point of view, in the hidden infirmary at Sterling Castle. Under her best lens, Catherine's blood from the brush shows something in its corpuscles she has no word for, a property that behaves like nothing in any Vanguard record. She drafts a letter north and tears it up, because a thing half-seen and reported as seen does more harm than silence. Then she goes down to the sanctuary to ask the boggart family about tracks found near the thinning ward on the fen-side wall. She questions the child the way she would examine a specimen: precise, unhurried, and relentless. The child bolts past Mrs. Ashworth and out through the open door into the snow. Harcourt stands in the empty doorway and remembers, unwillingly, a patient from twenty years ago whom she once treated exactly this way. The scene advances the cure thread by a single honest step, puts the boggart species on the page, and opens Harcourt's arc.

**Perspective and goal.** Harcourt (interlude). She wants the truth from the blood, and then the tracks.

**Obstacle.** Her own method.

**Turning point.** The child bolts, and she remembers a past patient.

**Consequence.** Her arc opens.

**Location.** The infirmary at Sterling Castle, then the sanctuary.

**Characters.** Harcourt, the boggart child, Mrs. Ashworth.

**Weather and light.** Norfolk, with snow falling outside.

**Mood.** Clinical, then self-reckoning.

**Date and time of day.** Friday 17 December 1852, about eight in the evening.

**Time since previous scene.** This runs in parallel with 12.3.

**Scene duration.** About an hour.

**Carried forward.** The brush from 6.1.

**Setups and plants.** Chapter 25 (the finding, and the tracks).

**Target length.** About 800 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 12.4**

**Continuity in.** Friday evening, Norfolk. Snow falling outside. The brush has been on Harcourt's table since 6.1. The outline says this runs parallel with 12.3, but it is hours later. Treat it as the same day.

**Continuity out.** Her arc opens. The cure thread has advanced one honest step.

**Dr. Harcourt (POV).**

* Mid-forties, compact, spectacles, sleeves to the elbow, hair scraped back, leather notebook. Exact period vocabulary: "property," "principle," "corpuscles," "contagion." Delivers truths unsoftened.  
* Under her best lens the blood shows something in its corpuscles she has no word for, a property that behaves like nothing in any Vanguard record.  
* She drafts a letter north and tears it up: a thing half-seen and reported as seen does more harm than silence.  
* She questions the child as she would a specimen: precise, unhurried, relentless. The child bolts.  
* She remembers, unwillingly, a patient of twenty years ago whom she treated exactly this way. The bibles do not say what the patient was. Do not specify.

**The boggart child.** Child-sized, mud-brown, amber eyes, long-fingered hands, wet-reed pelt. Clicks like a snipe. Bolts past Mrs. Ashworth and out into the snow.

**Mrs. Ashworth.** Neat, keys, pencil in her hair. She reads the clicks.

**Location.** The infirmary at Sterling Castle, then the sanctuary. Gas lamps too bright. Snow at the windows.

**Rules in play.**

* The brush blood is from before the bite (10.5, 3.3). It shows a property, not the disease. The idea that the disease might one day be driven back comes only in Ch. 25, from a post-bite sample.  
* She knows no cure and claims none.

**Threads touched.** The finding (25.x). The tracks at the thinning wall (12.5, 25.x). Harcourt's arc: personhood, consent (Dossier).

**Flags.** The Series Bible says Harcourt's sample comes from the brush and that she finds the disease might be driven back. Pre-bite blood cannot show the disease. **DECIDED:** here she finds only the unidentified property. The disease inference comes after she has a post-bite sample (25.x). Logged for the bible.

#### **Scene 5\. Easy now**

**Scene summary.** This interlude is from Thomas Barrow's point of view. He finds the boggart child in the frozen hedge beyond the kitchen garden, clicking like a snipe, and brings it in under his coat with the words that work on frightened stable boys: "Easy now." At the gate he meets Inspector Pike, out walking as he does twice a month. Pike looks at the bundle moving under Thomas's coat and at Thomas's face. He weighs the lie for its shape rather than its content and decides it is not worth the unpicking. He touches his hat and walks on. In the sanctuary, Mrs. Ashworth reads the family's clicks and tells Thomas and Harcourt what the child said: it had not made the tracks, and it will not go near that wall since the ward went thin. Something else has been at the wall. The scene carries Thomas's steward arc and Pike's chosen incuriosity. It plants a small home-front mystery: the tracks belong to an Institute watcher testing the castle while the family is away, which Chapter 25 resolves.

**Perspective and goal.** Thomas (interlude). He wants to recover the child quietly.

**Obstacle.** Pike.

**Turning point.** Pike lets it go.

**Consequence.** The home-front mystery is set.

**Location.** The hedge by the kitchen garden, the gate, and the sanctuary.

**Characters.** Thomas, the boggart child, Pike, Mrs. Ashworth, Harcourt.

**Weather and light.** Snow at night.

**Mood.** Gentle suspense.

**Date and time of day.** Friday 17 December 1852, about half past nine at night.

**Time since previous scene.** About ninety minutes.

**Scene duration.** About forty minutes.

**Carried forward.** The bolt in 12.4.

**Setups and plants.** The Institute watcher (Chapter 25).

**Target length.** About 700 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 12.5**

**Continuity in.** About ninety minutes after 12.4. Snow at night. The child has been out for over an hour.

**Continuity out.** The home-front mystery is set: something else has been at the wall.

**Thomas Barrow (POV).**

* Forties, big-handed, fair and freckled, coat too formal for the work. Norfolk voice, plainer with every week of responsibility.  
* Finds the child in the frozen hedge beyond the kitchen garden and brings it in under his coat. "Easy now" (it works on stable boys, and Niall says it too).  
* His discipline: literal truth. If he answers Pike about the bundle, keep it true and narrow.

**Inspector Pike.** Out walking, as he does twice a month. Heavy-lidded, greying moustache, brown coat too warm. Weighs the story for its shape rather than its content and decides it is not worth the unpicking. Touches his hat and walks on. He is never read in and chooses his own incuriosity.

**Mrs. Ashworth.** Reads the child's clicks aloud for Thomas and Harcourt: it did not make the tracks, and it will not go near that wall since the ward went thin.

**Harcourt.** Present in the sanctuary, quieter than an hour ago.

**Location.** The hedge by the kitchen garden, the gate, and the sanctuary. Snow at night.

**Rules in play.**

* The ward on the fen-side wall has thinned while the family is away.  
* Tracks: a gentleman's boot prints (Location Bible), left by an Institute watcher testing the whole wall. Chapter 25 resolves it. Farmer Hodge's earlier "something at the fences that leaves no track" is a different matter.

**Threads touched.** The Institute watcher (25.x). Pike's chosen incuriosity (26.x). Thomas's steward arc.

#### **Scene 6\. At the ward line**

**Scene summary.** On a ridge of standing pines half a mile north of the camp, where old protective work has discolored the bark, a slight figure waits just outside the line. Calum. Edward meets him with grave courtesy, as a man weighing a debt rather than a threat. Calum gives them what the Mark allows: patrol times, the thrall count, and the ritual chamber beneath the manor. When he tries to say what Catherine is, his voice stops in his throat as though a hand has closed on it, and his own hand goes flat over his heart. That is the Mark, forbidding it. He manages one thing that matters most. Hessian must finish soon, because she is learning. Her yes is failing, day by day, as she learns to tell what she wants from what he has made her want. Alexander says the obvious thing, coldly: Hessian could take him at any moment and turn everything he has just told them against them. Calum agrees, and seems grateful to be spoken to honestly. At the last, he and Niall look at each other across the ward line. "Nillo." "Colie." Then he is gone into the trees. The scene sets the Book One deadline, which is driven by Catherine's own recovering will, and it shows the Mark's limits on the page.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants intelligence.

**Obstacle.** The Mark stops Calum's speech.

**Turning point.** "She's learning."

**Consequence.** The deadline is set.

**Location.** The ward line of standing pines.

**Characters.** Alexander, Edward, Niall, Calum.

**Weather and light.** A cloudy night, with the half moon setting about eleven.

**Mood.** Eerie.

**Date and time of day.** Friday 17 December 1852, about ten at night.

**Time since previous scene.** This runs in parallel with 12.5.

**Scene duration.** About twenty minutes.

**Carried forward.** Calum's resistance from 10.5.

**Setups and plants.** Chapters 13, 16, and 23\.

**Rules to honor.** The Mark can forbid speech.

**Target length.** About 800 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 12.6**

**Continuity in.** Parallel with 12.5, Friday about ten at night. Calum has resisted Hessian since 10.5. The moon is nearly a half and low in the southwest, setting about eleven.

**Continuity out.** The deadline is set: Hessian must finish soon, because Catherine is learning. Calum has vanished into the trees.

**Calum (returning).**

* Slight and wiry, dark brown curls, grey eyes that will not meet anyone's, hands opening and closing. Pale, a hand going flat over his heart when the Mark tightens.  
* Old Town Scots: "lass," "ken," "nae." Apologizes before anyone accuses him. Available line: "I'm sorry. I ken it's nae use to ye, but I'm sorry." (For the gorge and Niall's arm.)  
* Gives what the Mark allows: patrol times, the thrall count, the ritual chamber beneath the manor. When he tries to say what Catherine is, his voice stops in his throat as though a hand has closed on it. He manages the one thing that matters: Hessian must finish soon, because she is learning, and her yes is failing day by day.  
* Grateful to be spoken to honestly.

**Edward.** Meets him with grave courtesy, weighing a debt rather than a threat.

**Alexander.** Coldly says the obvious: Hessian could take him at any moment and turn everything he has just told them against them. Calum agrees.

**Niall.** Across the ward line: "Nillo." "Colie." Then Calum is gone. Do not resolve anything between them.

**Location.** The ridge of standing pines half a mile north of the camp, where old protective work has discolored the bark. Calum waits just outside the line. Cloudy night.

**Rules in play.**

* The Mark can forbid speech. It cannot be argued with.  
* Catherine asked Hessian, about midnight the night before (Thursday 16 into Friday 17 December), to move the ritual into the covenant chamber (14.5). Calum's "ritual chamber" is that. **DECIDED:** the outline's "three nights earlier" in 14.5 counts from Sunday 19, not from this scene.  
* Percival does not know Calum came (12.3 DECIDED).

**Threads touched.** Chapters 13, 16, 23\. Calum's resistance. The covenant chamber.

---

### **Chapter 13: What the Water Keeps**

*Loch Maree, Winter 1852*

**CHAPTER OVERVIEW**

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander, witnessing all three revelations. He wants the truth about Catherine, about Niall, and about his family.

**Obstacle.** What each person has kept hidden.

**Turning point.** Catherine is a vampire, and the family chooses to attack.

**Consequence.** He ends the chapter composed and resolved, closer to Niall, trusted by his father, on the night before the passage. The Midpoint shift: from reaction to action.

**Span and length.** Saturday 18 December 1852\. Target about 4,800 words.

#### **Scene 1\. The birch bark**

**Scene summary.** Amelia asks for the birch bark Alexander has carried since the crossing. At the loch's edge she lays it on the water in a shallow basin, and the water does what water does for her: it remembers. Blood carries traces of the one it came from, and this blood carries cold, and the memory of an opened wrist, and a drink. Amelia's nose begins to bleed. Her hands shake, and the skin beneath her eyes darkens as if bruised, the cost her magic always takes. She does not protect her son from what she has found. She tells Edward and Alexander together, in the same breath, because she has learned. Catherine was bitten and she drank. She is a vampire. There is no partial state and no way back to what she was. Sebastian, at the tent's edge, begins to speak of protocol for the progeny of a hostile maker, and Beatrice cuts him off with a single word. Alexander does not shout. He goes very still. His sister is dead and not dead, and every question the Vanguard asks of a creature now has to be asked of her. This is the Midpoint's dark truth, and it reframes the rescue and the book at once.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to know what his mother sees.

**Obstacle.** The cost to her.

**Turning point.** "She is a vampire."

**Consequence.** The rescue is reframed.

**Location.** The loch edge.

**Characters.** Alexander, Edward, Amelia, Beatrice, Sebastian.

**Weather and light.** A grey morning with a thin, freezing mist.

**Mood.** Dread.

**Date and time of day.** Saturday 18 December 1852, about half past nine in the morning.

**Time since previous scene.** About eleven and a half hours.

**Scene duration.** About twenty-five minutes.

**Carried forward.** The birch bark from 10.3.

**Setups and plants.** Chapters 15, 18, and 23\.

**Rules to honor.** Blood memory. Amelia's nosebleed is the cost of her magic.

**Target length.** About 900 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 13.1**

**Continuity in.** About eleven and a half hours after 12.6. Saturday 18 December, half past nine. The camp has woken to the news that Percival is here and that Calum came in the night. Alexander has carried the birch bark since 10.3 (Calum let it fall; Catherine inked it after the bite).

**Continuity out.** The Midpoint's dark truth is on the table: Catherine is a full vampire. Both parents and the son learn it in the same breath. The rescue is now a rescue of someone changed.

**Amelia (POV object).**

* Field leathers, hair braided, the thin silver-grey lines under the skin of her palms. Dry wit is gone this morning; clinical calm takes its place, which in her means she is frightened.  
* Water-memory: she lays the bark on the surface of a shallow basin at the loch's edge and the water gives up what the blood remembers. Cold. The memory of an opened wrist. A drink.  
* The cost is on the page: nosebleed, trembling hands, the skin beneath her eyes darkening as if bruised. She refuses the chair and refuses help.  
* She tells Edward and Alexander together, in one breath. She has learned (11.5, "we were deciding for you"). No softening and no delay.  
* Available line, plain: "She is a vampire."

**Edward.** Grave and economical. Heels of the hands to the eyes when the weight lands. He does not speak first.

**Alexander.** Does not shout. He goes very still and the fingertips stop. No half-smile. He wants to know what his mother sees, and asks it exactly, once. His sister is dead and not dead, and every question the Vanguard asks of a creature now has to be asked of her.

**Beatrice.** Practical wool, iron-grey coil, brooch. Ends Sebastian's sentence with a single word. Not yet Acting Commander (Edward lives).

**Sebastian.** At the tent's edge, groomed, begins on protocol for the progeny of a hostile maker. Do not let him finish the sentence.

**Location.** The loch's edge near the camp, a shallow basin set on the shingle. Grey morning, thin freezing mist. Sunrise was about 8:39.

**Rules in play.**

* Blood carries traces of the one it came from. Water-memory reads sensation and impression, not a clear film. It cannot name the maker.  
* The blood on the bark is post-bite (10.5). The brush blood (6.1) is pre-bite.  
* Turning: a bite, then drinking the maker's blood. No partial state and no way back that anyone in this scene knows of. Harcourt's later finding (25.x) is not available to them.  
* Vanguard ethic (2.3): reason, hunger, fear, coercion or cruelty, consent. Catherine's case sits inside that question and nobody says so yet.  
* Amelia's magic always costs her. This is her last full day of life (she dies 19 December). Do not foreshadow it.

**Threads touched.** Chapters 15, 18 and 23\. The seal (Catherine's compelled yes, 12.2). Calum's "she is learning" (12.6). Amelia's cost, and the Vanguard's protocol against Alexander's love of his sister.

**Flags.**

* The outline says Beatrice cuts Sebastian off with "a single word" and does not give it. **DECIDED:** "Enough."  
* Whose opened wrist is unstated. **DECIDED:** the reading gives the wrist as an image without a face or name. Hessian is not named by anyone in this scene.

#### **Scene 2\. Unasked**

**Scene summary.** Niall finds Alexander on the shingle and does what he promised in Chapter 5 and failed to do in Chapter 10\. He tells him everything, before Alexander has to ask. He tells him how he learned in 1848 what had been done to Calum. He tells him about two winters of searching, and the shame of having been the one who left Edinburgh a fortnight before Calum vanished. He admits that he was afraid Alexander would look at Calum and see a monster, and more afraid of what Alexander would think of a man who had loved someone who became one. Alexander hears the word "loved" and lets it pass without examining it. Niall does not notice it either. "Next time," Alexander says, "tell me before I have to ask." "Aye." It is not forgiveness yet, but it is the ground forgiveness will be built on. The scene begins the repair of trust and takes the romance one visible degree further. It also shows Niall's arc, from protective secret-keeper to honest equal, turning.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants honesty.

**Obstacle.** None. Niall comes on his own.

**Turning point.** The word "loved."

**Consequence.** The ground for trust is laid.

**Location.** The shingle.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall.

**Weather and light.** The mist is lifting.

**Mood.** Tender.

**Date and time of day.** Saturday 18 December 1852, about eleven in the morning.

**Time since previous scene.** About ninety minutes.

**Scene duration.** About thirty minutes.

**Carried forward.** The rupture from 10.4.

**Setups and plants.** "Tell me before I have to ask" (Chapters 19 and 23).

**Rules to honor.** No confession.

**Target length.** About 800 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 13.2**

**Continuity in.** About ninety minutes after 13.1. Eleven in the morning. Niall has watched the scene from the edge of the camp and has been turning something over since. Alexander is on the shingle alone, the mist lifting.

**Continuity out.** Trust is repaired to the ground level, not further. "Tell me before I have to ask" is now a standing term.

**Niall.** Comes on his own. Left forearm still bandaged from 10.3. Thumb on the palm scar. The burr thickens as he goes.

* He tells the whole of Calum: the collapsed close stair in Edinburgh, the boy who dragged him out; the fortnight after he left Edinburgh in 1845 when Calum vanished; the Grassmarket in winter 1848, the night of his mother's burial, when Calum warned him off and could not say his maker's name; two winters of searching; the shame of having been the one who left.  
* Admits the fear: that Alexander would look at Calum and see a monster, and more, what Alexander would think of a man who had loved someone who became one.  
* The register is care given as plain speech. He uses "Alex" once, at the honest moment. He does not perform.

**Alexander.** Wants honesty. Hears the word "loved" and lets it pass. Then: "Next time, tell me before I have to ask." Niall: "Aye." He is not forgiving, but he has stopped tapping and asks nothing more.

**Location.** The shingle at the loch edge, a little way from the camp. Mist lifting, low pale light, the water still.

**Rules in play.**

* No confession. The romance moves one visible degree and no more.  
* Niall still withholds (Book Two): the money to the MacDuffs and Portobello, Isla, the coded notebook entry, the rib scar (18.2), and his feelings. He does not raise them. Alexander does not ask about them. Niall must not lie if asked.  
* The promise he broke (5.6: "I'll not decide for you again what you're fit to carry") is answered here by doing the thing unasked.

**Threads touched.** The rupture (10.4). "Tell me before I have to ask" (19.x, 23.x). Niall's arc from protective secret-keeper to honest equal. Calum and Catherine (10.5), which Niall does not know about.

**Flags.** "Loved" is left ambiguous in the outline. **DECIDED:** the reader takes it as the brotherly love the bible gives (near-brother, Old Town close). Alexander hears the other reading without examining it. Neither man resolves it.

#### **Scene 3\. The standing stones**

**Scene summary.** At Amelia's request Alexander is present, because she has decided there will be no more rooms her son is kept out of. Seraphina breaks her oath at the two standing stones where Rhoswen's crossing to the camp begins. She tells Amelia that she is not the late lord's daughter. Her true father was a man of magic in the circle of Seraphina's coven, of an old line out of the east that was hunted for centuries, and he died in the massacre of 1815\. Before he died he bound Seraphina by oath to keep his daughter's parentage secret and his line hidden, and that oath is why Seraphina went looking for a girl at the Edinburgh lectures. Then she tells Amelia the worst of it. At the festival, in Hessian's distortion, she felt an echo of that same old line. She could not account for it, and she stayed silent, and her incomplete reading of the man let the sale go through. As she speaks, the oath takes its price. The silver in her altered eye goes out like a lamp, and the eye goes dark and does not come back. Amelia's voice shakes, not with tears but with something harder. "You'll tell me everything. Every detail. Not because I've forgiven you. I haven't, and I don't know that I will." None of them knows what the echo means, and Seraphina does not know that the line runs through Hessian himself. The scene delivers the cost of magic on the page, and makes the family's secrecy their own inheritance.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander, witnessing. He wants no more hidden rooms.

**Obstacle.** Seraphina's oath.

**Turning point.** Her eye goes dark.

**Consequence.** Amelia doesn't forgive her.

**Location.** The two standing stones on the northern shore of Loch Maree, east of the cave entrance in the cliff face. This is the ground where the Rowan Circle was massacred in 1815\. Seraphina has not stood on this shore since that night.

**Characters.** Alexander, Amelia, Seraphina.

**Weather and light.** Pale noon sun, low in the south.

**Mood.** Confession.

**Date and time of day.** Saturday 18 December 1852, about noon.

**Time since previous scene.** About an hour.

**Scene duration.** About forty minutes.

**Carried forward.** Seraphina's failure from 1.4 and 2.2.

**Setups and plants.** Chapter 20 (Seraphina as mentor).

**Rules to honor.** The oath-mark takes its price as she speaks. The massacre of 1815 happened on this shore — the same ground where the standing stones mark Rhoswen's crossing. Seraphina has not returned to this northern shore in thirty-seven years; she does not name that, and no one asks. Seraphina doesn't know the line runs through Hessian.

**Target length.** About 1,000 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 13.3**

**Continuity in.** About an hour after 13.2. Noon. At Amelia's request Alexander walks out with her and Seraphina. She has decided there will be no more rooms her son is kept out of. Amelia is still marked by the morning: bruised eyes, a cloth for the nose.

**Continuity out.** Seraphina has paid. Her eye is dark and stays dark. Amelia does not forgive her. The echo of the old line is planted but unexplained.

**Seraphina (returning).**

* Aged in the way of one born in 1731\. Upright, spare, dark eyes, the amulet at her throat, one altered eye that silvers when she is afraid.  
* Guilt: she rations the truth, then over-confesses. Tired, she falls into archaic speech. Available surprise line: "Well. That's not nothing."  
* She has not stood on this shore since the night in 1815\. She does not name that and no one asks. She was on the eastern shore drawing water when the hunters came.  
* Tells Amelia three things in order: Amelia is not the late lord's daughter; her true father was a man of the Circle, an old line out of the east that was hunted for centuries, who died on this ground in 1815 after binding Seraphina by oath; and that at the festival she felt an echo of that same old line in Hessian's distortion, kept silent, and let her own incomplete reading of him stand.  
* As she speaks the oath takes its price: the silver in the altered eye goes out like a lamp and does not come back.

**Amelia.** Angry, terrifying, quiet: the eyes go black and the voice drops. Her voice shakes with something harder than tears. Line, close to the outline: "You'll tell me everything. Every detail. Not because I've forgiven you. I haven't, and I don't know that I will." She blames herself too (she never read Hessian), and does not say so.

**Alexander.** Witness. He wants no more hidden rooms and he asks nothing. He is learning that his mother's blood is old and hunted.

**Location.** The two standing stones on the northern shore of Loch Maree, east of the cave entrance in the cliff face, on the flat ground where the Rowan Circle died. Pale noon sun low in the south, sunset about 3:13.

**Rules in play.**

* The oath: Anton Veress bound her on his own blood, at his dying hand: "Keep her name from them, and keep them from her" (Main Bible). Breaking it costs the sight of the eye permanently. After this her fear shows only in the hand at the amulet (Main Bible).  
* Seraphina does not know the line runs through Hessian himself (Book Four).  
* Amelia dies never knowing whose blood it is.  
* Nobody here knows what the echo means.

**Threads touched.** Seraphina as mentor (20.x). The family's secrecy as inheritance. The festival failure (1.4, 2.2). The covenant's older law.

**Flags.**

* The outline says Seraphina tells Amelia "she is not the late lord's daughter". **DECIDED:** "she" is Amelia (the girl at the Edinburgh lectures). Lord Marchbank is the "late lord" (Supporting Bible).  
* The bibles name Anton Veress but the outline does not say whether Seraphina names him on the page. **DECIDED:** she names him, as the Circle's scholar. She does not name Ilona or Elisabeta, which she does not know.  
* The bibles do not say what Seraphina knows of Lady Isobel Marchbank (whether she knew, how they met). **DECIDED:** Seraphina does not know. Amelia asks; Seraphina says she does not. Gap, for Fox to decide before Book Two.  
* The outline calls these "the two standing stones where Rhoswen's crossing to the camp begins". Earlier packets (11.3) put the camp crossing at the eastern stones. **DECIDED:** these are the northern-shore pair, where the channel to the refuge begins (14.x). Rhoswen's water touches both places. Drop "to the camp".  
* The camp's exact position relative to these stones is not in the outline. **DECIDED:** they are a walk from camp along the shore, no more.

#### **Scene 4\. Twenty-five**

**Scene summary.** Edward walks with his son along the loch shore and tells him why the age was twenty-five. His own father initiated him at fifteen, without warning, on a hunt that went wrong. He does not say how his brother died, and he never will (Proposed, minor, now approved). He says only that it cost him something no one should have to pay at fifteen, and that he set twenty-five so his son would never have to pay it, and that he was wrong. He does not mean wrong about the danger. He means wrong about whose choice it was. Then he does something he has never done. He asks Alexander what he thinks of the plan, as one planner to another. "That's the question I ought to have asked three days ago." The scene gives Edward his late positive turn before his death: he recognizes Alexander as an adult and admits what secrecy cost.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants his father to see him.

**Obstacle.** Edward's reserve.

**Turning point.** "I ought to have asked three days ago."

**Consequence.** He is recognized.

**Location.** The loch shore.

**Characters.** Alexander, Edward.

**Weather and light.** Low winter sun and long shadows. Sunset is about 3:13.

**Mood.** Reconciliation.

**Date and time of day.** Saturday 18 December 1852, about two in the afternoon.

**Time since previous scene.** About two hours.

**Scene duration.** About forty minutes.

**Carried forward.** The question "Would he?" from 9.6.

**Setups and plants.** Edward's death (Chapter 17).

**Rules to honor.** Edward never tells how his brother died.

**Target length.** About 800 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 13.4**

**Continuity in.** About two hours after 13.3. Two in the afternoon. Edward has been alone with the plan and with the last scene's news. Sunset about 3:13, so the light is already long.

**Continuity out.** Alexander is recognized as an adult and as a planner. The way is open for him to be named to the party in 13.5.

**Edward.**

* Greying dark hair silvered at the temples, scarred hands, the discipline of a man who does not hurry. Grave and economical.  
* Walks with his son along the shore and does not open with the point. Both hands on the shoulders when words will not serve.  
* Says: his own father initiated him at fifteen, without warning, on a hunt that went wrong. It cost him something no one should pay at fifteen. He set twenty-five so his son would never pay it, and he was wrong. Not about the danger. About whose choice it was.  
* Then asks Alexander what he thinks of the plan, as one planner to another. Line, adjusted (see flags): "That's the question I ought to have asked years ago."  
* Optional, drawn from the Bible: he answers "Would he?" (9.6) through 1838, when a creature freed from an Ashcroft transport cost him Alasdair Fraser. Keep it to a sentence.

**Alexander.** Wants his father to see him. Answers as he does in 9.3: what can each person do. He builds on his 12.3 point that the signal must belong to the party below. Reads twice before he trusts his voice, and does here. Does not show that being asked moves him.

**Location.** The loch shore, a low path above the shingle. Low winter sun, long shadows, cold.

**Rules in play.**

* Edward never says how his brother died, in this scene or ever. He may say "my brother" and may name Richard. Alexander already knows an uncle Richard died in 1808 (Main Bible).  
* The sixteenth Duke (Edward's father) initiated both sons on one hunt in 1808\. Richard died. Edward, the spare, became heir.  
* Edward's late positive turn comes before his death (17.x). Do not overcook it.

**Threads touched.** Edward's death (17.x). Protection against agency, the series theme (11.5). "Would he?" (9.6). The watch and the coat (14.1, 18.x).

**Flags.** The outline has Edward say the question should have been asked "three days ago." No anchor in the timeline fits (Alexander reached camp yesterday; Edward left Norfolk well before). **DECIDED:** "years ago." Logged as an outline correction.

#### **Scene 5\. Not waiting**

**Scene summary.** In council, the question is whether to wait. Beatrice's full strength is still days away, and so is any word from the Crown. Everyone at the table knows Beatrice's history: once, waiting for authorization cost a selkie mother, her two children, and Beatrice's own husband. She touches the brooch. Then, against every habit of her career, she chooses not to wait. Edward lays out the plan. Beatrice, Sebastian, and Ellery's operatives will make a loud and visible approach by the western road, to draw every eye Hessian has. Percival's engine will suppress the wards above for a window whose signal belongs to the party below. The family party, meaning Edward, Amelia, Seraphina, Alexander, Niall, and Gideon, will go through Rhoswen's channel, one way, into the refuge, and reach Catherine before the ritual. Edward names his son to the party without a pause, and Sebastian notices. This is the Midpoint turn, from reaction to action, and it is the plan whose failure will open Part Four.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to go in.

**Obstacle.** Waiting for authorization.

**Turning point.** Beatrice chooses not to wait.

**Consequence.** The plan, and the Midpoint shift.

**Location.** The map tent.

**Characters.** The whole council.

**Weather and light.** Dark, with wind rising.

**Mood.** Resolve.

**Date and time of day.** Saturday 18 December 1852, about six in the evening.

**Time since previous scene.** About four hours.

**Scene duration.** About an hour.

**Carried forward.** Calum's deadline from 12.6.

**Setups and plants.** Chapters 14 to 17\.

**Target length.** About 700 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 13.5**

**Continuity in.** About four hours after 13.4. Six in the evening, full dark since about 3:13. The council sits in the map tent. Percival's schematics are on the table; Percival himself is outside the perimeter (12.3). Calum's warning that Hessian must finish soon (12.6) sits over everything. Nobody in the tent knows the date of the ritual.

**Continuity out.** The plan is made. The Midpoint shift from reaction to action is complete. Alexander is named to the party.

**Beatrice.** Wool, brooch, iron-grey coil. She touches the brooch. Then, against every habit of her career, she decides not to wait. Not yet Acting Commander. She is a senior officer with emergency authority.

**Edward.** Lays out the plan without ornament. Names his son to the party without a pause.

**Sebastian.** Notices, and it costs him. His ledger stays closed. He does not object aloud. Do not let it read as a plot.

**Ellery.** Economical. Speaks in distances and hours. Her operatives will make the loud approach.

**Amelia.** Bruised eyes still, refuses the chair. Nothing said about Catherine that changes 13.1.

**Niall, Gideon, Seraphina.** Niall stays several steps ahead, arms folded. Gideon dry about the risks. Seraphina says little, her dark eye turned away.

**Alexander.** Wants to go in. Reads the room twice.

**The plan.** Beatrice, Sebastian and Ellery's operatives make a loud, visible approach by the western road to draw every eye Hessian has. Percival's engine suppresses the wards above for a measured window, and the signal belongs to the party below (12.3). The family party (Edward, Amelia, Seraphina, Alexander, Niall, Gideon) goes through Rhoswen's channel into the refuge and reaches Catherine before the ritual. Ewan stays with the horses and boats (14.1).

**Location.** The map tent. Dark, wind rising, canvas snapping.

**Rules in play.**

* Beatrice's full strength and any word from the Crown are days away (the gales that delayed her party, 12.1). Waiting has a price the reader knows and Alexander does not.  
* The party enters by Rhoswen's water and does not plan on leaving that way. Her terms are only confirmed at 14.2.  
* The engine's window is measured in minutes. Percival's cooperation is partial and will be tested (14.x, 17.x).

**Threads touched.** Chapters 14 to 17\. The override and early shutdown. The council fracture. Beatrice's grief and the brooch.

**Flags.**

* The outline says "everyone at the table knows Beatrice's history". Alexander does not learn the story until 19.4, when she tells him. **DECIDED:** here nobody says it. Alexander sees the brooch, sees Sebastian look away, and knows only that something happened. Keep the selkie mother, the children and Laurence out of dialogue.  
* The outline has Edward say the passage is "one way". **DECIDED:** Edward speaks of it as intention, in the family's own reading of Rhoswen's water, and 14.2 gives the fact. Nobody states it as certain here.

#### **Scene 6\. The notebook**

**Scene summary.** On the night before the passage, Alexander and Niall check each other's kit at the loch's edge. The metal will come off again at the stones in the morning, and they check it anyway, out of habit, because it gives their hands something to do. Niall is writing by lantern light in his father's field notebook, in Gaelic. Alexander asks what he writes. "Things I'll not say out loud." Alexander does not ask which things. Out on the water a ripple moves against the wind toward them and dies at the shingle, which is Rhoswen's way of saying she has heard. The scene plants Niall's Gaelic notebook, and holds the romance at the edge of speech without crossing it. It is the last quiet before the refuge.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to be near Niall.

**Obstacle.** The unsaid.

**Turning point.** "Things I'll not say out loud."

**Consequence.** Quiet.

**Location.** The loch edge.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall, and Rhoswen's water.

**Weather and light.** A clear night with a waxing gibbous moon.

**Mood.** Tender.

**Date and time of day.** Saturday 18 December 1852, about eleven at night.

**Time since previous scene.** About five hours.

**Scene duration.** About twenty minutes.

**Carried forward.** None.

**Setups and plants.** The notebook (Chapters 24 and 26).

**Target length.** About 600 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 13.6**

**Continuity in.** About five hours after 13.5. Eleven at night, Saturday 18 December. The plan is set. The party will go in tomorrow. Ewan delivered the steel to camp on the 17th (11.3), so the kit is here to be checked.

**Continuity out.** The last quiet before the refuge. The notebook is planted. The romance is held at the edge of speech.

**Niall.** Sits with his father's field notebook open on his knee by a shuttered lantern. He writes in Gaelic in the margins, in his own hand. Does not close it fast and does not hide it.

* Answer to Alexander: "Things I'll not say out loud." Nothing added.  
* The coded entry (Book Two) is a separate matter and is not in play.

**Alexander.** Wants to be near him. Checks the kit for something to do with his hands: buckles, the practice knife's case, the folded birch bark and the swallow handkerchief. He sees the Gaelic and does not lean to read it, and does not ask which things. Fingertips still.

**Rhoswen's water.** A ripple crosses against the wind, from the deep toward the shingle, and dies at their feet. She has heard. No voice, no form.

**Location.** The loch edge by the camp. Hard frost, a light wind off the water. Clear night, a half moon low in the west-southwest and close to setting.

**Rules in play.**

* No confession. "Alex" only if it comes as the honest thing.  
* They expect the 11.3 terms to hold (metal off at the stones tomorrow) and check the kit anyway. 14.2 changes her condition to "nothing hidden" and the metal goes in openly. Neither man knows that yet.  
* The breast-pocket touch is not used here. It first belongs to him at 20.5.  
* Amelia dies on 19 December. Do not hint.

**Threads touched.** The notebook (24.x, 26.x, and the cipher in Book Two). Rhoswen's terms. Niall's arc from secret-keeper to honest equal.

**Flags.**

* The outline says "a waxing gibbous moon." On 18 December the moon is at first quarter, a half. **DECIDED:** a half moon, low in the west-southwest and close to setting (it sets about quarter past midnight on the 19th, 17.2, so a little before that on the 18th). (14.1 also gives "65 percent, in the southeast"; check it there.)  
* The outline says the metal comes off "in the morning". The passage is mid-afternoon on the 19th. **DECIDED:** "tomorrow, before the light goes."  
* Whether Alexander reads Gaelic is not in the bibles. **DECIDED:** he does not. He sees the script and asks nothing.
