## **PART FOUR: THE REFUGE**

---

### **Chapter 14: The One-Way Passage**

*Loch Maree, Winter 1852*

**CHAPTER OVERVIEW**

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander, with a Catherine interlude. He wants to get into the refuge and reach Catherine before the ritual.

**Obstacle.** Rhoswen's terms, Percival's bad faith, and a passage that only opens once.

**Turning point.** Rhoswen catches the override, and the way closes behind them.

**Consequence.** He ends the chapter inside the refuge with no retreat, feeling the covenant answer him and telling no one. The clock is set. That sets up the exploration in Chapter 15\.

**Span and length.** Sunday 19 December 1852, dusk to early evening, plus the interlude three nights earlier. Target about 4,600 words.

#### **Scene 1\. Dusk**

**Scene summary.** The party gathers at the loch's western shore as the short December light fails. Edward, Amelia, Seraphina, Alexander, Niall, and Gideon will go below. Beatrice, Sebastian, and Ellery's operatives are already marching for the western road, where they will make the loud approach, and Ewan stays with the horses and the boats. At the water's edge Edward takes off his own heavy coat, reinforced at the shoulders and forearms, and puts it on his son without a word, because the channel is cold and Alexander's coat is thinner. Alexander protests and Edward overrules him with a look. It is the most ordinary thing his father does all winter, and the last ordinary thing. Edward asks, quietly, whether Alexander is afraid, and Alexander answers truthfully that he is. "Good," Edward says. "Watching you stand here asking me the harder question instead of the easier one. That's evidence enough." The scene plants the coat that holds the watch and the rifle ball, and it gives father and son one clean exchange before the refuge.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to go in as an equal, not be shielded.

**Obstacle.** His father's protectiveness, in the form of the coat.

**Turning point.** "Good. Watching you stand here asking me the harder question..."

**Consequence.** One clean exchange between father and son. The coat is on him.

**Location.** Loch Maree's western shore, by the standing stones at the channel mouth.

**Characters.** Edward, Amelia, Seraphina, Alexander, Niall, Gideon, Ewan (staying). Beatrice's party has already marched.

**Weather and light.** The last light fails at about 3:13. A waxing gibbous moon (65 percent) stands in the southeast, the frost is hard, and the water is still.

**Mood.** Solemn tenderness.

**Date and time of day.** Sunday 19 December 1852, about quarter to three to three in the afternoon.

**Time since previous scene.** About sixteen hours.

**Scene duration.** About fifteen minutes.

**Carried forward.** The plan from 13.5, and Ewan's steel returned by boat.

**Setups and plants.** The coat, the watch, and the rifle ball (Chapter 18).

**Rules to honor.** Edward's dry humor. The reinforced shoulders and forearms of his coat.

**Target length.** About 700 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 14.1**

**Continuity in.** About sixteen hours after 13.6. Sunday 19 December, a quarter to three. Beatrice, Sebastian and Ellery's operatives marched for the western road at first light of the afternoon. Ewan stays with the horses and boats. The steel Ewan rowed round (11.3) is with the party. Amelia's morning of the 18th has left her bruised under the eyes; she is steady.

**Continuity out.** One clean exchange between father and son. The coat is on Alexander, with the rifle ball in it. Sets up the Rhoswen scene.

**Edward.** Grave, dry. Takes off his heavy coat (reinforced at shoulders and forearms) and puts it on his son without a word. Overrules the protest with a look. Then, quietly, asks whether he is afraid. His line, close to the outline: "Good. Watching you stand here asking me the harder question instead of the easier one. That's evidence enough." This is the most ordinary thing he does all winter, and the last ordinary thing.

**Alexander.** Wants to go in as an equal. Protests once. Answers the fear question truthfully: yes. Fingertips tap, then still. Reads the moment twice.

**Amelia, Seraphina.** Amelia is clinical and quiet; her hands are gloveless. Seraphina stands apart, the dark eye turned toward the water, a hand at the amulet.

**Niall and Gideon.** Niall checks Alexander's buckles again by habit. Gideon is dry and brief, carrying his stamped iron token (11.3).

**Ewan.** Staying. Steady, tired, 24\. He carries the steel back to the boats after the metal question is settled (14.2).

**Location.** The stones at the channel mouth, on the northern shore near the loch's western end (the pair of 13.3). Sunset about 3:13. Hard frost, still water. Moon a little past half in the south-southeast.

**Rules in play.**

* The coat: heavy, reinforced. It carries the flattened rifle ball from 1838 (Edward has kept it for life, Main Bible). Alexander will find it in 18.x.  
* The coat carries Edward's pocket watch and the flattened rifle ball. Edward does not mention either as he hands it over (see flags).  
* Amelia has no gloves now (her burned hands healed; the silver-grey lines remain). The gloves of 5.1 were 1845\.

**Threads touched.** The coat, the ball and the watch (18.x, 26.x). "Would he?" (9.6, 13.4). Amelia and Edward's last hours.

**Flags.**

* The outline puts the stones on "the western shore"; 13.3 puts them on the northern shore. **DECIDED:** one pair, on the northern shore near its western end (the Location Bible's siting).  
* The outline gives the moon as "waxing gibbous (65 percent) in the southeast". At first quarter on the 19th it is a little past half (about 55 percent), high in the south-southeast at 3 p.m. **DECIDED:** use "a little past half."  
* The outline says the coat "holds the watch and the rifle ball", yet in 14.6 Edward checks his watch after the coat is on Alexander, and 18.x has the watch stopped in the coat pocket. **DECIDED:** the coat holds both, as the outline says. At 14.6 Edward reaches into the coat on his son's chest, takes out the watch, checks and winds it, and puts it back, so the watch stays in the coat and Alexander sees it. The ball he does not see until 18.4. The watch stops in the flood (17.4).

#### **Scene 2\. Nothing hidden**

**Scene summary.** Rhoswen rises between the standing stones at the mouth of a drowned channel that runs under the hill toward the sea. She will hold the water back so they can walk the channel with their kit. Her terms for this passage are not the terms of the Chapter 11 crossing. This time the condition is that nothing goes into her water hidden. Percival has come down to the shore to confirm the signal arrangements, and as he steps near the water she strikes at his boot with a speed that sends him sprawling. "Your other pocket, Lord Percival," Niall says, in a voice flat with contempt held carefully in check. Percival draws out a second device, iron-cased and no bigger than a pocket watch, with coiled wire at one edge. It is an override that would let him seize back control of the ward window from above, the exact control Alexander took from him in Chapter 12\. "A precaution," Percival says, and his charm cracks at the edges. Edward's disappointment is colder than anger. Amelia tells him Rhoswen has now shown him twice what her patience costs, and he should not test a third offense. Alexander, who objected to this alliance, says nothing, which says a good deal. The scene pays off Percival's promised truce in part and marks Percival as untrustworthy before the collapse.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants the passage opened.

**Obstacle.** Percival's hidden device.

**Turning point.** Rhoswen strikes at Percival, and the override is exposed.

**Consequence.** Percival is marked untrustworthy. Edward is coldly disappointed.

**Location.** The same shore.

**Characters.** As in 14.1, plus Percival and Rhoswen.

**Weather and light.** Dusk is deepening and the moon is brightening.

**Mood.** Tense and exposing.

**Date and time of day.** Sunday 19 December 1852, about quarter past three.

**Time since previous scene.** About fifteen minutes.

**Scene duration.** About fifteen minutes.

**Carried forward.** The timing clause from 12.3.

**Setups and plants.** The early shutdown (Chapter 17).

**Rules to honor.** Rhoswen won't let anything hidden into her water.

**Target length.** About 800 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 14.2**

**Continuity in.** About fifteen minutes after 14.1. Quarter past three, dusk deepening. Percival has walked down from the perimeter to confirm the signal arrangements. Rhoswen has been waiting in the water since the party arrived.

**Continuity out.** Percival is marked untrustworthy before the collapse. Rhoswen's terms are known. Edward is coldly disappointed. Sets up the channel.

**Rhoswen (returning).**

* Rises between the stones in her half-shifted, human-leaning form: silver eyes, fine scales at the temples and backs of the hands, silver burn scars across the palms, reed-tangled dark hair. Beautiful as a Highland storm.  
* No speech. Her terms travel through water and gesture. She will hold the channel back so they can walk it with their kit. The condition, and the way she shows it, is that nothing goes into her water hidden.  
* She strikes at Percival's boot with a speed that sends him sprawling. It is the old wound (the Ashcroft chain, 1845\) answering iron.

**Percival (returning).**

* Immaculate, silver-headed cane, collar a fraction too high over the scar. Two fingers to the scar when composure slips.  
* He says "Sterling" in place of Alexander's title, as before. He checks his watch (a tic) as he steps near the water and a hand drifts to the left coat pocket.  
* Draws out a second device: iron-cased, no bigger than a pocket watch, coiled wire at one edge. An override that would let him seize back control of the ward window from above (the control Alexander took from him at 12.3). His word: "A precaution." The charm cracks.

**Niall.** Reads the drift of the hand to the pocket and the wrong weight of the coat. Flat contempt, held in check.

**Edward.** Disappointment colder than anger, as at 7.x and 11.5. He takes the device from Percival himself, and does not raise his voice.

**Amelia.** Tells Percival that Rhoswen has now shown him twice what her patience costs (1845 and today) and that he should not test a third. Clinical, quiet.

**Alexander.** Objected to the alliance (12.3). Says nothing, which says a good deal.

**Ewan.** Takes the device on Edward's word, to be given to no one but Edward.

**Location.** The stones and the water's edge. Deepening dusk, the moon brightening. Cold.

**Rules in play.**

* Rhoswen's terms so far: the eastern crossing (11.3) took no metal; this passage takes any kit that is declared, and nothing hidden. Do not let her speak. She has chosen, not been commanded.  
* Iron near kelpie water hurts her. Silver burned her (1845 and Beag, 9.3).  
* The plan still needs the engine. Percival keeps the primary engine. The signal to start it belongs to the party below (12.3), by flare (16.x).

**Threads touched.** The early shutdown (17.x). Percival's partial truce. "Sterling" without a title.

**Flags.**

* Niall says "Lord Percival". Silas is a baron (Series Bible), so Percival's courtesy style is "the Honourable Mr. Ashcroft"; nobody would say "Lord Percival". **DECIDED:** Niall says "Mr. Ashcroft" and keeps the contempt. (If Fox prefers the mock-honorific as deliberate insult, restore it and keep it a single use.)  
* The outline gives no fate for the override device. **DECIDED:** Edward takes it and Ewan holds it. The main engine is unaffected, and the flare still starts it (16.x, 17.x).  
* Amelia says "twice". **DECIDED:** count 1845 (the chain, where Percival was present in the prequel) and this scene.

#### **Scene 3\. The channel**

**Scene summary.** The water stands back on either side of them like glass walls. The light turns thin and green, filtered through a loch that should be over their heads, and the passage twists under the hill toward the refuge's lower halls. The cold is enormous. Alexander walks inside his father's coat. Nobody speaks, because Rhoswen's water seems to ask for silence, and because the walls of it are close enough to touch. Gideon counts steps under his breath, as a man counts cards. The scene is atmosphere and dread. It widens the hidden world, since this passage has not been walked in living memory.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to get through.

**Obstacle.** Cold, fear, and silence.

**Turning point.** None. This is atmosphere.

**Consequence.** The hidden world opens further.

**Location.** The drowned channel under the hill, between walls of held water.

**Characters.** The six of the family party.

**Weather and light.** Green, filtered moonlight through the water, and enormous cold.

**Mood.** Dreamlike dread.

**Date and time of day.** Sunday 19 December 1852, about half past three to half past four.

**Time since previous scene.** About ten minutes.

**Scene duration.** About an hour.

**Carried forward.** The coat.

**Setups and plants.** The geography.

**Target length.** About 600 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 14.3**

**Continuity in.** About ten minutes after 14.2. Half past three to half past four. Rhoswen holds the water back. Nobody has spoken since the shore.

**Continuity out.** They are through. The hidden world has widened, and nobody has walked this way in living memory.

**Alexander.** Wants to get through. Walks inside his father's coat, which is too broad in the shoulder. The cold is enormous. He is afraid and does not let it into his stride.

**Edward.** Walks behind his son, coatless in the cold, not complaining. His breath shows.

**Amelia.** Feels the water on both sides as one who works it. Her lips are set; her palm hovers near the wall and does not touch it. No nosebleed here (she saves it).

**Niall.** Ahead, checking the footing, silent.

**Gideon.** Counts steps under his breath as a man counts cards. His count is the only sound.

**Seraphina.** Last but one. The dark eye turned toward the wall of water.

**Location.** The drowned channel under the hill toward the sea. Walls of held water, close enough to touch, glass-still. Green, thin light: moonlight through the loch. Enormous cold.

**Rules in play.**

* Rhoswen's water seems to ask silence. No speech, no light beyond the moon's, no lamps (sees 14.4).  
* The channel is one-way. It will close behind them (14.4).  
* This passage has not been walked in living memory (outline), so no one can say where it ends.

**Threads touched.** The geography of the refuge (15.x). The flood that will come back up this way (17.x). Dread and atmosphere; no plot.

**Flags.** The moon is behind and to the south of them under the hill and cannot light a channel that runs beneath rock. **DECIDED:** the light is Rhoswen's water itself, faintly luminous, not literal moonlight. The moon is the source in the reader's mind.

#### **Scene 4\. The way closes**

**Scene summary.** They come up the last slope of wet stone into a vaulted threshold. Behind them the walls of water fold together without a sound, and the channel is simply gone. Amelia puts her palm to the rock and feels the covenant move. "The way we came is not the way we'll be leaving by. Whatever the crossing's asked of us, it's asked it once, and it means to hold us to the once." Edward receives it with the calm of a man who has walked into worse. Alexander feels, for the first time, something in the stone that answers to him, a pressure like a hand on his shoulder, and does not tell anyone. The scene establishes that there is no retreat, and it gives the first quiet sign that the covenant knows Sterling blood.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants a way back.

**Obstacle.** The covenant permits only one way.

**Turning point.** The water closes behind them.

**Consequence.** No retreat. He feels the stone answer him.

**Location.** The vaulted threshold of the refuge.

**Characters.** The six.

**Weather and light.** Underground. Wet stone and lamplight.

**Mood.** Committed.

**Date and time of day.** Sunday 19 December 1852, about quarter to five.

**Time since previous scene.** About fifteen minutes.

**Scene duration.** About ten minutes.

**Carried forward.** The Sterling blood at the crossing.

**Setups and plants.** The door answers to him (Chapter 22). The covenant's recognition.

**Target length.** About 500 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 14.4**

**Continuity in.** About fifteen minutes after 14.3. A quarter to five. They climb the last slope of wet stone.

**Continuity out.** No retreat. Alexander has felt the covenant answer him and has told no one.

**Amelia.** Puts her palm on the rock and feels the covenant move. Line, close to the outline: "The way we came is not the way we'll be leaving by. Whatever the crossing's asked of us, it's asked it once, and it means to hold us to the once."

**Edward.** Receives it with the calm of a man who has walked into worse. Nods once.

**Alexander.** Feels a pressure in the stone like a hand on his shoulder, that answers to him. Does not speak. He does not know the covenant answers only to Sterling guardianship and Sterling blood. Reads it as fear.

**Niall and Gideon.** Niall listens for the water behind them and turns to see it fold shut. Gideon says nothing.

**Seraphina.** Watches Alexander, not the wall. She saw his face.

**Location.** The vaulted threshold of the refuge. Wet stone, old lamplight (lamps lit by whom: leave open). The channel is gone behind them.

**Rules in play.**

* The covenant knows Sterling blood (12.2). The deepest seal answers to it.  
* The channel closes the instant the last of them is through. No going back the way they came.  
* Alexander's private knowledge is planted for 22.x (the door answers to him).

**Threads touched.** The door that answers to him (22.x). The covenant's recognition. The one-way passage (Rhoswen's terms).

**Flags.** Lamps in the vault are unexplained. **DECIDED:** old lamps burn low along the threshold, lit long ago and kept by the refuge's own working. Do not explain further.

#### **Scene 5\. The covenant chamber, her choosing**

**Scene summary.** This is an interlude from Catherine's point of view, three nights earlier. She has asked Hessian to move his ritual out of the manor's chapel and down into the covenant chamber proper, where the old claim was first made. She tells him it is because the old law should witness what she gives. The truth is that Calum has told her the covenant chamber sits nearest the drowned ways, and that the old law is strongest there, so a refusal made there will weigh the most. Hessian is pleased, and his pleasure frightens her, because she cannot tell whether she asked for the move out of her own clear thinking or because he wanted her to. She has been cutting her thumb for a month to leave swallows on stone. Before they take her down she leaves one last thing behind in her room, for whoever comes: the atlas, open, with a route inked across it in blood. "So that whoever finds that room first finds proof I was there, and proof I was thinking, and not merely proof I was caught." The scene puts Catherine's agency in the book's engine and sets up the discovery in Chapter 15\.

**Perspective and goal.** Catherine (interlude). She wants her refusal to carry the greatest possible weight.

**Obstacle.** She can't be sure her own thoughts are her own.

**Turning point.** She leaves the atlas behind.

**Consequence.** Her agency is set in motion.

**Location.** The refuge: Hessian's rooms, then her own room.

**Characters.** Catherine, Hessian, and Calum (mentioned).

**Weather and light.** Underground and night.

**Mood.** Fear and resolve.

**Date and time of day.** Thursday 16 December 1852, about midnight (three nights earlier).

**Time since previous scene.** A flashback.

**Scene duration.** About thirty minutes.

**Carried forward.** Calum's intelligence from 10.5.

**Setups and plants.** The atlas route (Chapter 15). The covenant chamber (Chapters 16 and 23).

**Rules to honor.** Compulsion makes self-doubt possible.

**Target length.** About 800 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 14.5**

**Continuity in.** A flashback. Thursday 16 December, a little before midnight (into Friday 17), three nights before 14.1. Catherine has been a full vampire for about eight weeks. Calum has told her, through a door left a hand's width open, that the covenant chamber lies nearest the drowned ways.

**Continuity out.** Catherine's agency is in the book's engine. She leaves the atlas open with a route inked in her own blood, for whoever comes.

**Catherine (POV).**

* Auburn hair in natural waves, vivid green eyes now red-tinged at the rim when she is hungry, a small beauty mark near her left eye, grey pallor. Slender, dancer's balance, never still: in captivity she is still for the first time in her life, and hates it.  
* Voice bright and quick in her head; her outer voice is careful. Airy sentences, questions, wandering. Under pressure her thinking sharpens and catalogues.  
* She cannot tell whether her own thoughts are her own. She tests each thought as she has an idea she likes: Is this mine? Compulsion makes self-doubt possible.  
* Line: "So that whoever finds that room first finds proof I was there, and proof I was thinking, and not merely proof I was caught."  
* She has been leaving swallows on stone for a month, cutting her thumb. Post-bite blood: cold.

**Hessian.**

* Reads late thirties, pale, fine-boned, dark hair worn slightly long, eyes shifting between not-quite-brown and the black of old blood. Correct, expensive, faintly out of date black coat. Low, formal, unhurried voice. Fastidious titles. "Owed," "given," "yours."  
* Calls her Elisabeta. She has stopped correcting it aloud (correcting costs days, 6.x).  
* Pleased that she asked. His pleasure is the fear.

**Calum.** Mentioned only. He has told her the covenant chamber is nearest the drowned ways and that the old law is strongest there.

**Catherine addresses him** as "Count." She does not call him "Wilhelm" until 23.x.

**Location.** The refuge: Hessian's rooms, then her own room (fine linen, narrow bed, looking-glass turned to the wall, salt in the damp). Underground, night, lamplight.

**Rules in play.**

* The ritual was to be in the manor's chapel above. Catherine has asked to move it down to the covenant chamber proper, where the old claim was made. Her stated reason: the old law should witness what she gives. Her real one: a refusal made there weighs most.  
* Her compelled yes is only partial (12.2), and it is failing (12.6). She half-knows.  
* She leaves the atlas behind, open. A map of the refuge's lower ways that Calum drew is inside it, with her route inked over it in blood (15.1).

**Threads touched.** The atlas (15.1, 15.4). The covenant chamber (16.x, 23.x). Swallow marks. Catherine's arc from being asked to choosing.

**Flags.**

* "Three nights earlier" (Sunday 19\) is the night of Thursday 16 into Friday 17\. This makes 12.6 (Friday, 10 p.m.) about a day after her request. **DECIDED** and consistent with 12.6.  
* The outline has the route inked "across" the atlas in blood, while 15.1 has it inked "across a map ... that Calum must have drawn for her." **DECIDED:** Calum's map sits inside the atlas, and Catherine inks the route in blood over the map.  
* Where Catherine is held from Friday to Sunday is not in the outline. **DECIDED:** left open; do not specify. 16.x picks her up at the dais.

#### **Scene 6\. The bell**

**Scene summary.** Deep in the refuge, a bell begins to toll, faint and irregular. It is not an alarm, and it is not a summons. It sounds like something old waking up. Edward checks his watch, the ritual gesture of every evening of Alexander's life, and says they have perhaps six hours before the ward window is due, at eleven. The scene raises pressure and sets the clock for the first rescue.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to know the timing.

**Obstacle.** None. This is a clock.

**Turning point.** The bell.

**Consequence.** The clock is set: the window is at eleven.

**Location.** The refuge threshold passage.

**Characters.** The six.

**Weather and light.** Underground.

**Mood.** Ominous.

**Date and time of day.** Sunday 19 December 1852, about five in the afternoon.

**Time since previous scene.** About fifteen minutes.

**Scene duration.** About five minutes.

**Carried forward.** None.

**Setups and plants.** The eleven o'clock window. Beatrice will be at the gate at half past ten.

**Rules to honor.** Edward checks his watch, his habitual gesture.

**Target length.** About 300 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 14.6**

**Continuity in.** About fifteen minutes after 14.4. Five o'clock on Sunday 19 December. The party has not yet moved from the threshold.

**Continuity out.** The clock is set: the window is at eleven, six hours off. Edward has checked the watch, taken from the coat his son wears, and put it back.

**Edward.** Checks his watch (a check for the plan's timing; he winds it later, at nine). Tells them they have perhaps six hours before the ward window, at eleven. He does this by reaching into the pocket of the coat his son now wears, taking the watch out, and returning it. No word about it, or a single dry one. Grave, economical. "Someone should keep the hour" is available, but only if it fits. Sets both hands on his son's shoulders if words won't serve.

**Alexander.** Wants to know the timing. Reads the bell as something old waking. Notices where the watch lives now. Does not ask about it. Takes it as read that his father keeps time.

**Niall, Gideon, Amelia, Seraphina.** Listening. Gideon's hand is on his iron token. Amelia's palm is flat on the wall again.

**The bell.** Faint and irregular, from deep in the refuge. Not an alarm and not a summons. Something old waking up. Do not say who or what rings it.

**Location.** The refuge's threshold passage. Underground, damp, low lamplight.

**Rules in play.**

* The window opens on the party's signal (12.3, 13.5), and eleven is the agreed earliest time. Beatrice will be at the gate at half past ten.  
* The plan: the flare goes up at eleven from the party below, and the engine starts on it (16.x).  
* The watch: Edward winds it at nine in the evening (the habit in the Main Bible). At five he only checks it. Alexander winds it at the same hour in 26.6.

**Threads touched.** The eleven o'clock window (16.x). Beatrice at the gate at 10:30. The watch (18.x, 26.6).

**Flags.**

* The Main Bible says Edward winds the watch "at the same hour each evening" and never gives the hour. **DECIDED:** nine o'clock at night (26.6 falls about nine, and the outline puts the winding at the hour Edward always did). The five o'clock look at 14.6 is a check for the plan's timing, not the winding. Logged for the bible.  
* The outline has the bell tolling "deep in the refuge" without cause. **DECIDED:** unexplained here; 15.x may explain.

### **Chapter 15: The Ancient Refuge**

*The northern refuge, Scottish Highlands, Winter 1852*

**CHAPTER OVERVIEW**

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander, with a Seraphina interlude. He wants to reach Catherine.

**Obstacle.** The refuge's geometry, the thrall patrols, and what they find.

**Turning point.** The cages, and Catherine's face.

**Consequence.** He ends the chapter in the antechamber holding his changed sister's plan. He is devastated and focused. That sets up the ritual in Chapter 16\.

**Span and length.** Sunday 19 December 1852, about half past five to nine in the evening. Target about 4,700 words.

#### **Scene 1\. The unmirrored wing**

**Scene summary.** The refuge was built by hands not entirely human, in a geometry that almost repeats and does not. Twice the party turns down a passage that is a mirror of the last and ends in blank stone. It is Alexander who sees the tell. The false wings are not quite symmetrical, because one carving in each is missing its twin, and he catches the second false turning before it costs them more than a held breath. Edward looks at him and says nothing, and the nothing is approval. Thrall patrols cross so close overhead that Alexander feels their tread through his boot soles. The scene shows Alexander's strength as synthesis and pattern, not force, and it widens the hidden world.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to find the right way.

**Obstacle.** The false wings.

**Turning point.** He spots the missing twin carving.

**Consequence.** Edward's silent approval.

**Location.** The refuge corridors, including the unmirrored wing.

**Characters.** The six, with thrall patrols overhead.

**Weather and light.** Underground.

**Mood.** Tense.

**Date and time of day.** Sunday 19 December 1852, about half past five.

**Time since previous scene.** About thirty minutes.

**Scene duration.** About forty minutes.

**Carried forward.** The road-reading skill from 9.1.

**Setups and plants.** Synthesis as strength (Chapter 21).

**Target length.** About 700 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 15.1**

**Continuity in.** About thirty minutes after 14.6. Sunday 19 December, half past five. The party has left the threshold and is walking the refuge's corridors. Edward's watch is in the pocket of the coat Alexander wears (14.6), so Alexander now holds the hour.

**Continuity out.** Alexander's gift for pattern is shown, and Edward's silence is approval. The party is on the way to the cages.

**Alexander.** Wants the right way. Wears the coat, which drags at the shoulders. Reads the passages as he read the road (9.1): twice, then trusts himself. He catches the second false turning before it costs them more than a held breath. His method is synthesis and pattern, not force. Fingertips tap once, then stop.

**Edward.** Looks at his son and says nothing, and the nothing is approval. No word of praise, and none needed. He would have taken the first turning himself.

**Niall.** Checks the false wing's blank end with his hand, looks back to Alexander. Says nothing.

**Amelia, Seraphina, Gideon.** Amelia keeps her palm near the wall; her lips are tight. Seraphina carries a lamp and says nothing. Gideon counts under his breath and adds a wry aside at a dead end.

**Location.** The refuge corridors, including the unmirrored wing. Geometry that almost repeats and does not. Twice the party turns down a passage that mirrors the last and ends in blank stone. Low lamps, dust, cold.

**Rules in play.**

* The refuge was built by hands not entirely human. The false wings are almost symmetrical, and one carving in each is missing its twin.  
* Thrall patrols cross overhead, near enough that Alexander feels their tread through his boot soles. Thralls are heard only, not seen. Do not describe one yet.  
* Alexander does not yet know the covenant answers to him (14.4). Do not let it help him find the way.

**Threads touched.** Synthesis as strength (21.x, the ruined croft). The road-reading skill (9.1). The coat and the watch. Edward's approval, one of the last.

**Flags.** None beyond keeping the thralls unseen.

#### **Scene 2\. The cages**

**Scene summary.** Gideon finds them first, by the smell. Along a lower gallery stand iron cages holding people Hessian's thralls have taken from the glens over the past year: a drover, a shepherd's widow, a boy of perhaps twelve, all with bandaged arms. Amelia frees the boy with a working that leaves her hands shaking, and Gideon, who has a smuggler's gift for locks, starts on the rest. The boy watches them go with an expression Alexander cannot read at first, gratitude and fear and something wearier than either. At the far door he understands what it was. It was doubt, the look of a creature so long used to cages that it no longer trusts an open door. They cannot take the captives with them, because the way out is not yet known. Gideon stays with them, promising to bring them out by whatever road opens. The scene reveals what Catherine has been drinking, a truth the reader carries into her reunion with her family, and it gives Gideon a choice that is freely made.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to free the captives.

**Obstacle.** The way out isn't known.

**Turning point.** Gideon chooses to stay.

**Consequence.** What Catherine has been drinking is revealed. Gideon commits freely.

**Location.** The lower gallery with the cages.

**Characters.** The six, a drover, a shepherd's widow, and a boy of about twelve.

**Weather and light.** Underground. The smell of blood and old straw.

**Mood.** Horror and compassion.

**Date and time of day.** Sunday 19 December 1852, about half past six.

**Time since previous scene.** About twenty minutes.

**Scene duration.** About thirty minutes.

**Carried forward.** The cups from 7.3.

**Setups and plants.** The captives' escape (Chapter 17). Hessian's hunger (Chapter 22).

**Rules to honor.** The captives are human.

**Target length.** About 800 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 15.2**

**Continuity in.** About twenty minutes after 15.1. Half past six. Gideon smells blood and old straw ahead of the others. They come down to a lower gallery.

**Continuity out.** What Catherine has been drinking is on the page. Gideon has stayed by his own choice. The party is now five.

**The captives.** Human, all with bandaged arms (cut-and-cup feeding, Dossier). A drover, a shepherd's widow, a boy of about twelve. Iron cages. All taken from the glens over the past year. Unnamed. None speaks except, at most, a whisper.

**The boy.** Twelve, thin, bandage on the forearm. His look, when the cage door opens, is something Alexander cannot read: gratitude, fear, and something wearier than either. At the far door Alexander understands it. It is doubt, the look of a creature so long used to cages that it will not trust an open door. Echoes Davey Coop (7.1), twelve and chalk-fingered, for Gideon.

**Amelia.** Frees the boy with a working that leaves her hands shaking. Cost, shown once and not again this scene. Clinical, quiet, gentle with the child. She does not say what she is thinking about her own.

**Gideon.** Has a smuggler's gift for locks. Sets to the rest with his picks. Dry humor drops away. He does not leave the cages: he stays with the captives, promising to bring them out by whatever road opens. His choice is free, and Edward does not ask it of him. Do not make it a joke. He has paid debts he could walk away from (8.5).

**Edward.** Grave. Says the way out is not known and that they cannot take them. Does not order Gideon to stay.

**Alexander.** Wants to free them all. Realizes what the bandages mean and does not say it. He does not connect it aloud to Catherine.

**Niall.** Keeps watch on the gallery mouth. Quiet contempt at the cages.

**Location.** The lower gallery with the cages. Iron, old straw, the smell of blood. Low light.

**Rules in play.**

* The captives are human, and the Vanguard ethic applies to them without argument (2.3).  
* Cut-and-cup feeding: a cut, a cup, no fangs, blood spoils within hours. The bandaged arms explain the cups (7.3) and the cup Catherine drinks slowly with both hands.  
* The way out is not known. The channel closed behind them (14.4). The captives are the reason the "whatever road opens" is not yet in sight.  
* Hessian's hunger is the subject of 22.x. Do not name him here beyond "his thralls took them."

**Threads touched.** The captives' escape (17.x, the sea caves). The cups (7.3). Gideon's steady arc toward loyalty (25.x, where he refuses payment). Amelia's cost.

**Flags.** The outline lists the party as "the six" and then has Gideon stay. **DECIDED:** from 15.3 on the party is five (Edward, Amelia, Seraphina, Alexander, Niall).

#### **Scene 3\. The relief**

**Scene summary.** In a round hall older than anything above it, a carved relief covers one wall. A crowned human figure stands beside a figure that is not human, their hands on the same staff. Beneath them runs a line of seven linked sigils. Niall stops dead. The family of the carving is the same as the one he glimpsed in the Loch Maree cave tunnel seven years ago, the one that matched nothing he was trained to recognize. Seraphina, her dark eye useless in this light, reads the sigils by touch. "One of seven," she says. "There were seven of these, once." Edward commits the image to memory. Alexander does too, without meaning to. The scene uncovers two of the series' long mysteries at once, the nonhuman co-founder and the seven sigils, and it places the prize Percival's surveyor will copy in Chapter 17\.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to understand what he's seeing.

**Obstacle.** None. This is wonder.

**Turning point.** "One of seven."

**Consequence.** The series mysteries are planted.

**Location.** The round hall with the relief.

**Characters.** The five (Gideon is with the cages).

**Weather and light.** Underground. The stone is ancient.

**Mood.** Awe.

**Date and time of day.** Sunday 19 December 1852, about quarter past seven.

**Time since previous scene.** About fifteen minutes.

**Scene duration.** About fifteen minutes.

**Carried forward.** The carving Niall glimpsed in 1845\.

**Setups and plants.** Percival's rubbings (Chapter 17). The door (Chapter 22). The co-founder mystery.

**Rules to honor.** The relief shows a crowned human figure beside a nonhuman one. Seven linked sigils.

**Target length.** About 600 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 15.3**

**Continuity in.** About fifteen minutes after 15.2. A quarter past seven. They have left Gideon. The corridor opens into a round hall older than anything above it.

**Continuity out.** The nonhuman co-founder and the seven sigils are planted. Everyone present is holding an image they cannot yet interpret.

**The relief.** Covers one wall. A crowned human figure stands beside a figure that is not human, hands on the same staff. Beneath them runs a line of seven linked sigils. (Series Bible: the founder is Macbeth, the co-founder Eithne. Nobody knows that yet. Do not name either.)

**Niall.** Stops dead. The family of the carving is the same as the one he glimpsed in the Loch Maree cave tunnel seven years ago (1845), which matched nothing he was trained to recognize. He files it and says nothing until asked.

**Seraphina.** Reads the sigils by touch. Her good eye is useless in this light and the other, the oath-eye, is blind since 13.3. Line: "One of seven. There were seven of these, once."

**Edward.** Commits the image to memory. Grave, quiet, a scholar's attention. He studied dead and ancient languages.

**Alexander.** Commits it to memory too, without meaning to. Awe, not fear.

**Amelia.** Puts her palm out toward the stone and does not touch it (her hand is shaking). She feels the water in the walls.

**Location.** The round hall with the relief. Ancient stone, very old air, low lamps. Nothing else in the room.

**Rules in play.**

* The relief shows a crowned human figure beside a nonhuman one. Seven linked sigils. Percival's surveyor will make rubbings of one line of them (17.x).  
* Series Bible: the sigils show the British covenant is one of seven. The other six sites are unknown to the Vanguard. Seraphina knows only that there were seven and cannot name a single other.  
* This is the door hall for the second assault (22.x): the ancient door behind the relief does not answer to Seraphina or Gideon's picks.

**Threads touched.** The co-founder (Books Four and Seven). The seven sigils and the travel engine (Books Three, Six, Seven). Percival's rubbings (17.x, 24.x). The door (22.x).

**Flags.**

* The outline has Seraphina's "dark eye useless in this light." The Location Bible has her "good eye useless in the dark." After 13.3 the altered eye is the blind one. **DECIDED:** the good eye is the one failing in the dimness, and she reads the sigils by touch.  
* "One of seven" could mean seven reliefs or seven sites. **DECIDED:** it means seven covenants, as in the Series Bible, and the relief marks the British one.

#### **Scene 4\. Her room**

**Scene summary.** They find the room where Catherine has been kept, with its fine linen, a narrow bed, and a looking-glass turned to the wall. On the table lies her atlas, open, with a route inked across a map of the refuge's lower ways that Calum must have drawn for her. It leads to the covenant chamber by a servants' passage Hessian does not watch. Alexander stands in the doorway of the room where his sister has lived for a year and cannot move. Niall puts a hand on his back, brief and hard, the way he has steadied him a hundred times, and Alexander moves. Amelia touches the turned mirror and does not turn it round. The scene pays off the atlas: the book that carried Hessian's compulsion now carries Catherine's own intention. It also puts the family's grief on the page before the rescue.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to find Catherine's trail.

**Obstacle.** Grief freezes him.

**Turning point.** Niall's hand on his back.

**Consequence.** He moves.

**Location.** Catherine's room.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall, Amelia, Edward, Seraphina.

**Weather and light.** Underground.

**Mood.** Grief.

**Date and time of day.** Sunday 19 December 1852, about eight in the evening.

**Time since previous scene.** About thirty minutes.

**Scene duration.** About fifteen minutes.

**Carried forward.** The atlas from 14.5.

**Setups and plants.** The route (Chapter 17, scene 5).

**Rules to honor.** The mirror is turned to the wall.

**Target length.** About 700 words.

**Seraphina.** Silent at the door.

**The room.** Fine linen, a narrow bed, dark stone that holds the cold, salt under the damp, a table with the atlas open. On the atlas: Calum's map of the refuge's lower ways, and Catherine's route inked across it in blood. Dry and dark now. Nothing else of hers. **DECIDED:** Alexander takes the atlas with him, inside the coat (it comes out salt-stained at 26.x, and the route is followed at 17.5).

#### **Scene 5\. The passage**

**Scene summary.** This interlude is from Seraphina's point of view. She takes the rear as the party follows Catherine's route, and at a junction she feels the ceiling begin to fail. Hessian's wards are shifting the refuge's stone to seal the servants' way. She sends the others ahead, presses both hands flat to the cracked vault, and begins, alone and without thanks, the long work of holding back a collapse that she knows she does not have the strength left to hold for long. She thinks of Amelia, of the oath, of the eye she has already paid. She does not call for help. The scene makes Seraphina actively accountable, as her arc requires, and it splits the party at the worst possible moment.

**Perspective and goal.** Seraphina (interlude). She wants to hold the vault so the others can pass.

**Obstacle.** Her strength is spent.

**Turning point.** None. This is endurance.

**Consequence.** The party is split, and she is exhausted.

**Location.** A junction of the servants' way.

**Characters.** Seraphina.

**Weather and light.** Underground. Dust falling.

**Mood.** A solitary vigil.

**Date and time of day.** Sunday 19 December 1852, about half past eight.

**Time since previous scene.** About thirty minutes.

**Scene duration.** About ninety minutes, continuing.

**Carried forward.** The oath's cost from 13.3.

**Setups and plants.** Chapter 16 (she holds the collapsing passage) and Chapter 17, scene 5\.

**Rules to honor.** The cost of magic. Her accountability arc.

**Target length.** About 600 words.

* About 121 years old (born 1731). Spare, upright. One eye blind since 13.3; the other, her good eye, is failing in the dark. Amulet at her throat.

* She presses her lamp into Alexander's hand and sends the others ahead (**DECIDED:** this is the lamp he carries at 17.5), presses both hands flat to the cracked vault, and starts the long work of holding a collapse she knows she cannot hold long. She does not call for help, and does not thank anyone for the choice.

Check against 16.x.

#### **Scene 6\. The antechamber**

**Scene summary.** The route brings them to a small antechamber off the covenant chamber, and to Catherine. She is standing, waiting for them, because Calum told her they would come. Alexander sees her grey face and her sunken eyes, and the red that rises in them at the sound of so many heartbeats in a small room. She sees him see it. "Don't look at me as though I've already been buried," she says. Amelia holds her daughter with cold arms around her and does not let go. Catherine tells them, fast and practical, that the ritual is tonight and that Hessian will bring her to the dais himself. She tells them she will refuse him there, in the chamber, where the old law will hear it. She tells them they must wait for her refusal before they move. Then Amelia tells her, gently, about the cages, and Catherine understands where the cups came from. She does not weep. She goes very still in the inhuman way, and then her hands shake. The scene is the reunion, the full truth of what she is, and her own plan put forward as the plan.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants his sister back.

**Obstacle.** What she has become.

**Turning point.** "Don't look at me as though I've already been buried."

**Consequence.** Her plan becomes the plan. She learns about the cages.

**Location.** The antechamber off the covenant chamber.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall, Edward, Amelia, Catherine.

**Weather and light.** Underground.

**Mood.** Heartbreak.

**Date and time of day.** Sunday 19 December 1852, about nine in the evening.

**Time since previous scene.** About thirty minutes.

**Scene duration.** About twenty minutes.

**Carried forward.** The cages from 15.2.

**Setups and plants.** Waiting for her refusal (Chapter 16).

**Rules to honor.** Grey face, sunken eyes, red at hunger.

**Target length.** About 900 words.

* Grey face, sunken eyes, the red that rises in them at the sound of so many heartbeats in a small room. Auburn hair, green eyes reddening at the rim, the mark by her left eye. She still moves like a dancer.

### **Chapter 16: The First Rescue**

*The northern refuge, Winter 1852*

**CHAPTER OVERVIEW**

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to carry out Catherine's plan and get her out.

**Obstacle.** Hessian's trap, and Alexander's own fear.

**Turning point.** He attacks before the signal.

**Consequence.** He ends the chapter in a fight he started, with Niall down and his father taking command. This is catastrophe, and it sets up Chapter 17\.

**Span and length.** Sunday 19 December 1852, about half past nine to twenty to eleven at night. Target about 4,500 words.

#### **Scene 1\. Edward's plan, her plan**

**Scene summary.** Edward reshapes the rescue around what Catherine has told them. They will wait for the ward window, then take her out through the servants' way while her refusal breaks the ritual and Beatrice's noise on the western road draws the thralls above. Calum slips into the antechamber, his hand pressed to his chest. He says Hessian will bring her down within the hour, and that he will try to open the covenant chamber's lower ward from inside so they have a second way out. Niall and Calum exchange four words in Old Town Scots that no one else follows. Alexander counts the plan's parts and feels the whole weight of it resting on one thing, which is waiting. The scene sets up the plan's single point of failure, and that point is Alexander's temperament.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to wait.

**Obstacle.** His temperament.

**Turning point.** Calum arrives with the timing.

**Consequence.** The single point of failure is clear.

**Location.** The antechamber.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall, Edward, Amelia, Catherine, Calum.

**Weather and light.** Underground.

**Mood.** Coiled.

**Date and time of day.** Sunday 19 December 1852, about half past nine.

**Time since previous scene.** About ten minutes.

**Scene duration.** About fifteen minutes.

**Carried forward.** Catherine's plan.

**Setups and plants.** The attack (16.4).

**Target length.** About 600 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 16.1**

**Continuity in.** About ten minutes after 15.6. Sunday 19 December, half past nine. The four (Edward, Amelia, Alexander, Niall) are in the antechamber with Catherine. Seraphina is alone at the failing vault (15.5). Gideon is with the captives (15.2). Alexander carries Edward's watch in the coat pocket (14.6).

**Continuity out.** The plan's single point of failure is clear: waiting. Calum has given the timing. Catherine has gone back to where Hessian will find her.

**Edward.** Grave, economical. Reshapes the rescue around what Catherine has told them. They wait for the ward window, then take her out by the servants' way while her refusal breaks the ritual and Beatrice's noise on the western road (10:30 at the gate) draws the thralls above. The servants' way is held open by Seraphina for as long as she can. He says nothing of the cost to her.

**Amelia.** Diagnostic questions, fired rapidly (her fear register). Has hold of Catherine's hand until she leaves.

**Catherine.** Practical, quick, the laugh arriving too early for the joke, then gone. She has to go back before Hessian comes. She does not say goodbye, she says "I'll see you at the dais" or the like. A look at Alexander.

**Calum (returning).**

* Slight and wiry, dark brown curls, grey eyes that will not meet anyone's, hands opening and closing. Pale, one hand pressed flat to his chest where the Mark burns. Old Town Scots: "lass," "ken," "nae." Apologizes before anyone accuses him.  
* Slips in from the servants' passage. Says Hessian will bring her down within the hour. He will try to open the covenant chamber's lower ward from inside, so they have a second way out.  
* With Catherine, "lass," and no touch beyond hands.

**Niall and Calum.** Four words in Old Town Scots that no one else follows. Suggested: Calum, "Ye came." Niall, "Aye. Course." Do not translate for Alexander. Do not resolve the rest.

**Alexander.** Wants to wait. Counts the plan's parts (the window, her refusal, Beatrice's noise, the way out) and feels the whole weight rest on one thing, which is waiting. He does not know his own temperament is the flaw.

**Location.** The antechamber off the covenant chamber. Low lamplight.

**Rules in play.**

* The window opens on the party's flare (12.3, 14.6), and the engine's window is measured in minutes.  
* The Mark: Hessian's command runs through Calum. He gives what it allows.  
* Hessian steered Catherine's request to move the ritual down (14.5). He knows the chamber suits him.

**Threads touched.** The attack (16.4). Calum's resistance. Catherine's plan, and Alexander's flaw (protection overriding agency).

**Flags.**

* The outline puts Catherine in the scene and has Hessian arrive with her on his arm at 16.3. **DECIDED:** she leaves at the end of 16.1 and Hessian collects her from the far side of the chamber.  
* The four-word exchange is not given. **DECIDED:** the suggested pair above. Change freely.

#### **Scene 2\. He's felt me reaching**

**Scene summary.** Calum lays his palm against the ward-stone in the chamber wall and reaches into it. It hurts him visibly, and the Mark flares like a coal under his shirt. The ward gives an inch and then snaps back. "He's felt it," Calum says, pulling his hand free. "He's felt me reaching, and he knows now precisely which corridor I reached from." He staggers back with his hand pressed to his heart. There is no longer any second way out, and Hessian knows someone is inside. The scene raises the pressure and gives Calum's resistance a price.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants a second exit.

**Obstacle.** Hessian feels Calum's reach.

**Turning point.** "He's felt me reaching."

**Consequence.** No second way out, and discovery.

**Location.** The antechamber's ward-stone.

**Characters.** Calum, Alexander, Niall.

**Weather and light.** Underground.

**Mood.** Alarm.

**Date and time of day.** Sunday 19 December 1852, about ten to ten.

**Time since previous scene.** About five minutes.

**Scene duration.** About five minutes.

**Carried forward.** Calum's resistance from 12.6.

**Setups and plants.** Hessian moves early (16.3).

**Rules to honor.** The Mark flares.

**Target length.** About 500 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 16.2**

**Continuity in.** About five minutes after 16.1. Ten to ten. Calum has gone to the ward-stone in the antechamber wall to try the covenant chamber's lower ward.

**Continuity out.** No second way out. Hessian now knows exactly where inside the refuge Calum reached from.

**Calum.** Palm to the ward-stone. It hurts him visibly. The Mark flares like a coal under his shirt. The ward gives an inch and snaps back. Line, close to the outline: "He's felt it. He's felt me reaching, and he knows now precisely which corridor I reached from." He staggers back, a hand pressed to his heart.

**Alexander.** Wants a second exit. Watches Calum pay for it. Says nothing helpful because there is nothing.

**Niall.** Steadies Calum by the arm. Quiet. He counts the cost.

**Location.** The antechamber's ward-stone. Low light, the stone faintly warm under Calum's hand.

**Rules in play.**

* The Mark flares when Calum resists and when Hessian pulls.  
* The ward is tied to the covenant and to Hessian's lawful hold over the land above. A vampire without Sterling blood cannot open it alone.

**Threads touched.** Calum's resistance (12.6, 23.x). The price of it. Hessian moves early (16.3).

**Flags.** Calum's reach tells Hessian where the intruders are, but Hessian "has let them come" (Location Bible; 16.3). **DECIDED:** Hessian has known since the threshold that they were inside. The Mark passed him Calum's fear when Calum met the family (12.6), and he let it stand. Calum's reach gives the location, not the fact. Nobody in the antechamber knows he knew. Leave the mechanism vague on the page.

#### **Scene 3\. Welcome**

**Scene summary.** Hessian comes down into the covenant chamber with Catherine on his arm, correct and unhurried, and he does not look toward the antechamber. Then he does. "Welcome," he says, "to the covenant's true heart, Sterling family. I have waited a very long while for all of you to stand here together." He has let them come. He wants Catherine to choose him in front of the people who made her, so that her yes, given before her family, will be whole. "Nothing in this chamber is yours," he tells Edward, courteously, as a statement of fact. Thralls fill the gallery above. The ward window has not opened. The scene springs Hessian's trap, and he answers every question in the family's plan before they can ask it.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to stay hidden.

**Obstacle.** Hessian already knows.

**Turning point.** "Welcome..." and "Nothing in this chamber is yours."

**Consequence.** The trap is sprung.

**Location.** The covenant chamber, with its dais and seal and a gallery above.

**Characters.** Hessian, Catherine, the family, thralls.

**Weather and light.** Underground. Torchlight on ancient stone.

**Mood.** Menace.

**Date and time of day.** Sunday 19 December 1852, about quarter past ten.

**Time since previous scene.** About twenty-five minutes.

**Scene duration.** About ten minutes.

**Carried forward.** The eleven o'clock window hasn't opened.

**Setups and plants.** The line returned in Chapter 23\.

**Target length.** About 800 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 16.3**

**Continuity in.** About twenty-five minutes after 16.2. A quarter past ten. Beatrice's party is due at the gate at half past. The ward window (eleven) has not opened. The family has moved to a stair door onto the covenant chamber's gallery level, from where they can see.

**Continuity out.** The trap is sprung. Hessian has answered every question in the family's plan before they could ask it.

**Hessian (returning).**

* Reads late thirties, pale, fine-boned, dark hair slightly long. Eyes shift between not-quite-brown and black. Correct, faintly out of date black coat. Pearl powder at the collar. Low, formal, unhurried, possessive courtesy. Titles fastidiously correct.  
* Comes down into the chamber with Catherine on his arm and does not look toward the antechamber. Then does.  
* Line, close to the outline: "Welcome to the covenant's true heart, Sterling family. I have waited a very long while for all of you to stand here together." To Edward, courteously, as fact: "Nothing in this chamber is yours."  
* He wants her yes given before the people who made her, so that it will be whole.  
* Calls Catherine "Elisabeta." Addresses Edward as "Your Grace." Never raises his voice.

**Catherine.** On his arm, face composed. She does not look at her family. Her hand is steady. Inside, she counts her own thoughts.

**Edward.** Grave. Calm under a pressure that would break most men. Says nothing. Hand on Alexander's arm.

**Alexander.** Wants to stay hidden and cannot. Sees the dais, the seal in the floor, the gallery. His fingertips tap and he stops them.

**Thralls.** Fill the gallery above. They are seen here for the first time. Human, still, silent, faces slack under compulsion. Numbers per Calum's count (12.6).

**Location.** The covenant chamber. A large chamber with a dais, an ancient seal in the floor, and a gallery above. Torchlight on ancient stone.

**Rules in play.**

* The chamber answers to Sterling blood and to the purpose for which authority is claimed. Hessian holds the land above by lawful sale (12.2). The seal answers only to Sterling guardianship and blood. Catherine is Sterling-blooded.  
* Her compelled yes gives only a partial answer (12.2). He wants it whole.  
* The ward window has not opened. All wards are at full strength.

**Threads touched.** "Nothing in this chamber is yours" answered at 23.x. The lawful sale. Hessian's design, seen at last.

**Flags.** Hessian's knowledge is the cause of everything that follows. See 16.2. Do not have him explain it.

#### **Scene 4\. Before the signal**

**Scene summary.** Hessian leads Catherine to the dais. He speaks to her in the voice he has used all year, and something in her face softens in a way Alexander has never seen on his sister and cannot bear. It is compulsion, and it is working. Edward's hand is on Alexander's arm. Wait for her. Wait for the window. Then Hessian lifts her wrist toward the seal, and Alexander moves. He has read every document twice and walked every corridor and learned every rule, and he believes that knowing is the same as being ready. He is across the chamber floor with his father's sword before Catherine has had the chance to refuse. The scene is the book's fatal-flaw beat: his action is driven by fear for someone he loves, and it overrides her choice.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to save her from what he sees.

**Obstacle.** His father's hand, and the plan.

**Turning point.** He moves.

**Consequence.** The fatal-flaw beat.

**Location.** The chamber floor.

**Characters.** Alexander, Edward, Hessian, Catherine.

**Weather and light.** Underground.

**Mood.** Panic.

**Date and time of day.** Sunday 19 December 1852, about half past ten.

**Time since previous scene.** About five minutes.

**Scene duration.** About a minute.

**Carried forward.** The compulsion softening Catherine's face.

**Setups and plants.** The mirror at the threshold (Chapter 22, scene 6).

**Target length.** About 700 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 16.4**

**Continuity in.** About five minutes after 16.3. Half past ten. Hessian leads Catherine up the dais. Beatrice's noise is due at the gate now.

**Continuity out.** The fatal-flaw beat has happened. Alexander is across the floor with a sword. No one has heard Catherine's refusal.

**Hessian.** Speaks to Catherine in the voice he has used all year, low and warm. Lifts her wrist toward the seal.

**Catherine.** Something in her face softens in a way Alexander has never seen on his sister and cannot bear. It is compulsion, and it is working. She has not yet had the chance to refuse.

**Edward.** Hand on Alexander's arm. "Wait for her. Wait for the window."

**Alexander.** Wants to save her from what he sees. Has read every document twice, walked every corridor, learned every rule, and believes knowing is the same as being ready. He moves. He is across the chamber floor with Edward's reserve sword (the plain silver-etched blade from the armory, 6.6) before she can refuse. He knows without checking the watch how early it is.

**Niall.** Reaches for him half a second too late.

**Location.** The chamber floor, dais steps. Torchlight, cold.

**Rules in play.**

* The fatal flaw: his action, driven by fear for someone he loves, overrides her choice. This is the protection-against-agency theme at its point of failure.  
* The refusal has not happened. The window is not open. Nothing about this moment is the plan.  
* Catherine's yes is compelled. Her face is not.

**Threads touched.** The mirror scene at 22.6, where he waits (gallery, 5 a.m. on the 23rd). The correction of his flaw (21.3). "Can you imagine?" (21.2).

**Flags.** The outline calls it "his father's sword". Edward's own silver-etched broadsword (17.x) is different from the reserve sword Alexander has carried since 6.6. **DECIDED:** it is the reserve sword.

#### **Scene 5\. The ward awake**

**Scene summary.** The chamber answers the attack. The wards, still at full strength with no window open, wake the refuge, and the thralls come down the gallery stairs. Hessian turns, faster than anything Alexander has seen move, and Catherine is thrown clear. Niall is firing the signal flare, late and useless, into a stair shaft to the surface. Somewhere above, Percival's engine starts at last. The scene turns the rescue into a fight, and every consequence that follows flows from Alexander's choice.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to survive.

**Obstacle.** The wards are awake.

**Turning point.** Niall fires the signal late, and the engine starts.

**Consequence.** A fight.

**Location.** The chamber.

**Characters.** Everyone present, thralls, and Percival's engine far above.

**Weather and light.** Underground.

**Mood.** Chaos.

**Date and time of day.** Sunday 19 December 1852, about twenty-five to eleven.

**Time since previous scene.** Seconds.

**Scene duration.** A few minutes.

**Carried forward.** None.

**Setups and plants.** Percival's window (Chapter 17, scene 2).

**Target length.** About 600 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 16.5**

**Continuity in.** Seconds after 16.4. Twenty-five to eleven. The wards, still at full strength, wake.

**Continuity out.** The rescue is a fight. Every consequence flows from Alexander's choice. The engine has started but too late to matter.

**The wards.** Wake the refuge. Old stone shifts, the seal flares, thralls come down the gallery stairs in a silent line.

**Hessian.** Turns faster than anything Alexander has ever seen move. Catherine is thrown clear across the dais.

**Niall.** Fires the signal flare, late and useless, up a stair shaft to the surface. (It was meant for ten to eleven and the window; it now goes up after the fight begins.)

**Percival's engine.** Far above, it starts at last.

**Alexander.** Wants to survive. He has a sword and no plan. Sees Catherine on the floor and does not reach her.

**Edward.** Draws his silver-etched broadsword. Grave, precise. Moves to cover the servants' way.

**Amelia.** Already working. Fire, or water, or the old warding; she chooses fire.

**Catherine.** Thrown clear. Rises fast. She is still a person, still herself.

**Location.** The covenant chamber. Chaos. Torchlight, steel, the sound of thralls' feet on stone.

**Rules in play.**

* The window opens only after the flare. The engine needs time to reach strength, and at 10:35 it has barely begun.  
* Silver wounds a vampire. Hessian is fast, and cannot be hurt easily.  
* Thralls are human under compulsion; the Vanguard ethic applies. Do not treat them as monsters.

**Threads touched.** Percival's window (17.2). The early shutdown (17.x). The flare, meant for the party below to control (12.3).

**Flags.** "Late" for the flare means late to matter. **DECIDED:** it is fired earlier than the planned eleven, but after the attack, so it cannot help. The plan had it later, timed to the window.

#### **Scene 6\. Kill the knife-thrower**

**Scene summary.** Hessian speaks a single command, and Calum's body stops being his own. "Kill the knife-thrower." Calum comes for Niall, and Niall does not raise a knife against him. Alexander steps between them, and the blade meant for Niall's throat comes for his instead. Niall takes it. He turns into the blow and catches it along his ribs, and goes down. Catherine drags Calum off him with a strength neither of them expected from her. Edward's voice cuts across the chamber, the voice of the seventeenth Duke giving orders in a room he means to hold: "Amelia. The children." The scene brings the violence home to the romance's center and ends on Edward taking command of how he will die.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to protect Niall.

**Obstacle.** The command in Calum.

**Turning point.** Niall takes the blade.

**Consequence.** Niall is down, and Edward commands.

**Location.** The chamber stair.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall, Calum, Catherine, Edward, Hessian.

**Weather and light.** Underground.

**Mood.** Horror.

**Date and time of day.** Sunday 19 December 1852, about twenty to eleven.

**Time since previous scene.** A minute.

**Scene duration.** About two minutes.

**Carried forward.** "Kill the knife-thrower" from 10.3.

**Setups and plants.** Niall's wound (Chapter 18). The climax's inversion (Chapter 23).

**Target length.** About 700 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 16.6**

**Continuity in.** A minute after 16.5. Twenty to eleven. Niall has fired. The thralls are coming. Hessian is in the middle of the floor.

**Continuity out.** Niall is down with a wound along his ribs. Edward has taken command of how he will die. Calum is still under command.

**Hessian.** Speaks a single command. "Kill the knife-thrower." (The same words Calum spoke flat and borrowed at 10.3.)

**Calum.** His body stops being his own. The flat, borrowed steadiness. He fights the Mark, a sound like tearing, and cannot break it here. He comes for Niall with a short blade taken from the nearest thrall. Faster than anything human.

**Niall.** Does not raise a knife against him. He has never once been slow, and is not now. He steps past Alexander and turns into the blow, and it catches him along the ribs. He goes down.

**Alexander.** Steps between them first. The blade meant for Niall's throat comes for his. Niall's turn saves him. Alexander does not land a blow.

**Catherine.** Drags Calum off Niall with a strength neither of them expected from her. She is new to it and it frightens her.

**Edward.** Voice across the chamber, the voice of the seventeenth Duke giving orders in a room he means to hold: "Amelia. The children."

**Location.** The chamber stair, at the dais foot. Torchlight, blood on stone.

**Rules in play.**

* "Kill the knife-thrower" (10.3, 23.x). The command overrides Calum's will while it holds.  
* Niall wears the wound over his childhood rib scar (18.2 tells its story). Do not tell it here.  
* Catherine's strength is a new, unpracticed thing.  
* Edward's line ends the scene. Nothing further from him.

**Threads touched.** Niall's wound (18.x). The inversion of the rescue (17.5, 23.x). Calum's guilt. The romance at its violent center: he takes the blade for Alexander.

**Flags.**

* The outline gives Calum a blade and no source. **DECIDED:** a short blade taken from a thrall.  
* In 10.3 Calum turned the blow to Niall's forearm; here he cannot. **DECIDED:** the command is Hessian's own voice in the room, stronger than at range. Calum fights it anyway and the blow lands on ribs, not throat.
