### **Chapter 17: The Fall of the Old Order**

*The northern refuge, Winter 1852*

**CHAPTER OVERVIEW**

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander, with a brief look above at Percival. He wants to save everyone.

**Obstacle.** Hessian, the flood, and Percival's betrayal.

**Turning point.** Edward's death, Amelia's safeguard, and Percival's early shutdown.

**Consequence.** He ends the chapter at the mouth of a sea cave at dawn, the signet in his fist, with half his family gone. This is grief and guilt, and it sets up the aftermath in the sea caves.

**Span and length.** Sunday 19 December 1852, about quarter to eleven at night, to Monday 20 December, about quarter to eight in the morning. Target about 4,900 words.

#### **Scene 1\. The passage**

**Scene summary.** The only way out of the chamber is the narrow passage back toward the servants' way, and Hessian is between them and it. Edward goes to meet him with the silver-etched broadsword he has carried since Alexander was a child. Before he does, he closes his son's hand around his true signet. "Protect," he says. "Not own. Remember that when you're angry. Anger is evidence of conscience, not a plan of action." That is the prequel's load-bearing ethic, handed on in the only minute left to hand it. Then he turns and fights a four-hundred-year-old vampire in a doorway, and he fights well enough to lay silver across Hessian's face and chest and make him scream. The scene is Edward's death, active and purposeful. He passes on the signet and a purpose rather than a solution.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to reach his father.

**Obstacle.** Hessian.

**Turning point.** "Protect. Not own."

**Consequence.** The signet is in his hand and his father has gone to fight.

**Location.** The narrow passage from the chamber.

**Characters.** Edward, Alexander, Hessian.

**Weather and light.** Underground, with smoke beginning.

**Mood.** Heroic grief.

**Date and time of day.** Sunday 19 December 1852, about quarter to eleven.

**Time since previous scene.** Continuous.

**Scene duration.** About five minutes.

**Carried forward.** The silver-etched broadsword.

**Setups and plants.** The signet (Chapter 20). The line about anger (Chapter 20).

**Rules to honor.** Edward dies actively and on purpose. Silver wounds heal slowly.

**Target length.** About 900 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 17.1**

**Continuity in.** Continuous with 16.6. Sunday 19 December, a quarter to eleven. Niall is down at the dais stair. Hessian stands between the family and the narrow passage back toward the servants' way. Smoke is beginning. Alexander wears Edward's coat, with the watch and the ball in it (14.1, 14.6), and carries the reserve sword.

**Continuity out.** The signet is in Alexander's fist and his father has gone to fight. Edward has passed on a purpose, not a solution.

**Edward.** Grave, economical. Draws the silver-etched broadsword he has carried since Alexander was a child. Before he goes, he closes his son's hand around his signet ring. Line: "Protect. Not own. Remember that when you're angry. Anger is evidence of conscience, not a plan of action." He turns and fights Hessian in the doorway, and lays silver across Hessian's face and chest and makes him scream. He dies actively and on purpose (17.4). He is without his coat, in shirtsleeves and waistcoat, his scarred hands sure.

**Alexander.** Wants to reach his father. Cannot. The signet is cold and heavy in his hand. He does not say anything in reply, or a single word. Anger is a thing he feels and has nowhere to put.

**Hessian.** A four-hundred-year-old vampire. Fast, formal, unhurried until the silver. He screams at the burn. Does not raise his voice otherwise. His face and chest are scored and heal slowly.

**Location.** The narrow passage from the chamber. Smoke beginning, torchlight, stone.

**Rules in play.**

* Silver wounds a vampire and heals slowly (Dossier). Hessian's scars persist (24.x, "starved and silver-scored").  
* The ethic: protect, not own. It is the prequel's load-bearing ethic. Do not paraphrase it.  
* Edward's death is active and purposeful. He passes on the signet and a purpose.  
* The signet has no power without rightful acceptance (20.x). Alexander does not know that yet.

**Threads touched.** The signet (20.1 to 20.5). The line about anger (20.x). The coat. Edward's arc: protection against trust, resolved at last.

**Flags.**

* The outline says "his true signet". **DECIDED:** it is Edward's signet ring as he wears it, the Sterling guardianship signet. Do not invent a second ring.  
* The reserve sword (Alexander's) and Edward's broadsword are both silver-etched. Keep the two distinct.

#### **Scene 2\. The window closes**

**Scene summary.** Above, Percival's surveyor has the relief. His rubbings of the seven sigils are safe in an oilskin, and Hessian's thralls are converging on the engine. Percival weighs the Sterlings' lives against his prize and his own skin, and shuts the engine down early. The wards snap back like a slammed door. Deep in the hill, the drowned channel that closed behind the party gives way, and Rhoswen's loch comes into the refuge's lower halls in a black roar. The scene shows the Institute's share of the catastrophe, the payoff of Edward's pragmatic bargain, and the start of the flood.

**Perspective and goal.** A short passage from Percival's side, above ground. He wants to secure his prize.

**Obstacle.** The thralls are converging on the engine.

**Turning point.** He shuts the engine off.

**Consequence.** The wards snap back and the flood begins.

**Location.** The manor's upper ground, and the engine emplacement on the headland.

**Characters.** Percival, his surveyor, and thralls.

**Weather and light.** Clear moonlit night, with the moon high in the south and setting about quarter past twelve. Frost.

**Mood.** Cold calculation.

**Date and time of day.** Sunday 19 December 1852, about five past eleven.

**Time since previous scene.** About twenty minutes.

**Scene duration.** About five minutes.

**Carried forward.** The rubbings from 15.3.

**Setups and plants.** Chapter 24, scene 2\.

**Target length.** About 500 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 17.2**

**Continuity in.** About twenty minutes after 17.1's start, though the outline runs this after 17.1. Five past eleven. The flare went up at about ten to eleven (16.5) and the engine has run since. Above ground: clear, frost, the moon low in the south-southwest.

**Continuity out.** The wards snap back. The channel gives way and the flood starts. Percival has his rubbings and his skin.

**Percival (POV).**

32. Athletic, handsome, deceptively friendly. Sandy blond hair arranged to look tousled, blue eyes, charming at a distance and cold up close. Absurdly overdressed, collar a fraction too high over the scar, silver-headed cane. Checks his watch.  
* Two fingers to the scar when composure slips. His father's disappointment is the fear under his charm (7.5, Silas: "observe, record, do not engage").  
* Interior voice: self-flattering, resentful, calculating.  
* Watches thralls converging on the engine. Weighs the Sterlings' lives against his prize and his own skin, and shuts the engine down early. He does not agonize; he calculates.

**The surveyor.** Unnamed. Thin, ink-stained, nervous. Entered the relief hall through the manor cellars while the wards were down and has made rubbings of one line of seven linked sigils, now safe in an oilskin.

**Thralls.** Human, slack-faced, silent, coming up from the manor toward the headland.

**Location.** The manor's upper ground, and the engine emplacement on the headland. Frost, clear moonlit night, the sea below.

**Rules in play.**

* The engine suppresses the wards above for a measured window, started by the party's flare (12.3, 16.5). Shutting it early lets the wards return at full strength.  
* Percival's successes are always partial (Dossier). He gets the rubbings and loses the alliance.  
* The flood is not Percival's plan and he does not know it follows.

**Threads touched.** Percival's rubbings (24.x). His partial escape (22.x). "Sterling," a word that changes meaning (22.x, 24.x). Silas's letter.

**Flags.**

* The outline says the moon is "high in the south". At 11 p.m. on the 19th, a first-quarter moon is low in the south-southwest and sets about quarter past midnight. **DECIDED:** low in the south-southwest.  
* The outline gives no length for the window. **DECIDED:** the agreed window was half an hour from the flare; Percival cuts it at about fifteen minutes. Fox may change the numbers.  
* How the surveyor reached the relief is not in the outline. **DECIDED:** through the manor cellars, the same route Gideon later uses (22.x), while the wards were down.  
* The outline's order (17.1 at 10:45, 17.3 at 10:50, 17.2 at 11:05) is non-monotonic. **DECIDED:** 17.2 sits between 17.3 and 17.4 in time.  
* Beatrice's diversion at the gate road (10:30) is never shown. **DECIDED:** Percival hears it faintly and it is enough; do not resolve it here.

#### **Scene 3\. The safeguard**

**Scene summary.** Amelia has Catherine's face between her hands and her son's signet hand pressed under her own. What she makes, she makes with everything left in her. It is fire and water-memory and purification by extraction, and it is paid for all at once. Her nose streams blood, and the silver-grey lines Loch Maree left in her palms blaze white. She plants Catherine's own self, uncompelled, in her daughter's blood beneath the cold bite: the girl who embroidered swallows and saved her pin money and wanted to see the world for its own sake, not for anyone's. She binds it to the signet and to the covenant, so that it will wake only when Alexander claims this place to protect rather than to own. She tells him none of that. She tells him, "Take her. Go." Then she turns her last fire on the thralls in the gallery, and the gallery burns. Amelia dies making the safeguard, and it gives Alexander the means to choose rather than doing the choosing for him.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander, witnessing. He wants his mother to come with them.

**Obstacle.** Her choice.

**Turning point.** "Take her. Go."

**Consequence.** The safeguard is planted and Amelia dies.

**Location.** The chamber and the gallery.

**Characters.** Amelia, Catherine, Alexander, and thralls.

**Weather and light.** Underground. Fire.

**Mood.** Terrible grace.

**Date and time of day.** Sunday 19 December 1852, about ten to eleven.

**Time since previous scene.** Overlaps 17.1.

**Scene duration.** About five minutes.

**Carried forward.** Amelia's palms from 2.2.

**Setups and plants.** The warmth under the bite (Chapters 18 and 20).

**Rules to honor.** and the definition of the safeguard.

**Target length.** About 900 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 17.3**

**Continuity in.** Overlaps 17.1. Ten to eleven. Catherine has been thrown clear and has risen. Thralls are on the gallery stairs. Alexander is beside his mother with the signet in his fist.

**Continuity out.** The safeguard is planted. Amelia has burned the gallery and stays behind. Alexander has the means to choose, and does not know it.

**Amelia.**

* Field leathers, braid coming loose, silver-grey lines in her palms. She holds Catherine's face between her hands and Alexander's signet hand pressed under her own.  
* What she makes, she makes with everything left in her: fire, water-memory, purification by extraction, paid all at once. Her nose streams blood. The silver-grey lines Loch Maree left in her palms blaze white. Her eyes go black and bruised.  
* She plants Catherine's own self, uncompelled, in her daughter's blood beneath the cold bite: the girl who embroidered swallows, saved her pin money, and wanted to see the world for its own sake. She binds it to the signet and to the covenant, so that it will wake only when Alexander claims this place to protect rather than to own.  
* She tells him none of that. Line: "Take her. Go."  
* She turns her last fire on the thralls in the gallery, and the gallery burns.  
* She might whisper something to Catherine that Alexander does not hear.

**Catherine.** Held. She feels something cold at the bite turn warm. She does not know what it is.

**Alexander.** Wants his mother to come with them. She will not. He does not argue: he knows what her hands look like. Tells himself he will come back for her.

**Thralls.** In the gallery, still and silent, burning.

**Location.** The covenant chamber and the gallery above. Fire on stone, smoke, heat and cold.

**Rules in play.**

* The safeguard: Catherine's uncompelled self, planted in her blood beneath the bite, bound to the signet and covenant, waking only when a Sterling claims the covenant to protect. It empowers a choice and does not solve the climax (Main Bible).  
* It binds to Catherine as readily as it does because it carries a trace of the Elisabeta blood they share, which Seraphina recognizes in Book Four. Amelia does not know this.  
* Amelia's magic always costs her. She is never merely Edward's support.

**Threads touched.** The warmth under the bite (18.x, 20.1). The safeguard wakes at 23.4. The signet (20.x). Amelia's cost.

**Flags.**

* The outline's "Rules to honor" line is cut off at "and the definition of the safeguard." **DECIDED:** the definition above.  
* The outline says "Amelia dies making the safeguard", yet her voice is heard at 17.5 after the flood begins. **DECIDED:** she plants the safeguard at about five to eleven, burns the gallery, stays behind, and dies about twenty past eleven, her last working audible as the survivors flee. The cost of the safeguard is what kills her.

#### **Scene 4\. Edward**

**Scene summary.** Through the smoke Alexander sees his father in the doorway, run through, still standing, still holding the passage with a sword arm that no longer lifts. Hessian, wounded and smoking, has fallen back into the dark. Edward's eyes find his son. He does not speak. He looks, as he did at the camp, as though looking is all he has patience for. Then the flood reaches the doorway and takes him. The scene makes Alexander witness his father's death, which is worse than hearing of it and is where the guilt that drives Part Four comes from.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to reach his father.

**Obstacle.** Smoke and water.

**Turning point.** The flood takes Edward.

**Consequence.** He has witnessed the death.

**Location.** The passage doorway.

**Characters.** Alexander, Edward.

**Weather and light.** Rising black water.

**Mood.** Devastation.

**Date and time of day.** Sunday 19 December 1852, about ten past eleven.

**Time since previous scene.** About ten minutes.

**Scene duration.** About a minute.

**Carried forward.** Edward's look from 11.4.

**Setups and plants.** Guilt (Chapter 19).

**Target length.** About 500 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 17.4**

**Continuity in.** About ten minutes after 17.3. Ten past eleven. The gallery burns. The flood has begun (17.2). Alexander turns at the servants' way to look back.

**Continuity out.** Edward is dead. The guilt that drives Part Four is set.

**Edward.** Grave to the last. In the narrow doorway, run through, still standing, still holding the passage, a sword arm that no longer lifts. His eyes find his son. He does not speak. He looks, as he did at the camp (11.4), as though looking is all he has patience for. The flood reaches the doorway and takes him.

**Alexander.** Wants to reach his father. Cannot: smoke and water. He does not shout. He watches. Fingertips still.

**Hessian.** Wounded and smoking, has fallen back into the dark. Not seen again in this scene.

**Location.** The passage doorway. Smoke, rising black water, the roar of it.

**Rules in play.**

* Edward dies in view. Worse than hearing of it.  
* His body is never recovered (Location Bible). The public account says buried abroad (26.x).  
* Nobody speaks. This is silent. The watch in Alexander's pocket stops in the flood (18.x).

**Threads touched.** Guilt (19.x). Edward's look (11.4). The watch, stopped (18.x, 26.6).

**Flags.** The outline says "run through" and does not say by whom or what. **DECIDED:** leave unspecified. Do not attribute it to Hessian's hand or a thrall's blade.

#### **Scene 5\. The water**

**Scene summary.** Niall cannot walk. Catherine picks him up as if he weighed nothing and carries him through water chest-deep and rising, following the route inked in her own atlas. Alexander goes ahead with Seraphina's lamp, and Seraphina herself, grey and shaking from holding the vault, comes stumbling out of the dark to meet them. Calum is gone. Hessian has seized him back through the Mark and dragged him away into the refuge's depths. Behind them, the last thing Alexander hears before stone and distance swallow it is his mother's voice raised in something that is not quite a working and not quite a prayer, and then nothing. The scene gets the survivors out by Catherine's strength and Catherine's plan. It inverts the rescue, since the rescued carries the rescuer.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to get everyone out.

**Obstacle.** The water, and Niall's wound.

**Turning point.** Catherine carries Niall.

**Consequence.** The rescue is inverted and Calum is lost.

**Location.** The flooding servants' way.

**Characters.** Alexander, Catherine, Niall, Seraphina.

**Weather and light.** Chest-deep water, very cold.

**Mood.** Desperate.

**Date and time of day.** Sunday 19 December 1852, about quarter past eleven, to Monday 20 December, about two in the morning.

**Time since previous scene.** About five minutes.

**Scene duration.** About three hours, compressed.

**Carried forward.** The atlas route from 15.4.

**Setups and plants.** Calum under the Mark (Chapter 22).

**Rules to honor.** Vampire strength.

**Target length.** About 900 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 17.5**

**Continuity in.** About five minutes after 17.4. A quarter past eleven, Sunday night, running to about two on Monday morning, compressed. Niall cannot walk. The flood is chest-deep and rising in the servants' way.

**Continuity out.** The survivors are out of the chamber by Catherine's strength and Catherine's plan. Calum is lost under the Mark. Amelia is last heard, then silent.

**Catherine.** Picks Niall up as if he weighed nothing and carries him through water chest-deep and rising. Follows the route inked in her own atlas (Alexander has it inside the coat). She is new to her strength and does not stop to think about it.

**Niall.** Wounded along the ribs (16.6). Grey, sweating, silent. Conscious and mortified.

**Alexander.** Goes ahead with Seraphina's lamp (she gave it to him at 15.5). Reads the map twice. Numb. Wet through, in his father's coat.

**Seraphina.** Grey and shaking from holding the vault, comes stumbling out of the dark to meet them. Archaic speech may surface: she is tired. She says little.

**Calum.** Gone. Hessian seized him back through the Mark and dragged him into the refuge's depths.

**Amelia's voice.** The last thing Alexander hears before stone and distance swallow it: his mother's voice raised in something not quite a working and not quite a prayer, and then nothing.

**Location.** The flooding servants' way. Chest-deep, very cold, black water.

**Rules in play.**

* Vampire strength.  
* Water: the flood is Rhoswen's loch. It is not her decision (17.2).  
* Vampire blood-hunger: the party is wet and bleeding; Catherine must not feed. No attack on Niall.  
* Keep the flood in fragments, not narrative summary: lamp, water, voice, dark.

**Threads touched.** The atlas route (15.4). Calum under the Mark (22.x, 23.x). The inversion of the rescue. The watch stops.

**Flags.** The outline has no beat for Gideon and the captives here. **DECIDED:** they have already gone ahead (17.6).

#### **Scene 6\. Into the sea caves**

**Scene summary.** The servants' way ends where the refuge meets the sea. Salt air, black rock, and a grey smear of coming dawn at a cave mouth. Gideon is there already, with the freed captives, soaked and shaking, having found the way out by following the water. Alexander stands in his father's coat with the signet in his fist and cannot make his hand open. Catherine, who cannot go further toward the dawn, sets Niall down in the dark at the back of the cave and stays there with him. The scene is the escape, and the image that closes it is the one that will haunt Part Four: the family of four that went in has become two, and one of the two cannot walk into the light.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to breathe.

**Obstacle.** The coming sun.

**Turning point.** None. This is an image.

**Consequence.** Two survivors from the four, and one of them can't step into the light.

**Location.** The sea cave mouth.

**Characters.** Alexander, Catherine, Niall, Seraphina, Gideon, the captives.

**Weather and light.** A grey dawn and salt wind. Sunrise is about 8:41.

**Mood.** Numb.

**Date and time of day.** Monday 20 December 1852, about quarter to eight in the morning.

**Time since previous scene.** About five and a half hours, spent lost and resting in the dark.

**Scene duration.** About ten minutes.

**Carried forward.** The signet in his fist.

**Setups and plants.** The bothy (Chapter 18).

**Rules to honor.** Catherine shelters in the dark at the back of the cave.

**Target length.** About 600 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 17.6**

**Continuity in.** About five and a half hours after 17.5's end, lost and resting in the dark. Monday 20 December, a quarter to eight in the morning. The servants' way has ended at the sea.

**Continuity out.** Two of the family's four survive (Alexander and Catherine). One cannot step into the light. The bothy comes next.

**Alexander.** Wants to breathe. Stands at the cave mouth in his father's coat with the signet in his fist and cannot make his hand open. Numb. No speech, or one word.

**Catherine.** Cannot go further toward the dawn. Sets Niall down in the dark at the back of the cave and stays there with him. Grey, still, one hand on Niall's wrist.

**Niall.** Conscious or half so. The wound is along the ribs.

**Seraphina.** Sits against the cave wall, spent, the dead eye closed.

**Gideon.** There already with the freed captives, soaked and shaking, having found the way out by following the water. Dry humor absent. Says only what is needed.

**The captives.** The drover, the shepherd's widow, the boy of about twelve, and the others. Huddled, quiet.

**Location.** The sea cave mouth, where the servants' way meets the sea. Black rock, salt wind, a grey smear of coming dawn. Sunrise about 8:41.

**Rules in play.**

* Sunlight: about thirty minutes to kill a vampire. Catherine shelters in the dark (Dossier).  
* The image: a family of four went in, and two came out. One cannot walk into the light.  
* The signet is in his fist, unworn and unopened.

**Threads touched.** The bothy (18.1). Guilt (19.x). The signet (20.x). Grief.

**Flags.** "Half his family gone" and "family of four" leave Niall and Seraphina out of the count. **DECIDED:** the family of four is Edward, Amelia, Alexander and Catherine. Niall is not counted, which the scene should not dwell on.

### **Chapter 18: The Sea Caves**

*The coastal safehouse, Scottish Highlands, Winter 1852*

**CHAPTER OVERVIEW**

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander, with an Ewan interlude. He wants to hold what's left together.

**Obstacle.** Grief, wounds, and hunger.

**Turning point.** He asks instead of telling (the cup), and he finds the watch and the ball.

**Consequence.** By night he has corrected one flaw in the moment, but grief is unresolved. That sets up the terms in Chapter 19\.

**Span and length.** Monday 20 December 1852\. Target about 4,500 words.

#### **Scene 1\. The bothy**

**Scene summary.** Ellery's operatives find them before noon and bring them by boat along the coast to a Vanguard safehouse, a stone bothy with a hidden room dug into the hillside behind it, windowless and dry. Catherine goes in by dark, wrapped in Ellery's coat. Beatrice's party comes in from the western road bloodied. Two operatives are dead and Sebastian is unhurt, and Sebastian's ledger is nowhere in sight for the first time since Alexander met him. Beatrice looks at Alexander once and knows everything. She does not say it. The scene regathers the survivors and makes the cost of the failed rescue visible across the whole company.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants safety.

**Obstacle.** Nothing but the cost to everyone.

**Turning point.** Beatrice knows everything with one look.

**Consequence.** The company is regathered.

**Location.** The coastal bothy and its hidden room.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall, Seraphina, Gideon, Beatrice, Sebastian, Ellery, operatives. Catherine arrives after dark by boat.

**Weather and light.** A grey morning with a heavy sea and spray.

**Mood.** Exhaustion.

**Date and time of day.** Monday 20 December 1852, about ten to noon.

**Time since previous scene.** About two hours.

**Scene duration.** About two hours.

**Carried forward.** Two operatives dead in the field.

**Setups and plants.** Sebastian's absent ledger. Beatrice's judgment (Chapter 19).

**Rules to honor.** Catherine must not travel in daylight. She comes after dusk.

**Target length.** About 700 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 18.1**

**Continuity in.** About two hours after 17.6 (a quarter to eight). Monday 20 December, about ten to noon. Ellery's operatives found the survivors at the sea cave before noon and took them by boat along the coast. Catherine stays in the cave until dusk, with two operatives, wrapped in Ellery's coat when she does cross (see flags). The signet is still in Alexander's fist.

**Continuity out.** The company is regathered and the cost is visible across all of it. Beatrice knows without being told. Sebastian's ledger is missing.

**Beatrice.** Practical wool, iron-grey coil, the ward-worked brooch. Comes in from the western road with her party bloodied. She looks at Alexander once and knows everything. She does not say it. Two of her operatives are dead. From this moment she is in effect Acting Commander (Edward is dead), though the title is not spoken yet.

**Sebastian.** Unhurt, groomed still, fair-skinned and prematurely grey. His ledger is nowhere in sight for the first time since Alexander met him. He is quiet. He counts the dead in his head and does not say so.

**Ellery.** Weathered, near forty, broad in the shoulders, hair close-pinned, a man's coat cut down. Clipped, in distances and hours. She lost two of her own and reports it in the same breath as everything else.

**Alexander.** Wants safety and knows there is none. Wears the coat. He does not put the signet away and does not open his hand fully. Fingertips still, or gripping.

**Niall.** Grey, on a pallet, conscious, breathing shallowly (ribs). Says nothing.

**Seraphina and Gideon.** Seraphina sits near the fire, the dead eye closed, the good one shut with fatigue. Gideon has seen the captives handed on to Ellery's people and is wet through.

**Location.** The coastal bothy and its hidden room. A stone bothy with a hidden room dug into the hillside behind it, windowless and dry. Grey morning, heavy sea, spray on the shingle.

**Rules in play.**

* Catherine must not travel in daylight. She comes after dusk.  
* The cost of the failed rescue is visible: two operatives dead, Edward and Amelia lost, Calum lost, Niall wounded, Seraphina spent.  
* Beatrice does not speak the news aloud. Do not have anyone say "The Duke is dead" in dialogue in this scene.

**Threads touched.** Sebastian's absent ledger (19.x). Beatrice's judgment (19.1). The council fracture. The bothy as the second refuge.

**Flags.**

* The scene summary has Catherine going in "by dark, wrapped in Ellery's coat", but the character list has her arriving after dark and 18.3 (4:30 p.m.) says she came just after dusk (sunset about 3:13). **DECIDED:** she is not in this scene. Her crossing (after dusk, about four o'clock) opens 18.3 in one line.  
* Alexander leaving his sister in the cave is not in the outline. **DECIDED:** Catherine sends him with Niall: "Go and see to him, Alex. I'd be no use in a boat by daylight." He goes.  
* The outline gives no fate for the captives. **DECIDED:** Ellery's operatives take them on by another boat to a place not named on the page.  
* The reason for Sebastian's missing ledger is not given. **DECIDED:** unexplained; Beatrice notes it and says nothing.

#### **Scene 2\. The swallow handkerchief**

**Scene summary.** Seraphina cleans Niall's wound, a long cut along the ribs that has opened the old curved scar he has kept hidden since boyhood. Alexander sees that scar for the first time and does not ask. He binds the wound with Catherine's swallow handkerchief, because it is the cleanest cloth in the bothy and because it is his. Niall, grey-faced, watches him do it. "Your father knew that arithmetic a long time before you did," he says. "I don't imagine he'd mind you learning it the hard way, so long as you go on remembering it." The scene carries the romance through the most physical care yet, and it plants the scar and the handkerchief.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to help Niall.

**Obstacle.** The wound.

**Turning point.** He sees the scar.

**Consequence.** The most physical care yet.

**Location.** The bothy, by the fire.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall, Seraphina.

**Weather and light.** Rain against the window.

**Mood.** Intimate.

**Date and time of day.** Monday 20 December 1852, about half past twelve.

**Time since previous scene.** About thirty minutes.

**Scene duration.** About twenty minutes.

**Carried forward.** The handkerchief from 1.2.

**Setups and plants.** Chapter 26 (Niall's handkerchief).

**Rules to honor.** Niall's hidden curved scar.

**Target length.** About 700 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 18.2**

**Continuity in.** About thirty minutes after 18.1 began. Monday, half past twelve. Niall lies by the fire in the main room. Rain has begun against the window.

**Continuity out.** Alexander has seen the old scar and asked nothing. The handkerchief is on the wound. Niall has said a line that does not absolve.

**Seraphina.** Cleans the wound, a long cut along the ribs that has opened Niall's old curved scar. Her good eye is bad in the firelight, and her hands are steady. She does not comment on the scar. Tired: archaic speech may slip in.

**Niall.** Grey-faced, dry even now. Watches Alexander bind him. His line, close to the outline: "Your father knew that arithmetic a long time before you did. I don't imagine he'd mind you learning it the hard way, so long as you go on remembering it." He may use "Alex" (private, honest, danger).

**Alexander.** Wants to help. Sees the curved scar, the old rib scar he has never seen, and does not ask. Binds the wound with Catherine's swallow handkerchief (one of the two from 1.2), because it is the cleanest cloth in the bothy and because it is his. His hands are careful. Tells himself the guilt is not this scene's to hold.

**Location.** The bothy, by the fire. Rain against the window, grey light, wood smoke.

**Rules in play.**

* Niall's curved rib scar is a childhood scar he has kept hidden (Main Bible). Its story is not told here. The scene ends without it, and Alexander does not ask.  
* "That arithmetic" refers to the cost of the failed attack, the weighing of lives against a plan. Do not spell it out.  
* The romance is care through behavior. No confession, no declaration.

**Threads touched.** The handkerchief (26.x, Niall keeps it). The scar and its story (Book Two). The romance's most physical care yet. The guilt over 16.4.

**Flags.**

* The outline's "that arithmetic" has no antecedent. **DECIDED:** it means weighing one life against a plan's timing.  
* Which of the two swallow handkerchiefs is used is not stated. **DECIDED:** Alexander's own. Niall's is not in play.

#### **Scene 3\. The cup**

**Scene summary.** Catherine's hunger has been rising since the refuge. She has counted every pulse in the bothy and hates that she can. There is no Harcourt here and no store of anything. Seraphina knows the method of cut and cup, and Catherine knows what Calum taught her. The question is who. Alexander rolls up his sleeve before anyone else can speak. Catherine refuses. He tells her it is not her decision to protect him from, and hears himself and stops, because that is exactly what their parents did to him. He asks her instead. She says yes. Seraphina makes the cut, and the cup fills, and Catherine drinks with both hands and her face turned away. Nothing visible happens. The scene makes the cut-and-cup feeding practice physical, and plants the Book Four bloodline payoff invisibly. It also shows Alexander correcting his flaw in the moment, for the first time.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to feed his sister.

**Obstacle.** Her refusal, and his own impulse to decide.

**Turning point.** He asks.

**Consequence.** The flaw is corrected in the moment, and the donor role is set.

**Location.** The hidden room.

**Characters.** Alexander, Catherine, Seraphina.

**Weather and light.** Underground and dark.

**Mood.** Sacred and awkward.

**Date and time of day.** Monday 20 December 1852, about half past four in the afternoon. (Catherine was brought over by boat just after dusk.)

**Time since previous scene.** About four hours.

**Scene duration.** About thirty minutes.

**Carried forward.** What Calum taught her.

**Setups and plants.** Alexander's blood carries the property (Book Four).

**Rules to honor.** Cut and cup. Nothing visible happens.

**Target length.** About 800 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 18.3**

**Continuity in.** About four hours after 18.2. Half past four in the afternoon. Catherine crossed by boat just after dusk (about a quarter past three) wrapped in Ellery's coat, and has been taken into the hidden room. Alexander has the atlas from 15.4 in the coat and gave it to her when she arrived.

**Continuity out.** The flaw is corrected in the moment. The donor role is set. Nothing visible happens.

**Catherine.** Grey, sunken-eyed, the red rising at the rim. Her hunger has climbed since the refuge, and she has counted every pulse in the bothy and hates that she can. She refuses Alexander's offer, and then says yes when he asks. She drinks with both hands and her face turned away. Calum taught her to hold the cup that way (7.3). Her dry lightness is thin tonight.

**Alexander.** Wants to feed his sister. Rolls up his sleeve before anyone else can speak. Catherine refuses. He tells her it is not her decision to protect him from, and hears himself and stops, because that is exactly what their parents did to him. He asks instead. Grief and something like awe. He does not look away.

**Seraphina.** Knows the method of cut and cup. Makes the cut on the inner forearm and holds the cup. Says little. Her hands are quick and clean. Her dead eye is closed. She takes note of what she does not say (Book Four).

**Location.** The hidden room. Underground, windowless, dry, dark, lit by one lamp turned low.

**Rules in play.**

* Cut and cup: a cut, a cup, no fangs. Blood spoils within hours, so it is drunk at once.  
* Consent: he asks, she says yes. The Vanguard ethic (2.3).  
* Nothing visible happens. Alexander's blood carries the same curative property as Catherine's (Main Bible), unknown to anyone here. Do not hint at it.  
* There is no Harcourt and no store of anything here.

**Threads touched.** Alexander's flaw corrected (21.3). The bloodline (Book Four). The cups (7.3, 15.2). Catherine's consent over her body.

**Flags.** The amount taken is not stated. **DECIDED:** a cup, modest; Alexander is light-headed and no more.

#### **Scene 4\. The pockets**

**Scene summary.** Alone in the loft after dark, Alexander goes through the pockets of his father's coat. In one he finds Edward's pocket watch, stopped. He does not wind it, and he will not wind it for weeks. In another he finds a flattened rifle ball, its lead misshapen by an impact, carried so long the edges have worn smooth. He has no idea what it is or why his father kept it. He puts it back in the pocket with the watch and keeps both there. The scene plants the watch and the rifle ball, and holds a secret Alexander does not know he is keeping.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to be alone.

**Obstacle.** Grief.

**Turning point.** He finds the ball.

**Consequence.** He carries a secret he doesn't know he's keeping.

**Location.** The bothy loft.

**Characters.** Alexander.

**Weather and light.** Rain easing, full dark outside.

**Mood.** Quiet grief.

**Date and time of day.** Monday 20 December 1852, about half past five in the evening.

**Time since previous scene.** About an hour.

**Scene duration.** About fifteen minutes.

**Carried forward.** The coat from 14.1.

**Setups and plants.** Chapter 26 (he winds the watch).

**Target length.** About 500 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 18.4**

**Continuity in.** About an hour after 18.3. Half past five, full dark outside. Rain easing. Alexander is alone in the bothy loft in his father's coat.

**Continuity out.** He carries a secret he does not know he is keeping. The ball is in the pocket with the watch.

**Alexander.** Wants to be alone. Grief. Goes through the pockets of his father's coat. In one, Edward's pocket watch, stopped (the flood, 17.4). He does not wind it, and will not for weeks. In another, a flattened rifle ball, its lead misshapen by an impact, the edges worn smooth by carrying. He has no idea what it is or why his father kept it. He puts it back in the pocket with the watch and keeps both there.

**Objects.**

* The watch: Edward's, stopped at the flood. It has lived in the coat since 14.1 (14.6). He saw his father take it out and check it at 14.6.  
* The ball: flattened lead from 1838, the shot meant for Edward that killed Alasdair Fraser. Edward cut it from his own coat and carried it for life. Alexander does not connect it to Niall's father.

**Location.** The bothy loft. Rain easing, full dark outside, one candle.

**Rules in play.**

* He does not wind the watch (26.6).  
* He does not learn what the ball is. Niall knows the story and does not tell it here.  
* The signet is somewhere on him, still unworn (19.x). Keep it out of the pocket scene.

**Threads touched.** The watch (26.6). The ball (Book Two). Guilt (19.x).

**Flags.** The atlas is not among the coat's contents. **DECIDED:** he gave it to Catherine on her arrival (18.3).

#### **Scene 5\. No knocks**

**Scene summary.** This interlude is from Ewan's point of view. He comes in with the horses after dark and hears that the Duke is dead. He goes out behind the bothy and stands in the snow with his hand over the black-corded packet inside his coat, the second one Edward gave him seven years ago, with no address. He does not open it. No one has knocked three times at his door after dark on a night when no one sent for him. He decides that the packet's terms are the last order the Duke gave him, and that he will keep them. The scene holds a series thread in place and gives grief a second, humbler face.

**Perspective and goal.** Ewan (interlude). He wants to honor the Duke.

**Obstacle.** Temptation.

**Turning point.** He decides the packet's terms are his last order.

**Consequence.** The thread holds.

**Location.** Behind the bothy, in the snow.

**Characters.** Ewan.

**Weather and light.** A cold night with a waxing moon behind cloud.

**Mood.** Humble grief.

**Date and time of day.** Monday 20 December 1852, about half past six in the evening.

**Time since previous scene.** About an hour.

**Scene duration.** About ten minutes.

**Carried forward.** The packet.

**Setups and plants.** A later book.

**Rules to honor.** Three evenly spaced knocks, after dark, on a night no one has sent for him.

**Target length.** About 400 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 18.5**

**Continuity in.** About an hour after 18.4. Half past six in the evening. Ewan has come overland with the horses after dark and been told at the door that the Duke is dead.

**Continuity out.** The thread holds. Ewan will keep the packet's terms.

**Ewan (POV).**

24. Weather-hardened, lean from riding, sandy-brown hair, cap pulled low, a rider's bowed ease. Turns his cap once in his hands when nervous.  
* Soft Highland burr, plain, practical. "Sir" to Alexander. He met Niall at Loch Maree in 1845, when he was seventeen and rode to Sterling Castle the first time.  
* He goes out behind the bothy and stands in the snow with his hand over the black-corded packet inside his coat. He does not open it.  
* He decides that the packet's terms are the last order the Duke gave him, and that he will keep them.

**The packet.** Black-corded, no address, given to him by Edward seven years ago (1845). To be opened when someone knocks three times, evenly spaced, at his door after dark on a night when no one sent for him. Nobody has knocked. (Opened in Book Four.)

**Location.** Behind the bothy, in the snow. Cold night, a waxing moon (about two thirds) behind cloud.

**Rules in play.**

* The trigger is three evenly spaced knocks, after dark, on a night no one sent for him.  
* He does not open it, and does not tell anyone he has it.  
* Ewan was told plainly at the door. Do not make a scene of the telling.

**Threads touched.** A later book (Book Four). Grief's humbler face. Edward's last orders.

**Flags.**

* The outline calls it "the second one Edward gave him". Nothing in the bibles or prequel names the first. **DECIDED:** on the page it is only "the packet". Do not explain "second".  
* The outline has Ewan bring the horses "after dark" from the camp to a bothy on the coast. Route unstated. **DECIDED:** overland, unremarked.

#### **Scene 6\. What Catherine carries**

**Scene summary.** Catherine sits with Niall in the dark room while he sleeps. She touches the bite beneath her collarbone through her collar and finds it no longer entirely cold. There is a warmth under it that was not there before the refuge, faint as a hand remembered. She does not know what her mother did to her. She knows only that something of Amelia is in her now, and she will not let anyone take it out to look at it until she understands it. The scene sets up the safeguard's reading in Chapter 20, told through Catherine's body, and protects her consent over her own blood.

**Perspective and goal.** Catherine. She wants to understand the change in her.

**Obstacle.** Ignorance.

**Turning point.** She protects it.

**Consequence.** The safeguard is set up for its reading.

**Location.** The hidden room.

**Characters.** Catherine, Niall (asleep).

**Weather and light.** Dark.

**Mood.** Wonder.

**Date and time of day.** Monday 20 December 1852, about eleven at night.

**Time since previous scene.** About four and a half hours.

**Scene duration.** About ten minutes.

**Carried forward.** The safeguard from 17.3.

**Setups and plants.** Chapter 20, scene 1\.

**Target length.** About 500 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 18.6**

**Continuity in.** About four and a half hours after 18.5. Eleven at night. Niall is asleep in the hidden room from the fever and the wound. Alexander has gone to the loft. Catherine has been sitting with Niall.

**Catherine (POV).**

* Grey pallor, the mark by her left eye, auburn hair loose. Still, in the way she was never still before. Her interior is quick and sharp.  
* She touches the bite beneath her collarbone through her collar and finds it no longer entirely cold. There is a warmth under it that was not there before the refuge, faint as a hand remembered.

### **Chapter 19: Moonset Terms**

*The coastal safehouse, Winter 1852*

**CHAPTER OVERVIEW**

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to keep Catherine and find a way forward.

**Obstacle.** The ultimatum, Beatrice's refusal, the pull, and his own fear.

**Turning point.** Catherine: "The choice is mine."

**Consequence.** He sits on the shingle with the signet unworn. Niall promises to follow him. This is Pinch Point Two, and it sets up Plot Turn Two.

**Span and length.** Tuesday 21 December 1852, the winter solstice, the longest night. Target about 4,600 words.

#### **Scene 1\. The man on the shingle**

**Scene summary.** At noon a thin, colorless young man walks down the shingle path to the bothy and stands outside it without knocking, waiting to be seen. Josef. He delivers his message without inflection. The Sterling girl will come to the manor gate tomorrow night, before the moon goes down, of her own walking, or the Count will consider the bargain broken and act on every soul sheltering her. "He bids me add that he has been patient. He does not consider patience a debt he owes indefinitely." Alexander asks his name and whether anyone waits on him at home, and Josef simply repeats the terms, as if the question never reached whatever remains of him. He walks back up the path with the steadiness of compulsion rather than confidence. The scene brings in the Pinch Point Two pressure and puts Josef on the page before the cairn.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to understand the terms.

**Obstacle.** Josef's emptiness.

**Turning point.** The terms themselves.

**Consequence.** The deadline: moonset on the morning of 23 December, about quarter past five.

**Location.** The shingle path outside the bothy.

**Characters.** Alexander, Josef, Beatrice, and others.

**Weather and light.** A grey noon under flat cloud.

**Mood.** Chilling.

**Date and time of day.** Tuesday 21 December 1852, about noon.

**Time since previous scene.** About thirteen hours.

**Scene duration.** About ten minutes.

**Carried forward.** Hessian's hunger.

**Setups and plants.** Josef in the cairn (Chapter 24).

**Rules to honor.** Josef doesn't knock.

**Target length.** About 600 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 19.1**

**Continuity in.** About thirteen hours after 18.6. Tuesday 21 December, the winter solstice, noon. The company has slept badly. Alexander has not put the signet on. Catherine is asleep in the hidden room.

**Continuity out.** The deadline is set: moonset on the morning of Thursday 23 December, about quarter past five. The ultimatum has a face.

**First appearance: Josef (named).**

* Hessian's familiar since 1841\. A thin, colorless young man, eyes that hold nothing of their own. Human, so daylight is no barrier.  
* Walks down the shingle path and stands outside the bothy without knocking, waiting to be seen. Speaks only what he has been told, without inflection.  
* Delivers the message: "The Sterling girl will come to the manor gate tomorrow night, before the moon goes down, of her own walking, or the Count will consider the bargain broken and act on every soul sheltering her." Then: "He bids me add that he has been patient. He does not consider patience a debt he owes indefinitely."  
* Alexander asks his name and whether anyone waits on him at home. Josef simply repeats the terms, as if the question never reached whatever remains of him. He walks back up the path with the steadiness of compulsion rather than confidence.

**Alexander.** Wants to understand the terms. He asks the human question instead of the tactical one, and gets nothing back. Fingertips still.

**Beatrice.** At the bothy door with the others. Supplies the name and the years afterward, from the Vanguard's records. Brooch, wool, iron-grey coil. She does not intervene.

**Location.** The shingle path outside the bothy. Grey noon under flat cloud, sea sound, cold.

**Rules in play.**

* Josef does not knock. He waits to be seen.  
* Hessian's terms: "tomorrow night" is the night of Wednesday 22 into Thursday 23\. The deadline is moonset on the 23rd, about 5:12.  
* How Hessian knows where Catherine is: the maker's pull through the bite runs both ways (19.3).  
* The Vanguard's authority does not extend to Josef. He is human, and compelled.

**Threads touched.** Josef in the cairn (24.x). Hessian's hunger (22.x). The Mark and the bite. The ultimatum that sets the clock.

**Flags.**

* Josef's name has not been given on the page. **DECIDED:** Beatrice gives it after he leaves. Josef does not give it himself.  
* The outline does not say how Hessian found the bothy. **DECIDED:** through the pull in the bite (see rules). Nobody in the scene knows it yet.

#### **Scene 2\. Protocol**

**Scene summary.** Sebastian speaks first, as the book's political antagonist should. The Vanguard's protocol for the progeny of a hostile maker is containment, he says, and failing that, a Sterling heir in the south and a report to the Crown. Hessian has made his terms plain, and one vampire girl weighed against a whole company is arithmetic. Beatrice cuts him off. But she also will not give Alexander command. He is Duke now in law, but he has held that title for twelve hours, and his impatience in the covenant chamber is why the Duke and Duchess are dead. She says it to his face, because she respects him too much to say it behind his back. The scene is where the gap between title and standing becomes an open wound, and Sebastian's framing of ambition as concern is at its most dangerous.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants command.

**Obstacle.** Beatrice and Sebastian.

**Turning point.** Beatrice says it to his face.

**Consequence.** The title-standing wound is open.

**Location.** The bothy's main room.

**Characters.** Alexander, Beatrice, Sebastian, Ellery, Seraphina.

**Weather and light.** Sleet.

**Mood.** Confrontational.

**Date and time of day.** Tuesday 21 December 1852, about one in the afternoon.

**Time since previous scene.** About an hour.

**Scene duration.** About forty minutes.

**Carried forward.** The deaths.

**Setups and plants.** Chapters 20 and 24\.

**Rules to honor.** Sebastian frames ambition as concern.

**Target length.** About 900 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 19.2**

**Continuity in.** About an hour after 19.1. One in the afternoon. Sleet has begun. The company has gathered in the main room. Two of Beatrice's operatives are dead and Edward and Amelia are lost.

**Continuity out.** The gap between title and standing is an open wound. Sebastian has framed ambition as concern. Beatrice has said it to his face.

**Sebastian.** Fair-skinned, prematurely grey, groomed, controlled. The ledger is back in his hand or still missing (leave it absent, 18.1). Speaks first, as the political antagonist should. The Vanguard's protocol for the progeny of a hostile maker is containment, he says, and failing that, a Sterling heir in the south and a report to the Crown. One vampire girl weighed against a whole company is arithmetic. Repeats a phrase for emphasis when pressed ("Until it doesn't.").

**Beatrice.** Practical wool, brooch. Cuts him off, as at 13.1. But she also will not give Alexander command. He is Duke now in law, and has been for a day and a half, and his impatience in the covenant chamber is why the Duke and Duchess are dead. She says it to his face because she respects him too much to say it behind his back. Plain rebuke, never raised. She states it once: "I am Acting Commander until the Crown says otherwise."

**Alexander.** Wants command. Takes the blow. Quiet baritone, dropping lower as he presses. No shouting. He does not deny it.

**Ellery, Seraphina.** Ellery stands by the door and says nothing. Seraphina sits, the dead eye closed, the good one on Alexander.

**Location.** The bothy's main room. Sleet on the glass, peat smoke, a long table.

**Rules in play.**

* Alexander is Duke in law from the moment of Edward's death (the night of 19 December). No bodies, so the public account waits (26.x).  
* Beatrice is Acting Commander, never regent (Main Bible).  
* Sebastian frames ambition as concern. He is the book's political antagonist and must sound reasonable.  
* Vanguard protocol for progeny of a hostile maker: containment. Catherine's case is under the ethic (2.3), not just the protocol, and Beatrice knows it.

**Threads touched.** The title-standing wound (20.x, 24.x). The council fracture. Sebastian's arc (26.x). Beatrice's price (21.4).

**Flags.** The outline says he has held the title "twelve hours". Edward died about 11:10 p.m. on Sunday; it is now about 1 p.m. on Tuesday. **DECIDED:** "a day and a half."

#### **Scene 3\. The pull**

**Scene summary.** As the afternoon goes, Catherine feels the bite. Hessian's pull presses at it, cold as iron in winter, turning her toward the manor like a compass needle. She stands at the hidden room's door and does not know how she got there. Alexander finds her and, frightened, tells her she will not go. She will stay in this room. He will set a guard on the door. He hears himself become his father at the worst of him, and cannot stop. The scene shows Alexander's flaw at its peak, protection curdling into control, aimed at the person the book has most reason to defend.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to hold her.

**Obstacle.** The pull.

**Turning point.** He becomes his father at his worst.

**Consequence.** His flaw is at its peak.

**Location.** The door of the hidden room.

**Characters.** Alexander, Catherine.

**Weather and light.** The afternoon failing (sunset about 3:14).

**Mood.** Fear.

**Date and time of day.** Tuesday 21 December 1852, about half past three.

**Time since previous scene.** About ninety minutes.

**Scene duration.** About ten minutes.

**Carried forward.** Hessian's terms.

**Setups and plants.** 19.4.

**Rules to honor.** The maker's pull through the bite.

**Target length.** About 700 words.

#### **Scene 4\. The choice is mine**

**Scene summary.** Catherine does not raise her voice, and somehow that makes the correction land harder than shouting would. She is not walking to that gate tonight at a compelled man's word, she tells him. But she will thank him to remember that the choice is hers to make, and not his, and not Hessian's. A man who threatens a whole household to get her back is more frightened of losing her than he is letting on, and a frightened man guards his possessions badly. Alexander has no answer. The scene puts Catherine's agency against her brother's control, and it gives her the key insight of the second assault before he has one.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants her to obey.

**Obstacle.** Her will.

**Turning point.** "The choice is mine."

**Consequence.** The insight belongs to her.

**Location.** The hidden room.

**Weather and light.** Dark.

**Mood.** Correction.

**Time since previous scene.** Continuous.

**Scene duration.** About ten minutes.

**Carried forward.** 19.3.

**Setups and plants.** Chapter 21 (her plan).

**Target length.** About 700 words.

But he will remember that the choice is hers, not his and not Hessian's. Line: "The choice is mine." #### **Scene 5\. Everything spent**

**Scene summary.** By dusk the arithmetic is bare. Edward and Amelia are gone. Calum is back under the Mark. Percival has fled with what he came for. Two of Beatrice's operatives are dead and Niall cannot stand. Seraphina has a blind eye and little strength left. There is no Crown authorization and no reinforcement for days. The one way into the refuge the family knew has been drowned. Hessian is wounded, but he is healing, and he has a deadline that suits him. Alexander sits alone on the shingle with his father's signet in his fist and does not put it on. The scene is Pinch Point Two, in full: allies compromised, resources exhausted, and hope apparently lost.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants any way out.

**Obstacle.** Everything has been spent.

**Turning point.** None. This is the bottom.

**Consequence.** Pinch Point Two.

**Characters.** Alexander.

**Weather and light.** The longest night beginning, with a hard frost and a waxing gibbous moon behind cloud.

**Scene duration.** About thirty minutes.

**Setups and plants.** 19.6.

**Target length.** About 600 words.

#### **Scene 6\. Niall**

**Scene summary.** Niall has made Seraphina help him out to the shingle, which he should not have done. He sits down beside Alexander with a care that says how much it costs him. He does not tell Alexander it was not his fault, because it was, partly, and they both know it. He says that Edward made worse mistakes than that one and lived long enough to learn from them, and that Alexander has been given less time than his father and will have to learn faster. Then he says the thing he came out to say. "Whatever you decide tonight, I'll follow it. Even if it's not what I'd choose. I'll not decide for you again." Alexander looks at him for a long time. Neither of them says the other thing. The scene sets up the climax's inversion of command, in which Niall follows Alexander against his own instinct, and holds the romance at its most exposed point without a confession.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants absolution.

**Obstacle.** It isn't Niall's to give.

**Turning point.** "I'll follow it."

**Consequence.** The inversion of command is set up.

**Location.** The shingle.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall.

**Weather and light.** The moon breaking through.

**Mood.** Exposed.

**Date and time of day.** Tuesday 21 December 1852, about five in the afternoon.

**Time since previous scene.** About thirty minutes.

**Scene duration.** About twenty minutes.

**Carried forward.** The terms from 6.3.

**Setups and plants.** Chapter 23, scene 3\.

**Rules to honor.** No confession.

**Target length.** About 700 words.

Does not say it was not Alexander's fault, because it was, partly, and they both know it. He says Edward made worse mistakes than that one and lived long enough to learn from them, and that Alexander has been given less time and will have to learn faster.

**Seraphina.** Off at the bothy door, watching.

## **PART FIVE: FOR CROWN AND KIN**

---

### **Chapter 20: The Signet**

*The coastal safehouse, Winter 1852*

**CHAPTER OVERVIEW**

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants a way to win that doesn't repeat his mistake.

**Obstacle.** His guilt, his lack of standing, and not knowing what his mother did.

**Turning point.** "She planted something." Then "The covenant responds to purpose."

**Consequence.** He takes the signet into his breast pocket on the covenant's terms and sits the night beside Niall. He is steady for the first time. That sets up the plan in Chapter 21\.

**Span and length.** Tuesday 21 December, about seven in the evening, to Wednesday 22 December 1852, about six in the morning. Target about 4,500 words.

#### **Scene 1\. Her terms**

**Scene summary.** Seraphina asks Catherine's leave to read what Amelia put in her blood. Catherine gives it on terms of her own. Seraphina may read and may not take anything out, change anything, or tell anyone what she finds before telling Catherine first. Seraphina agrees without argument, which is itself a change in her. With her one living eye closed and her fingertips on the bite through the collar, she reads by touch. "Your mother didn't seal anything in you," she says at last. "She planted something." What Amelia planted was Catherine herself, the uncompelled girl, bound to the signet and to the covenant, and it will wake only when a Sterling claims the covenant to protect rather than to own. The scene is Plot Turn Two's key found, and it keeps Catherine in authority over her own body.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander, as witness at Catherine's invitation. He wants to know what Amelia did.

**Obstacle.** Catherine's right over her own body.

**Turning point.** "She planted something."

**Consequence.** The key is found.

**Location.** The hidden room.

**Characters.** Catherine, Seraphina, Alexander.

**Weather and light.** Night on the solstice. Dark.

**Mood.** Reverent.

**Date and time of day.** Tuesday 21 December 1852, about seven in the evening.

**Time since previous scene.** About two hours.

**Scene duration.** About thirty minutes.

**Carried forward.** The warmth from 18.6.

**Setups and plants.** Chapter 23, scene 2 ("She didn't seal it. She planted it.").

**Rules to honor.** The safeguard, as defined. Consent.

**Target length.** About 800 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 20.1**

**Continuity in.** About two hours after 19.6. Tuesday 21 December, seven in the evening. Catherine has not spoken to anyone about the warmth (18.6). Niall sleeps in the hidden room with the swallow handkerchief on his ribs.

**Continuity out.** The key is found. Catherine still holds authority over her own body.

**Catherine.** Grey, still, the mark by her left eye. Gives Seraphina leave on her own terms: Seraphina may read and may not take anything out, change anything, or tell anyone what she finds before telling Catherine first. She has invited Alexander to witness. Dry, careful, no lightness yet.

**Seraphina.** Agrees without argument, which is itself a change in her (she rations, then over-confesses, 13.3). Closes her one living eye and lays her fingertips on the bite through the collar. Reads by touch. Line: "Your mother didn't seal anything in you. She planted something."

* What Amelia planted was Catherine herself, the uncompelled girl, bound to the signet and to the covenant. It will wake only when a Sterling claims the covenant to protect rather than to own.  
* She does not identify the Elisabeta trace in the safeguard (Book Four). She reads what is there and no more.

**Alexander.** Witness at Catherine's invitation. Wants to know what his mother did. Silent. His hand stays at his side.

**Location.** The hidden room. Night on the solstice, dark, dry, one lamp low. Niall asleep, out of the lamp's reach.

**Rules in play.**

* The safeguard: Catherine's uncompelled self planted in her blood beneath the bite, bound to the signet and to the covenant (17.3). It wakes only when Alexander claims the place to protect rather than to own.  
* Consent: Catherine's terms are binding. Seraphina keeps them.  
* Seraphina can read but cannot alter or remove it.

**Threads touched.** "She didn't seal it. She planted it" (23.2). The bloodline (Book Four). Plot Turn Two's key. Catherine's agency.

**Flags.** Which of Seraphina's eyes is closed is easy to get wrong. **DECIDED:** the good eye closes so she can read by touch. The blind eye stays as it is.

#### **Scene 2\. Purpose**

**Scene summary.** Seraphina tells Alexander what the older law is, as far as she knows it. "The covenant does not respond to possession. It responds to purpose." The signet is a legal symbol, an heirloom, a command token, and proof of succession, and it has no power apart from rightful acceptance. Then she does what she failed to do at the festival. She tells him every part of what she knows and does not know, including the limits of her own reading, and offers to teach him the older law if he survives to learn it. The scene turns Seraphina from distant guardian into an accountable, active mentor, and it states the climax's rule plainly before the climax.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants the rule.

**Obstacle.** Seraphina's limits.

**Turning point.** "Purpose, not possession."

**Consequence.** Seraphina becomes a mentor. The rule is stated.

**Location.** The bothy fireside.

**Characters.** Alexander, Seraphina.

**Weather and light.** Frost, and a clear moon.

**Mood.** Instruction.

**Date and time of day.** Tuesday 21 December 1852, about eight in the evening.

**Time since previous scene.** About thirty minutes.

**Scene duration.** About forty minutes.

**Carried forward.** Her confession from 13.3.

**Setups and plants.** Chapter 23, scene 4\.

**Rules to honor.** The signet has no power without rightful acceptance.

**Target length.** About 700 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 20.2**

**Continuity in.** About thirty minutes after 20.1. Eight in the evening. Seraphina and Alexander move to the bothy fireside.

**Continuity out.** The rule is stated before the climax. Seraphina has become an accountable mentor.

**Seraphina.** Aged, upright, the amulet, the dead eye turned to the fire. Tired: archaic speech may slip in. Tells him what the older law is, as far as she knows it. Line: "The covenant does not respond to possession. It responds to purpose."

* The signet is a legal symbol, an heirloom, a command token and proof of succession, and it has no power apart from rightful acceptance.  
* Then she does what she failed to do at the festival. She tells every part of what she knows and does not know, including the limits of her own reading. She offers to teach him the older law if he survives to learn it.  
* "Well. That's not nothing." is available if he surprises her.

**Alexander.** Wants the rule. Reads it as he reads a document, twice. Asks precise questions. Does not argue. He has the signet in his coat, still unworn.

**Location.** The bothy fireside. Frost outside, a clear moon in the window, peat smoke.

**Rules in play.**

* The signet has no power without rightful acceptance.  
* The covenant responds to purpose, not possession. This is the climax's rule. Do not paraphrase it beyond Seraphina's line.  
* She still does not know the line runs through Hessian (Book Four).

**Threads touched.** The claim (23.4). Seraphina as mentor (Books Two to Four). Her confession (13.3) made active.

**Flags.** None.

#### **Scene 3\. An apology**

**Scene summary.** Alexander goes to the hidden room and apologizes to his sister for trying to decide for her. He does not dress it up. He tells her he did to her exactly what their parents did to him, and that he understands now what it cost him and what it would have cost her. Catherine accepts it, lightly, the way she accepts most things she means entirely. She says she has made her decision and will tell him at council, where everyone can hear it, because she is done with decisions made in small rooms. The scene completes the correction of Alexander's second flaw and restores the siblings as equals.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to repair things with his sister.

**Obstacle.** His pride.

**Turning point.** She accepts his apology lightly.

**Consequence.** They are equals again.

**Location.** The hidden room.

**Characters.** Alexander, Catherine.

**Weather and light.** Dark.

**Mood.** Warm.

**Date and time of day.** Tuesday 21 December 1852, about nine at night.

**Time since previous scene.** About twenty minutes.

**Scene duration.** About fifteen minutes.

**Carried forward.** 19.3 and 19.4.

**Setups and plants.** Chapter 21, scene 2\.

**Target length.** About 600 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 20.3**

**Continuity in.** About twenty minutes after 20.2. Nine at night. Catherine is in the hidden room. Niall is asleep there.

**Continuity out.** The siblings are equals again. She has made a decision and will say it at council.

**Alexander.** Goes to the hidden room and apologizes for trying to decide for her. He does not dress it up. He did to her exactly what their parents did to him, and he understands now what it cost him and what it would have cost her. Quiet, exact. No half-smile.

**Catherine.** Accepts it lightly, the way she accepts most things she means entirely. Her lightness returns for the first time since the refuge. She has made her decision and will tell him at council, where everyone can hear it, because she is done with decisions made in small rooms. Calls him "Alex." Her laugh may arrive before the joke.

**Niall.** Asleep in the same room. They speak low, at the far end of it.

**Location.** The hidden room. Dark, dry, warm from a low lamp.

**Rules in play.**

* Alexander's flaw (protection overriding agency) is corrected here, in words.  
* She does not tell him the decision. She says only that she has made it.  
* Catherine has known for a day that the pull can turn her. She says nothing of it.

**Threads touched.** Catherine's plan (21.2, "Can you imagine?" to "Watch me"). The flaw corrected (21.3). The siblings restored.

**Flags.** None.

#### **Scene 4\. Beatrice's price**

**Scene summary.** Alexander goes to Beatrice. He does not ask for command. He asks what she would need to see before she could lend him her people. She tells him about her husband, and the selkie mother and her two children, and the authorization that came an hour too late. Her hand is on the brooch the whole time. "Bring me a plan I can defend to the Crown," she says, "and you'll have my people. Not my command." It is the first step toward conditional trust, and it names the political thread's price in plain terms.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants Beatrice's people.

**Obstacle.** Her grief, and procedure.

**Turning point.** "Bring me a plan I can defend."

**Consequence.** The first step toward conditional trust.

**Location.** Outside the bothy, by the boats.

**Characters.** Alexander, Beatrice.

**Weather and light.** A cold, clear night and a bright moon on the sea.

**Mood.** Somber respect.

**Date and time of day.** Tuesday 21 December 1852, about ten at night.

**Time since previous scene.** About forty-five minutes.

**Scene duration.** About thirty minutes.

**Carried forward.** The refusal from 19.2.

**Setups and plants.** Chapter 21, scene 4\.

**Rules to honor.** Beatrice's backstory: her husband and the selkie family. The brooch.

**Target length.** About 700 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 20.4**

**Continuity in.** About forty-five minutes after 20.3. Ten at night. Beatrice has been by the boats on her own since dusk.

**Continuity out.** The first step toward conditional trust. The price is stated.

**Beatrice.** Practical wool, iron-grey coil, the ward-worked brooch. Her hand is on the brooch the whole time. She does not look at Alexander when she speaks of it. Somber, plain. Tells him about her husband, Laurence, and the selkie mother and her two children off Stronsay in 1839, and the authorization that came an hour too late. Line: "Bring me a plan I can defend to the Crown, and you'll have my people. Not my command."

* This is the first time Alexander hears the story (13.5, where nobody said it).

**Alexander.** Does not ask for command. He asks what she would need to see before she could lend him her people. Listens. Wants her people. Does not argue with her price. His voice drops.

**Location.** Outside the bothy, by the boats. A cold, clear night, a bright moon on the sea.

**Rules in play.**

* Beatrice's backstory: in 1839 she led an extraction off Stronsay and followed protocol to the letter. Laurence, an operative on the mission, died with the selkie family. They had no children.  
* She is still Acting Commander. The price is her people, not her command.  
* The brooch is ward-worked. Do not say who gave it.

**Threads touched.** The plan Beatrice can defend (21.4). Conditional trust, never full softening (Main Bible). The council fracture.

**Flags.** None.

#### **Scene 5\. The breast pocket**

**Scene summary.** Alone on the shingle near midnight, Alexander takes out his father's signet. He does not put it on his finger. He puts it in his breast pocket, over his heart, where it will ride from now on. He chooses, out loud to no one, to take it for protection and not for vengeance, with his father's sentence in his head: anger is evidence of conscience, not a plan of action. His hand goes to the pocket to check it is there. It is the first time he makes the gesture that will become his. This is Plot Turn Two's inner realization, the moment he accepts the signet on the covenant's terms.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants to accept the signet rightly.

**Obstacle.** His anger.

**Turning point.** The breast pocket, and his father's sentence.

**Consequence.** Plot Turn Two.

**Location.** The shingle.

**Characters.** Alexander.

**Weather and light.** Near midnight, with the moon high and frost on the stones.

**Mood.** Resolve.

**Date and time of day.** Tuesday 21 December 1852, about half past eleven at night.

**Time since previous scene.** About ninety minutes.

**Scene duration.** About twenty minutes.

**Carried forward.** "Protect. Not own." from 17.1.

**Setups and plants.** The gesture (Chapter 24, scene 6).

**Rules to honor.** The signet rides in the breast pocket.

**Target length.** About 600 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 20.5**

**Continuity in.** About ninety minutes after 20.4. Half past eleven at night. Alexander is alone on the shingle in his father's coat.

**Continuity out.** Plot Turn Two: he has accepted the signet on the covenant's terms. A gesture is born.

**Alexander.** Wants to accept the signet rightly. Takes out his father's signet. He does not put it on his finger. He puts it in the breast pocket of the coat, over his heart, where it will ride from now on. He chooses, out loud to no one, to take it for protection and not for vengeance, with his father's sentence in his head: anger is evidence of conscience, not a plan of action (17.1). His hand goes to the pocket to check it is there. This is the first time he makes the gesture that will become his.

* The half-smile is absent. Fingertips still. Voice low.

**Location.** The shingle. Near midnight, the moon high and past the south, frost on the stones, black sea.

**Rules in play.**

* The signet rides in the breast pocket. It is not worn.  
* The gesture begins here. From now on the hand goes to the breast pocket (24.6, 26.x): first as a check, then as a steadying.  
* The watch and the ball stay in the coat's other pockets (18.4).

**Threads touched.** The gesture (24.6, 26.x). "Protect. Not own." (17.1). Plot Turn Two.

**Flags.** The coat has several pockets. **DECIDED:** the signet goes in the inside breast pocket on the left, apart from the watch and the ball.

#### **Scene 6\. The watch he keeps**

**Scene summary.** Niall is asleep in the hidden room, feverish from the wound. Alexander sits the watch beside him through the night, as Niall sat through every night of the Grey Year without being asked. At one point Niall wakes and finds him there and does not say anything, and goes back to sleep. The scene reverses the Grey Year's roles and moves the romance by a visible degree, all through behavior and without declaration.

**Perspective and goal.** Alexander. He wants Niall to live.

**Obstacle.** Fever.

**Turning point.** Niall wakes and sees him there.

**Consequence.** The Grey Year's roles reversed.

**Location.** The hidden room.

**Characters.** Alexander, Niall.

**Weather and light.** The longest night passing into the pre-dawn.

**Mood.** Tender.

**Date and time of day.** Wednesday 22 December 1852, about midnight to five in the morning.

**Time since previous scene.** About thirty minutes.

**Scene duration.** About five hours, compressed.

**Carried forward.** 3.4 (Niall's Grey Year).

**Setups and plants.** The croft.

**Target length.** About 500 words.

##### **SCENE PACKET 20.6**

**Continuity in.** About thirty minutes after 20.5. From midnight into the small hours of Wednesday 22 December, about five in the morning, compressed. Niall is feverish from the wound. The stopped watch is still in the coat.

**Continuity out.** The Grey Year's roles are reversed. The romance has moved a visible degree without a word.

**Alexander.** Wants Niall to live. Sits the watch beside him through the night, as Niall sat through every night of the Grey Year without being asked (3.4). Changes the cloth on his forehead. Checks the handkerchief on the ribs. Says almost nothing. The stopped pocket watch is in the coat and he keeps time by the window.

**Niall.** Feverish, grey, asleep. At one point wakes and finds him there and says nothing, and goes back to sleep. The burr may thicken in his sleep. No word, or only "Alex."

**Location.** The hidden room. The longest night passing into pre-dawn. Dark, dry, lamp low.

**Rules in play.**

* No declaration. The scene is behavior.  
* The Grey Year: Niall sat the watch beside Alexander, unasked, every night (3.4). Keep the reversal implicit.  
* The stopped watch gives the scene an irony (a vigil kept without a clock). Use lightly or not at all.

**Threads touched.** The croft (21.x). The romance's slow rise. The Grey Year's debt repaid.

**Flags.** Catherine's whereabouts are not stated. **DECIDED:** she has gone up to the bothy's main room and gives them the room.
